## [0575] Drug-induced QT prolongation: risk stratification, offending drugs, monitoring

**Cluster**: qt_prolongation | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Many common drugs (antidepressants, antibiotics, antipsychotics, antimalarials, some cancer drugs) prolong the QT interval. Risk is managed by identifying high-risk patients, avoiding interacting combinations and monitoring ECGs.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2023 Canadian Cardiovascular Society update notes that certain beta-blockers are highly effective in long QT, defibrillators are infrequently required, and that many commonly prescribed drugs such as antidepressants and antibiotics prolong QT, with recommendations for their safe use. (PMID:37827588)
- Short courses of chloroquine or hydroxychloroquine in COVID-19 caused significant QT prolongation, and daily ECG monitoring and other mitigation were advised. (PMID:32438018)
- A later meta-analysis found that despite being listed as QT-prolonging, hydroxychloroquine and chloroquine did not increase major adverse cardiac events. (PMID:37881891)
- Among CDK4/6 inhibitors, ribociclib prolonged QTc about twice as much as the others and grade 3 prolongations occurred exclusively with ribociclib. (PMID:39254653)
- QT-prolonging drug-drug interactions are common in psychiatric prescribing, so precautionary safety interventions and vigilance are advised. (PMID:35968552)
- In acute antipsychotic poisoning, QT risk depends on the drug and dose ingested, time since ingestion, heart rate and co-ingestions, and the evidence quality is heterogeneous. (PMID:41255343)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Whether QT prolongation by hydroxychloroquine translates into clinical events.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Risk-factor combination for torsades: female sex, hypokalaemia, bradycardia, multiple QT drugs.
- Ribociclib versus other CDK4/6 inhibitors.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient started on a macrolide while taking citalopram, with QTc 520 ms.

**Reviewer Gap**: Electrolyte correction, magnesium for torsades, QTc thresholds and the formula for correction are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 32438018**: *QT prolongation, torsades de pointes, and sudden death with short courses of chloroquine or hydroxychloroquine as used in COVID-19: A systematic review* (2020) [systematic review]
- **PMID 35968552**: *Prevalence of QT-Prolonging Drug-Drug Interactions in Psychiatry: A Systematic Review and Meta Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 37827588**: *The 2023 Canadian Cardiovascular Society Clinical Practice Update on Management of the Patient With a Prolonged QT Interval* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 37881891**: *Hydroxychloroquine-Chloroquine, QT-Prolongation, and Major Adverse Cardiac Events: A Meta-analysis and Scoping Review* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39254653**: *QTc prolongation across CDK4/6 inhibitors: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41255343**: *QT interval prolongation in acute antipsychotic poisoning: systematic review and recommendations* (2025) [guideline]

## [0578] Drug-resistant epilepsy: epilepsy surgery workup, vagus nerve stimulation (VNS)

**Cluster**: drug_resistant_epilepsy | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Epilepsy is drug-resistant when seizures persist after adequate trials of two appropriate drugs; resective surgery and neuromodulation are the options, and they may also lower mortality.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found 46% of patients with drug-resistant epilepsy had at least 50% seizure reduction with vagus nerve stimulation, and VNS should be considered when antiseizure medication has failed. (PMID:39809131)
- In long-term comparisons, responsive neurostimulation and deep brain stimulation outperformed invasive VNS; transcutaneous methods showed promise but need confirmation. (PMID:39321920)
- Epilepsy surgery and neuromodulation may reduce overall mortality including SUDEP, but dedicated long-term prospective studies are needed. (PMID:36114753)
- Transcutaneous auricular VNS is under study as an adjunct, but methodological concerns mean well-designed RCTs are needed. (PMID:40158411, PMID:40367687)
- A consensus on ring chromosome 20 syndrome says ketogenic diet, VNS or deep brain stimulation can be considered for drug-resistant epilepsy. (PMID:42096279)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Which patients benefit most from each neuromodulation modality.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Definition of drug-resistant epilepsy and referral to an epilepsy surgery centre.
- VNS response rate (about half achieve 50% reduction).

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with focal seizures despite two ASMs and a hippocampal lesion on MRI.

**Reviewer Gap**: Presurgical work-up (video-EEG, MRI, PET, invasive monitoring), seizure-free rates after temporal lobectomy and the ILAE definition are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 36114753**: *Drug-resistant epilepsy and mortality-Why and when do neuromodulation and epilepsy surgery reduce overall mortality* (2022) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39321920**: *Comparative Efficacy of Neuromodulatory Strategies for Drug-Resistant Epilepsy: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39809131**: *Significant reduction of seizure frequency in patients with drug-resistant epilepsy by vagus nerve stimulation: Systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40158411**: *Can transcutaneous auricular vagus nerve stimulation be considered a viable adjuntive therapy in drug-resistant epilepsy? A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40367687**: *Efficacy of transcutaneous vagus nerve stimulation (t-VNS) in treating drug-resistant epilepsy: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 42096279**: *Management of ring chromosome 20 syndrome: Narrative review and consensus recommendations* (2026) [guideline]

## [0579] Drug-Resistant TB

**Cluster**: dr_tb | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Drug-resistant TB treatment has moved to shorter, all-oral regimens built on bedaquiline, pretomanid and linezolid (BPaL/BPaLM), replacing injectable-containing, long regimens in eligible patients.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ATS/CDC/ERS/IDSA 2025 update recommends novel drug-resistant TB regimens containing bedaquiline, pretomanid and linezolid with or without moxifloxacin, and all-oral shorter regimens for eligible individuals. (PMID:40693952)
- The WHO 2022 module groups drug-resistant TB recommendations in one document, and the 2023 update has been described as a turning point to shorter, all-oral, patient-centred MDR/RR-TB care. (PMID:36630546, PMID:36918080)
- The Spanish guideline recommends a six-month regimen (conditioned by fluoroquinolone resistance) for pulmonary MDR-TB, plus directly observed or video-observed treatment. (PMID:39764791)
- Austrian consensus states BPaLM (and BPaL) may be used for MDR, pre-XDR and rifampicin-resistant TB if prerequisites are met. (PMID:38810608)
- Pre-XDR and XDR burden among patients with MDR-TB is considerable, supporting drug-resistance surveillance. (PMID:37072053)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Implementation challenges of new regimens in low-resource settings.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Components of BPaLM and why it replaced injectable regimens.
- Directly observed or video-observed therapy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with rifampicin-resistant TB on GeneXpert: choice of regimen and monitoring.

**Reviewer Gap**: Adverse-effect monitoring (QT with bedaquiline, linezolid toxicity) and eligibility criteria are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 36630546**: *WHO consolidated guidelines on tuberculosis: Module 4: treatment - drug-resistant tuberculosis treatment, 2022 update* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 36918080**: *Update of drug-resistant tuberculosis treatment guidelines: A turning point* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 37072053**: *Epidemiology of extensively drug-resistant tuberculosis among patients with multidrug-resistant tuberculosis: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38810608**: *Treatment of MDR, Pre-XDR, XDR, and Rifampicin-Resistant Tuberculosis or in Case of Intolerance to at Least Rifampicin in Austria, Germany, and Switzerland* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39764791**: *Executive Summary: Clinical Practice Guidelines on the Management of Resistant Tuberculosis of the Spanish Society of Pulmonology and Thoracic Surgery (SEPAR) and the Spanish Society of Infectious Diseases and Clinical Microbiology (SEIMC)* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 40693952**: *Updates on the Treatment of Drug-Susceptible and Drug-Resistant Tuberculosis: An Official ATS/CDC/ERS/IDSA Clinical Practice Guideline* (2025) [guideline]

---

## [0581] dual antiplatelet therapy duration after pci

**Cluster**: dapt | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
After PCI for acute coronary syndrome, shortening dual antiplatelet therapy and continuing a potent P2Y12 inhibitor alone reduces bleeding without more ischaemic events, particularly with ticagrelor.

### Core Teaching Points
- P2Y12 inhibitor monotherapy after short DAPT in ACS halved bleeding without increasing ischaemic events; ticagrelor, but not clopidogrel, monotherapy reduced MACE, net adverse clinical events and mortality versus standard DAPT. (PMID:39054275)
- In a network meta-analysis, short DAPT followed by potent P2Y12 inhibitor monotherapy ranked best to reduce MACCE after drug-eluting stents in ACS; the safety of stopping aspirin in clopidogrel-treated patients remained unclear. (PMID:39382876)
- Adding a PPI to DAPT significantly lowered upper gastrointestinal bleeding and all-cause mortality without harming major cardiovascular outcomes. (PMID:40338683)
- A 2026 network meta-analysis suggested aspirin discontinuation may be optimal for women and switching the P2Y12 inhibitor to clopidogrel for men; this sex-difference signal needs confirmation. (PMID:40643264)
- The 2025 ACC/AHA ACS guideline updates many prior recommendations with new evidence. (PMID:40014670)
- The ESC consensus on antithrombotic therapy after CABG reviews the evidence and gives statements for best practice. (PMID:40884067)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal de-escalation strategy by sex and bleeding risk.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- De-escalation: shorten DAPT then P2Y12 monotherapy.
- PPI cover to reduce upper GI bleeding on DAPT.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient 1 month after PCI for NSTEMI with high bleeding risk: de-escalation options.

**Reviewer Gap**: Stable-disease and high-bleeding-risk durations, and specific recommended months, are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39054275**: *P2Y12 inhibitor monotherapy after short DAPT in acute coronary syndrome: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]

## [0582] Dual antiplatelet therapy: duration, P2Y12 selection, and bleeding risk balancing

**Cluster**: dapt | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
After PCI for acute coronary syndrome, shortening dual antiplatelet therapy and continuing a potent P2Y12 inhibitor alone reduces bleeding without more ischaemic events, particularly with ticagrelor.

### Core Teaching Points
- P2Y12 inhibitor monotherapy after short DAPT in ACS halved bleeding without increasing ischaemic events; ticagrelor, but not clopidogrel, monotherapy reduced MACE, net adverse clinical events and mortality versus standard DAPT. (PMID:39054275)
- In a network meta-analysis, short DAPT followed by potent P2Y12 inhibitor monotherapy ranked best to reduce MACCE after drug-eluting stents in ACS; the safety of stopping aspirin in clopidogrel-treated patients remained unclear. (PMID:39382876)
- Adding a PPI to DAPT significantly lowered upper gastrointestinal bleeding and all-cause mortality without harming major cardiovascular outcomes. (PMID:40338683)
- A 2026 network meta-analysis suggested aspirin discontinuation may be optimal for women and switching the P2Y12 inhibitor to clopidogrel for men; this sex-difference signal needs confirmation. (PMID:40643264)
- The 2025 ACC/AHA ACS guideline updates many prior recommendations with new evidence. (PMID:40014670)
- The ESC consensus on antithrombotic therapy after CABG reviews the evidence and gives statements for best practice. (PMID:40884067)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal de-escalation strategy by sex and bleeding risk.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- De-escalation: shorten DAPT then P2Y12 monotherapy.
- PPI cover to reduce upper GI bleeding on DAPT.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient 1 month after PCI for NSTEMI with high bleeding risk: de-escalation options.

**Reviewer Gap**: Stable-disease and high-bleeding-risk durations, and specific recommended months, are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39054275**: *P2Y12 inhibitor monotherapy after short DAPT in acute coronary syndrome: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39382876**: *Short-Term Dual Antiplatelet Therapy After Drug-Eluting Stenting in Patients With Acute Coronary Syndromes: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40014670**: *2025 ACC/AHA/ACEP/NAEMSP/SCAI Guideline for the Management of Patients With Acute Coronary Syndromes: A Report of the American College of Cardiology/American Heart Association Joint Committee on Clinical Practice Guidelines* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40338683**: *Role of Proton Pump Inhibitors in Prevention of Upper Gastrointestinal Bleeding in Patients on Dual Antiplatelet Therapy: Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40643264**: *Sex differences in dual antiplatelet therapy de-escalation strategies after percutaneous coronary intervention: a network meta-analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40884067**: *Antithrombotic therapy after coronary artery bypass graft surgery: a Clinical Consensus Statement of the ESC Working Group on Cardiovascular Surgery, the ESC Working Group on Cardiovascular Pharmacotherapy, and the European Association for Cardio-Thoracic Surgery (EACTS)* (2026) [guideline]

## [0583] Duchenne and Becker muscular dystrophies: dystrophin genetics, cardiomyopathy tracking

**Cluster**: duchenne | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Duchenne and Becker muscular dystrophies need multidisciplinary standards of care covering corticosteroids, cardiac surveillance and treatment, respiratory and orthopaedic care, transition to adult services and now gene therapy.

### Core Teaching Points
- A French guideline addresses choice of corticosteroid, dose and management of long-term side effects in DMD, including osteoporosis, growth and pubertal delay, weight gain, cataract, hypertension, behavioural disorders, vaccines and metabolic complications. (PMID:39332946)
- The ACTION committee consensus sets a standardised framework for cardiac care in DMD to improve cardiac prognosis. (PMID:40012319)
- French Becker muscular dystrophy guidelines cover neurological, respiratory, cardiac and orthopaedic follow-up and include cardiac management for female carriers. (PMID:37422773)
- The 2022 HRS consensus addresses evaluation and management of arrhythmic risk in neuromuscular disorders. (PMID:35500790)
- Consensus recommendations exist for delivery and monitoring of gene therapy in DMD, based on limited rather than well-established evidence. (PMID:41005046)
- International Delphi statements and the Adult North Star Network guideline support transition from paediatric to adult care and adult standards of care. (PMID:39892019, PMID:34511509)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Long-term benefit and risk of gene therapy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Cardiomyopathy surveillance in DMD and female carriers.
- Steroid side-effect management in boys with DMD.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 8-year-old boy with calf pseudohypertrophy, Gowers sign and raised creatine kinase.

**Reviewer Gap**: Dystrophin gene mutation classes (reading-frame rule) and exon-skipping therapy are not covered by the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 34511509**: *Adult North Star Network (ANSN): Consensus Guideline For The Standard Of Care Of Adults With Duchenne Muscular Dystrophy* (2021) [guideline]
- **PMID 35500790**: *2022 HRS expert consensus statement on evaluation and management of arrhythmic risk in neuromuscular disorders* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 37422773**: *Diagnosis and management of Becker muscular dystrophy: the French guidelines* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 39332946**: *Choice of compound, dosage, and management of side effects for long-term corticosteroid treatment in Duchenne muscular dystrophy: Guidelines from the Neuromuscular Commission of the French Society of Pediatric Neurology* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39892019**: *Transition of patients with Duchenne muscular dystrophy from paediatric to adult care: An international Delphi consensus study* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40012319**: *Cardiac treatment for Duchenne muscular dystrophy: consensus recommendations from the ACTION muscular dystrophy committee* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41005046**: *Consensus recommendations and considerations for the delivery and monitoring of gene therapy in patients with Duchenne muscular dystrophy* (2025) [guideline]

## [0584] Duodenal Pathology

**Cluster**: duodenal | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Duodenal pathology spans peptic ulcer, coeliac disease and neoplasia. Retrieval for this broad topic was weak, so only a few sourced points are given and the page needs a narrower scope.

### Core Teaching Points
- Japanese peptic ulcer guidelines recommend PPIs or vonoprazan to prevent ulcer recurrence in patients with a history of ulcer on low-dose aspirin, and suggest a histamine-2 receptor antagonist as an option. (PMID:33620586)
- ESGE recommends scheduled endoscopic surveillance with high-definition white light and chromoendoscopy after curative endoscopic submucosal dissection, biopsying only suspicious areas. (PMID:35523224)
- Japanese clinical practice guidelines for duodenal cancer were published in 2021. (PMID:36260172)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Topic scope is undefined.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Prevention of ulcer recurrence on low-dose aspirin.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient on aspirin with epigastric pain and melaena.

**Reviewer Gap**: Coeliac disease diagnosis, duodenal biopsy indications, H. pylori testing and ampullary lesions are not covered; split or redefine this topic before approval.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 33620586**: *Evidence-based clinical practice guidelines for peptic ulcer disease 2020* (2021) [guideline]
- **PMID 35523224**: *Endoscopic submucosal dissection for superficial gastrointestinal lesions: European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE) Guideline - Update 2022* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 36260172**: *Clinical practice guidelines for duodenal cancer 2021* (2022) [guideline]

## [0585] Dupilumab (IL-4/IL-13 inhibitor) biologic therapy

**Cluster**: dupilumab | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Dupilumab blocks IL-4/IL-13 signalling and treats atopic dermatitis and other type 2 diseases. Key safety points are conjunctivitis and the possibility of unmasking cutaneous T-cell lymphoma.

### Core Teaching Points
- Dupilumab may unmask or exacerbate cutaneous T-cell lymphoma, particularly mycosis fungoides and Sezary syndrome, and expert consensus advises reassessment when disease worsens or atypical features emerge, discouraging a switch to other Th2-targeting biologics. (PMID:41821352, PMID:41967816)
- In chronic hand eczema dupilumab showed efficacy; adverse effects included conjunctivitis (9.9%), herpes viral infection (1.4%) and facial erythema or dermatitis (1.3%). (PMID:39501849)
- The 2026 AAD paediatric atopic dermatitis guideline strongly recommends against systemic corticosteroids and conditionally recommends against topical antimicrobials and PUVA phototherapy. (PMID:41949508)
- A multidisciplinary Delphi consensus reached agreement on all statements about definition, diagnosis and management of moderate-severe paediatric atopic dermatitis. (PMID:41862987)
- Dupilumab improved quality of life in atopic dermatitis in a meta-analysis of randomised trials. (PMID:40556751)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Lymphoma risk profile and patient selection need prospective study.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Conjunctivitis as the commonest dupilumab adverse effect.
- Atypical or worsening eczema on dupilumab: think CTCL.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Adult on dupilumab with worsening plaques and new lymphadenopathy.

**Reviewer Gap**: Mechanism details and approved indications beyond atopic dermatitis (asthma, CRSwNP, eosinophilic oesophagitis, prurigo) are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39501849**: *Dupilumab for Chronic Hand Eczema: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40556751**: *Effects of dupilumab on quality of life in patients with atopic dermatitis: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41821352**: *Cutaneous T-cell lymphomas and dupilumab for atopic dermatitis: A systematic review and expert consensus* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 41862987**: *Multidisciplinary Delphi Consensus on management of children with moderate-severe atopic dermatitis* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 41949508**: *Guidelines of care for the management of atopic dermatitis in pediatric patients* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 41967816**: *Dupilumab therapy in atopic dermatitis when cutaneous lymphoma is suspected: Consensus recommendations from the EORTC Cutaneous Lymphoma Tumour Group* (2026) [guideline]

## [0587] Durable LVAD therapy for advanced heart failure

**Cluster**: lvad | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Durable LVAD therapy improves survival in advanced heart failure, and guideline-directed medical therapy after implant is associated with better outcomes yet is under-used.

### Core Teaching Points
- In an international multicentre study GDMT in LVAD patients was associated with improved survival, even with single-agent therapy, but only 30% received optimal triple therapy. (PMID:42325168)
- GDMT within 3 months of LVAD implant was associated with fewer all-cause readmissions and a non-significant trend to lower 12-month mortality. (PMID:42569659)
- Advancing age is associated with lower GDMT use in HeartMate 3 recipients, but other patient factors do not explain low prescribing. (PMID:40091512)
- Ventricular arrhythmias are frequent after LVAD implantation and are associated with higher all-cause mortality, particularly when early. (PMID:40561522)
- British societies have issued a guideline on managing emergencies in implantable LVAD recipients, including resuscitation. (PMID:38526578)
- An AHA scientific statement covers kidney dysfunction in advanced heart failure, including LVAD and transplant and palliative-care decisions. (PMID:39253806)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal GDMT regimen and timing after implant.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Arrhythmia burden after LVAD.
- GDMT remains indicated after LVAD.

### Case Generation Hooks
- LVAD recipient found unresponsive: how emergency management differs.

**Reviewer Gap**: Indications (INTERMACS profiles), bridge versus destination therapy and complications (pump thrombosis, stroke, GI bleeding) are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38526578**: *British societies guideline on the management of emergencies in implantable left ventricular assist device recipients in transplant centres* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39253806**: *Evaluation and Management of Kidney Dysfunction in Advanced Heart Failure: A Scientific Statement From the American Heart Association* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 40091512**: *Factors associated with the use of guideline-directed medical therapy in patients with left ventricular assist device* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40561522**: *Ventricular arrhythmias following left ventricular assist device implantation: a systematic review and meta-analysis of incidence and clinical impact* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 42325168**: *Clinical impact of guideline-directed medical therapy in patients with left ventricular assist device: an international multicentre study* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42569659**: *Guideline directed-medical therapy for heart failure within 3 months post LVAD implant and rehospitalization* (2026) [guideline]

## [0588] dyslipidaemia

**Cluster**: dyslipidaemia | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
The 2026 US dyslipidaemia guideline widens statin eligibility for primary prevention, while AACE guidance centres on ASCVD risk, individualised drug choice and shared decision-making. For patients who cannot reach LDL-C goals on statins, alternative strategies give comparable outcomes.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2026 ACC/AHA multi-society guideline addresses evaluation, management and monitoring of high cholesterol, hypertriglyceridaemia and elevated lipoprotein(a). (PMID:41824590)
- An analysis found the 2026 guideline substantially expands the US population recommended for primary-prevention statin therapy, predominantly among lower-risk individuals. (PMID:42475062)
- The 2025 AACE pharmacologic guideline offers effective and safe options for adults with or at increased risk of ASCVD who need additional lipid-lowering medication, with shared decision-making. (PMID:39919851)
- The 2025 AACE algorithm emphasises ASCVD risk assessment, pancreatitis risk, individualised pharmacotherapy, cost-effectiveness and health equity. (PMID:40938233)
- In ASCVD, an alternative LDL-lowering strategy (instead of high-intensity statin alone) had comparable 3-year efficacy for death or cardiovascular events with lower LDL-C, less new-onset diabetes and less intolerance. (PMID:39565634)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Net benefit of expanded primary-prevention statin eligibility in low-risk people.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Add-on options when LDL-C remains above goal on maximal statin.
- Lipoprotein(a) as a risk enhancer.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Post-MI patient with LDL-C 2.4 mmol/L on high-intensity atorvastatin.

**Reviewer Gap**: Numeric LDL-C targets, ezetimibe and PCSK9 inhibitor sequencing and familial hypercholesterolaemia thresholds are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39565634**: *Alternative LDL Cholesterol-Lowering Strategy vs High-Intensity Statins in Atherosclerotic Cardiovascular Disease: A Systematic Review and Individual Patient Data Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39919851**: *American Association of Clinical Endocrinology Clinical Practice Guideline on Pharmacologic Management of Adults With Dyslipidemia* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40938233**: *American Association of Clinical Endocrinology Consensus Statement: Algorithm for Management of Adults with Dyslipidemia - 2025 Update* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41824590**: *2026 ACC/AHA/AACVPR/ABC/ACPM/ADA/AGS/APhA/ASPC/NLA/PCNA Guideline on the Management of Dyslipidemia: A Report of the American College of Cardiology/American Heart Association Joint Committee on Clinical Practice Guidelines* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42475062**: *Implications of the 2026 Dyslipidemia Guideline for Primary Prevention Statin Therapy* (2026) [guideline]

---

## [0591] dyspepsia

**Cluster**: dyspepsia | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Uninvestigated dyspepsia without alarm features is managed by H. pylori test-and-treat and acid suppression; functional dyspepsia is diagnosed after exclusion and treated stepwise.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ACG/CAG guideline advises H. pylori eradication in infected patients with functional dyspepsia, then PPI, tricyclic antidepressant and prokinetic therapy in that order for non-responders or H. pylori-negative patients, and does not recommend routine GI motility testing. (PMID:28631728)
- The Italian functional dyspepsia guideline found insufficient evidence to recommend complementary and alternative treatments. (PMID:40628573)
- Korea's 2025 H. pylori guideline establishes quadruple therapies as the new standard via empirical treatment or PCR-guided tailored therapy, with P-CABs and bismuth-based regimens as key components. (PMID:41846442)
- Japan's 2024 revised guideline covers H. pylori management and gastric cancer prevention. (PMID:41902905)
- The ACG 2025 guideline covers diagnosis and management of gastric premalignant conditions and identifies research needs for defining high-risk populations. (PMID:40072510)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Regional antibiotic resistance drives regimen choice.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Test-and-treat versus endoscopy in dyspepsia.
- Stepwise treatment of functional dyspepsia.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 35-year-old with epigastric discomfort, no alarm features, and positive stool antigen.

**Reviewer Gap**: Alarm features that mandate endoscopy and regimen doses are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 28631728**: *ACG and CAG Clinical Guideline: Management of Dyspepsia* (2017) [guideline]
- **PMID 40072510**: *ACG Clinical Guideline: Diagnosis and Management of Gastric Premalignant Conditions* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40628573**: *Italian guidelines for the diagnosis and treatment of functional dyspepsia - joint consensus from the Italian societies of gastroenterology and endoscopy (SIGE), Neurogastroenterology and motility (SINGEM), hospital gastroenterologists and endoscopists (AIGO), digestive endoscopy (SIED) and general medicine (SIMG)* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41846442**: *Treatment of Helicobacter pylori Infection in Korea: An Evidence-Based Analysis of the Upcoming 2025 Guideline* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 41902905**: *Guidelines for the management of Helicobacter pylori infection in Japan: 2024 revised edition* (2026) [guideline]

## [0593] Dyspnoea and Breathlessness in Advanced Cancer

**Cluster**: cancer_dyspnoea | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Dyspnoea in advanced cancer is treated by finding reversible causes, using non-drug measures, and offering systemic opioids when those are inadequate.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ASCO guideline says that when non-pharmacologic interventions give inadequate relief, systemic opioids should be offered; corticosteroids and benzodiazepines are also discussed. (PMID:33617290)
- The Japanese Society for Palliative Medicine revised recommendations note a paucity of evidence for dyspnoea interventions in advanced disease and call for more research including observational studies. (PMID:39052451)
- The Spanish COPD guideline includes a recommendation on opioids for refractory dyspnoea in COPD. (PMID:35315327)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Evidence for individual drugs is limited.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Opioids for refractory breathlessness.
- Fans and non-drug measures first.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Lung cancer patient with refractory breathlessness at rest.

**Reviewer Gap**: Opioid dosing, benzodiazepine role and treatable causes (effusion, anaemia, PE) are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 33617290**: *Management of Dyspnea in Advanced Cancer: ASCO Guideline* (2021) [guideline]
- **PMID 35315327**: *Spanish COPD Guideline (GesEPOC) Update: Comorbidities, Self-Management and Palliative Care* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 39052451**: *Treatment Recommendation for Dyspnea in Patients with Advanced Disease: Revised Clinical Guidelines from the Japanese Society for Palliative Medicine* (2024) [guideline]

## [0594] Dystonia classification: focal (blepharospasm, torticollis) and botulinum toxin therapy

**Cluster**: dystonia | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Focal dystonias such as blepharospasm and cervical dystonia are treated first with botulinum neurotoxin, with deep brain stimulation for severe generalised or refractory forms.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of botulinum neurotoxin A found secondary treatment failure and immunogenicity vary by indication and formulation, and suggested incobotulinumtoxinA may be preferred to avoid these when efficacy is comparable. (PMID:40729416)
- Practical recommendations advise reassessing diagnosis, goals, targeting and technique before immunogenicity testing, product switching or alternatives in patients losing response to BoNT. (PMID:42506707)
- A Chinese consensus gives recommendations on 11 clinical questions for primary blepharospasm. (PMID:42379862)
- Long-term pallidal deep brain stimulation in paediatric non-degenerative dystonia appears effective with possible gene-specific differences. (PMID:38646731)
- A cerebral palsy and dystonia guideline update guides pharmacological and neurosurgical options through shared decision-making. (PMID:38640091)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Predicting DBS response by genotype.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- First-line treatment of cervical dystonia: botulinum toxin.
- Reasons for botulinum toxin treatment failure.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman with head turning to the left and neck pain, relieved by a sensory trick.

**Reviewer Gap**: Classification axes (clinical, aetiological), sensory trick and drug options (anticholinergics) are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38640091**: *Pharmacological and neurosurgical management of cerebral palsy and dystonia: Clinical practice guideline update* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38646731**: *Long-Term Globus Pallidus Internus Deep Brain Stimulation in Pediatric Non-Degenerative Dystonia: A Cohort Study and a Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40729416**: *Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Secondary Treatment Failure and Immunogenicity With Botulinum Neurotoxin A in Multiple Indications* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 42379862**: *[Chinese expert consensus on the diagnosis and treatment of primary blepharospasm (2026)]* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42506707**: *Botulinum Toxin Type A in Spasticity and Cervical Dystonia: Practical Clinical Recommendations from a Mexican Multidisciplinary Panel of Specialist Injectors* (2026) [guideline]

## [0595] Early Breast Cancer

**Cluster**: early_breast_cancer | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Adjuvant care of early breast cancer is increasingly biology-driven: CDK4/6 inhibitors for high-risk HR-positive disease, extended endocrine therapy in selected patients, and immune checkpoint inhibitors in the neoadjuvant setting for triple-negative disease.

### Core Teaching Points
- ASCO guideline updates address adjuvant CDK4/6 inhibitors, including abemaciclib with endocrine therapy for high-risk early breast cancer. (PMID:38768407, PMID:34878801)
- A patient-level meta-analysis of 12 randomised trials found extended aromatase inhibitor therapy reduced distant recurrence by about a quarter despite substantial non-adherence; mortality effects need longer follow-up. (PMID:40783288)
- Neoadjuvant immune checkpoint inhibitors plus chemotherapy have a manageable safety profile, but no benefit was observed with adjuvant checkpoint inhibitors; patient selection is a research priority. (PMID:39207778)
- Consensus guidance stresses dose intensity, adherence and early reporting of adverse effects for CDK4/6 inhibitors. (PMID:40124155)
- A follow-up consensus emphasises surveillance for treatment toxicities, quality of life and lifestyle measures after early breast cancer. (PMID:39437511)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Who benefits from extended endocrine therapy and from checkpoint inhibitors.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Adjuvant abemaciclib for high-risk HR-positive, HER2-negative disease.
- Neoadjuvant pembrolizumab concept in triple-negative cancer.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Premenopausal woman with node-positive HR+/HER2- cancer: adjuvant plan.

**Reviewer Gap**: Staging, surgical options, radiotherapy, HER2-targeted therapy and genomic assays are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 34878801**: *Abemaciclib With Endocrine Therapy in the Treatment of High-Risk Early Breast Cancer: ASCO Optimal Adjuvant Chemotherapy and Targeted Therapy Guideline Rapid Recommendation Update* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 38768407**: *Optimal Adjuvant Chemotherapy and Targeted Therapy for Early Breast Cancer-Cyclin-Dependent Kinase 4 and 6 Inhibitors: ASCO Guideline Rapid Recommendation Update* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39207778**: *Neoadjuvant Immune Checkpoint Inhibitors Plus Chemotherapy in Early Breast Cancer: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39437511**: *Follow-up of early breast cancer in a public health system: A 2024 AIGOM consensus project* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 40124155**: *Consensus Guidelines for the Use of Cyclin-Dependent Kinase (CDK) 4/6 Inhibitors in the Management of Hormone Receptor Positive (HR+ve), Her2-ve Early Breast Cancer (EBC)* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40783288**: *Extending the duration of endocrine treatment for early breast cancer: patient-level meta-analysis of 12 randomised trials of aromatase inhibitors in 22 031 postmenopausal women already treated with at least 5 years of endocrine therapy* (2025) [meta-analysis]

## [0596] early goal-directed therapy septic shock ProCESS trial

**Cluster**: egdt | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
The original 2001 single-centre trial of early goal-directed therapy showed benefit in septic shock, but three large multicentre trials (ProCESS, ARISE, ProMISe) found no mortality advantage of protocolised EGDT over usual care, reshaping sepsis resuscitation.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2001 Rivers trial concluded that early goal-directed therapy provides significant outcome benefits in severe sepsis and septic shock. (PMID:11794169)
- ProCESS found protocol-based resuscitation of septic shock diagnosed in the emergency department did not improve outcomes. (PMID:24635773)
- ARISE found EGDT did not reduce all-cause mortality at 90 days in early septic shock. (PMID:25272316)
- ProMISe found that, after adequate fluid resuscitation, management by a strict EGDT protocol did not improve outcome. (PMID:25776532)
- The Surviving Sepsis Campaign 2021 guidelines provide current international recommendations for management of sepsis and septic shock. (PMID:34599691)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Which elements of early resuscitation (fluids, antibiotics, vasopressors) drive benefit.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Why central venous oxygen saturation targets are no longer mandatory.
- Difference between Rivers 2001 and the later multicentre trials.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Hypotensive septic patient in the emergency department: what is mandatory in the first hour.

**Reviewer Gap**: Specific modern bundle elements (30 mL/kg crystalloid, lactate, antibiotics within the hour) are in the full SSC guideline but not in these abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 11794169**: *Early goal-directed therapy in the treatment of severe sepsis and septic shock* (2001) [other]
- **PMID 24635773**: *A randomized trial of protocol-based care for early septic shock* (2014) [other]
- **PMID 25272316**: *Goal-directed resuscitation for patients with early septic shock* (2014) [other]
- **PMID 25776532**: *Trial of early, goal-directed resuscitation for septic shock* (2015) [other]
- **PMID 34599691**: *Surviving sepsis campaign: international guidelines for management of sepsis and septic shock 2021* (2021) [guideline]

## [0597] Early mobilisation and physiotherapy in the ICU

**Cluster**: icu_mobilisation | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Early mobilisation of critically ill patients is supported by guidelines and may reduce delirium; recommended timing is within the first days of ICU stay, delivered as a team effort.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2025 SCCM update gave a conditional recommendation for enhanced mobilisation and rehabilitation over usual mobilisation in critically ill adults. (PMID:39982143)
- A German guideline on positioning and early mobilisation produced 46 recommendations, and its S3 summary points to mobilisation within 72 hours of ICU admission and added chapters on assistive devices and neuromuscular electrical stimulation. (PMID:39073582, PMID:40742531)
- Early mobilisation may reduce the risk and duration of delirium in critically ill patients. (PMID:40085206)
- The nurse's role in early mobilisation is variably defined, and role analysis is needed to standardise it. (PMID:39989266)
- In paediatric ICUs, graded early mobilisation matched to the child's condition may promote functional recovery. (PMID:42399446)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal intensity and timing; contribution of individual components.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Early mobilisation is part of the ABCDEF bundle and prevents ICU-acquired weakness and delirium.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Ventilated patient on day 3 with stable haemodynamics: mobilisation safety checks.

**Reviewer Gap**: Safety criteria, contraindications and functional outcome data (muscle strength, return to function) are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39073582**: *Guideline on positioning and early mobilisation in the critically ill by an expert panel* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39982143**: *A Focused Update to the Clinical Practice Guidelines for the Prevention and Management of Pain, Anxiety, Agitation/Sedation, Delirium, Immobility, and Sleep Disruption in Adult Patients in the ICU* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 39989266**: *Nurse-involved early mobilization in the intensive care unit: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40085206**: *Preventive effects of early mobilisation on delirium incidence in critically ill patients: systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40742531**: *[Positioning therapy and (early) mobilisation in intensive care units : Insights from the current S3 guideline]* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 42399446**: *Consensus statement on the use of standardized early mobilization in pediatric patients admitted to pediatric intensive care units in Italy* (2026) [guideline]

## [0599] early pregnancy bleeding

**Cluster**: early_pregnancy | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
First-trimester bleeding is assessed with ultrasound and serial hCG to separate viable pregnancy, early pregnancy loss, pregnancy of unknown location and ectopic pregnancy. Current guidelines support individualised, patient-centred management.

### Core Teaching Points
- SOGC Guideline 414 covers management of pregnancy of unknown location and tubal and non-tubal ectopic pregnancies. (PMID:33453378)
- For intrauterine early pregnancy loss, SOGC Guideline 460 recommends patient-centred care, ideally in an early pregnancy assessment clinic, so patients can make informed decisions. (PMID:40349974)
- The Society of Family Planning defines resolution after aspiration for undesired PUL by hCG decline of more than 50% at 24 hours, 70% at 48 hours or 80% at about 72 hours. (PMID:40122324)
- The RCOG recurrent miscarriage guideline suggests micronised vaginal progesterone 400 mg twice daily at the time of bleeding until 16 weeks for women with recurrent miscarriage and bleeding in early pregnancy (Grade B). (PMID:37334488)
- The 2025 ACR Appropriateness Criteria address imaging of first-trimester vaginal bleeding. (PMID:41193042)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Evidence quality varies; many treatment recommendations are conditional.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Pregnancy of unknown location: serial hCG and follow-up.
- Progesterone in recurrent miscarriage with bleeding.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman at 6 weeks with unilateral pain and spotting, empty uterus on ultrasound and hCG 1,800.

**Reviewer Gap**: Methotrexate criteria, hCG discriminatory zone, red-flag symptoms of rupture and Rh immunoglobulin are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 33453378**: *Guideline No. 414: Management of Pregnancy of Unknown Location and Tubal and Nontubal Ectopic Pregnancies* (2021) [guideline]
- **PMID 37334488**: *Recurrent MiscarriageGreen-top Guideline No. 17* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 40122324**: *Society of Family Planning Clinical Recommendation: Management of undesired pregnancy of unknown location and abortion at less than 42 days of gestation* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40349974**: *Guideline No. 460: Diagnosis and Management of Intrauterine Early Pregnancy Loss* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41193042**: *ACR Appropriateness Criteria® First Trimester Vaginal Bleeding: 2025 Update* (2025) [guideline]

---

## [0601] Early-stage NSCLC surgery versus SBRT

**Cluster**: nsclc_sbrt | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
For stage I NSCLC, surgery remains the reference for operable patients while stereotactic body radiotherapy is the preferred approach for those not considered appropriate surgical candidates; thermal ablation gives similar outcomes to SBRT in some analyses.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2025 ACCP early-stage NSCLC guideline states SBRT is the preferred approach for stage I disease in patients not appropriate for surgical resection, and that adjuvant targeted therapy improves overall survival in EGFR-mutant cancers. (PMID:40562304)
- ASCO endorsed the ASTRO SBRT guideline and emphasises multidisciplinary discussion of treatment options. (PMID:29106810)
- The ESTRO ACROP consensus gives detailed recommendations on implementation and practice of SBRT for peripherally located early-stage NSCLC. (PMID:28687397)
- A meta-analysis found comparable outcomes after SBRT and microwave ablation, both better than radiofrequency ablation. (PMID:37321074)
- Particle beam therapy showed no statistically significant survival benefit over photon SBRT after adjustment for confounders. (PMID:28545956)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- No retrieved randomised comparison of SBRT versus lobectomy in operable patients.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Treatment of stage I NSCLC in a patient with severe COPD (SBRT).
- SBRT versus ablation outcomes.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 78-year-old with FEV1 38% predicted and a 2.5 cm peripheral adenocarcinoma.

**Reviewer Gap**: The surgery versus SBRT randomised data (STARS/ROSEL pooled analysis, VALOR) and lobectomy versus sublobar resection are not in the retrieved abstracts; this page does not yet answer the 'versus' question.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 28545956**: *Comparison of particle beam therapy and stereotactic body radiotherapy for early stage non-small cell lung cancer: A systematic review and hypothesis-generating meta-analysis* (2017) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 28687397**: *ESTRO ACROP consensus guideline on implementation and practice of stereotactic body radiotherapy for peripherally located early stage non-small cell lung cancer* (2017) [guideline]
- **PMID 29106810**: *Stereotactic Body Radiotherapy for Early-Stage Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer: American Society of Clinical Oncology Endorsement of the American Society for Radiation Oncology Evidence-Based Guideline* (2018) [guideline]
- **PMID 37321074**: *Stereotactic body radiation therapy and thermal ablation for treatment of NSCLC: A systematic literature review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40562304**: *Management of Patients With Early-Stage Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: An American College of Chest Physicians Clinical Practice Guideline* (2025) [guideline]

## [0602] Eating disorder medical stabilization and refeeding

**Cluster**: eating_disorders | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Refeeding syndrome, with hypophosphataemia as its marker, is the main medical hazard when re-nourishing severely malnourished patients, including those with anorexia nervosa. Risk identification, cautious feeding and electrolyte replacement are the cornerstones.

### Core Teaching Points
- Australasian (ASPEN) consensus statements aim to improve identification and management of refeeding syndrome and risk, including electrolyte replacement considerations and route. (PMID:40090863)
- A Spanish eating-disorders consensus says refeeding syndrome must be prevented during renourishment and that anorexia nervosa in pregnancy, vegetarianism or type 1 diabetes needs specific care. (PMID:38328958)
- In hospitalised adolescents with anorexia nervosa, research is needed to define risk factors for refeeding hypophosphataemia and optimal delivery (oral versus enteral), macronutrient content and electrolyte replacement. (PMID:36123000)
- A systematic review of adult refeeding risk factors found too few high-quality studies to confirm individual factors for risk-prediction tools. (PMID:39187889)
- An umbrella review advises considering phosphate testing in high-risk scenarios and following evidence-based supplementation guidelines. (PMID:41146174)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal starting calorie level and risk-prediction rules.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Biochemical hallmark of refeeding syndrome: low phosphate, potassium and magnesium; thiamine.
- High-risk patients for refeeding.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Adolescent with anorexia nervosa BMI 14 admitted for renourishment; day-3 weakness and phosphate 0.4.

**Reviewer Gap**: NICE risk criteria, starting energy, thiamine dosing and electrolyte thresholds are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 36123000**: *Refeeding Hypophosphatemia in Hospitalized Adolescents With Anorexia Nervosa* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 38328958**: *[A consensus report by the Working Group on Eating Disorders of Sociedad Española de Nutrición Clínica y Metabolismo (GTTCA-SENPE). Evaluation, medical and nutritional management of anorexia nervosa. Update 2023]* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39187889**: *Risk factors for the development of refeeding syndrome in adults: A systematic review* (2025) [systematic review]
- **PMID 40090863**: *The Australasian Society of Parenteral and Enteral Nutrition: Consensus statements on refeeding syndrome* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41146174**: *Indications for the evaluation and supplementation of hypophosphatemia: an umbrella systematic review of reviews and guidelines* (2025) [guideline]

---

## [0604] ebola virus disease therapeutics and supportive care

**Cluster**: ebola | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Ebola disease still carries high case fatality, and monoclonal antibody therapy (REGN-EB3 or mAb114 as Inmazeb or Ebanga) plus optimised supportive care is the evidence-based standard in WHO guidance.

### Core Teaching Points
- A network meta-analysis found REGN-EB3 and mAb114 each reduced mortality compared with ZMapp, remdesivir or standard care, although certainty was low or very low in most comparisons. (PMID:35803293)
- WHO therapeutics guidance emphasises recommended monoclonal antibodies together with optimised supportive care, and gives practical guidance on administration. (PMID:42412964)
- The WHO guideline on clinical management of filovirus disease stresses timely assessment, individualised treatment and integration with infection prevention and control. (PMID:42384837)
- Case fatality for Ebola disease remains high and heterogeneous, so early diagnosis, early treatment where available and supportive care are important. (PMID:37992431)
- Survivors have persistent somatic symptoms; arthralgia, myalgia and abdominal pain decrease over time while headache, fatigue, numbness and hearing loss increase. (PMID:38840082)
- ACIP recommends Ervebo vaccine for specified at-risk groups such as responders and laboratory staff at biosafety level 4 facilities. (PMID:33417593)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Low-certainty comparative evidence.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Monoclonal antibodies for Ebola (Inmazeb, Ebanga) versus ZMapp.
- Post-Ebola syndrome in survivors.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Returning healthcare worker from an outbreak area with fever, vomiting and bleeding.

**Reviewer Gap**: Infection control, fluid and electrolyte supportive care specifics and diagnostics are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 33417593**: *Use of Ebola Vaccine: Recommendations of the Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices, United States, 2020* (2021) [guideline]
- **PMID 35803293**: *Effects of therapies for Ebola virus disease: a systematic review and network meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 37992431**: *Case fatality rate for Ebola disease, 1976-2022: A meta-analysis of global data* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38840082**: *Prevalence of somatic symptoms among Ebola Virus Disease (EVD) survivors in Africa: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 42384837**: *WHO guidelines for the clinical management of filovirus disease* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42412964**: *Therapeutics for Ebola virus disease: 19 August 2022* (2022) [guideline]

## [0605] ECG interpretation: common acute findings and life-threatening arrhythmias

**Cluster**: ecg_interpretation | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Acute ECG interpretation is time-critical, particularly in STEMI, where prehospital ECG transmission and short ECG-to-device times improve survival. Retrieval gave STEMI-focused evidence and no general ECG interpretation source, so this page is limited.

### Core Teaching Points
- Prehospital digital ECG transmission in STEMI networks reduced door-to-device and first-medical-contact-to-device times and was associated with 47% lower mortality (OR 0.53). (PMID:39525334)
- ECG-diagnosis-to-sheath time under 90 minutes was associated with lower 30-day mortality (10.5% versus 17.7%), with a number needed to prevent one death of 39 overall and 14 in the elderly. (PMID:36056722)
- In inferior STEMI, ST elevation in III greater than II together with ST depression in aVL greater than I may be the most accurate and convenient ECG method to identify the culprit artery; complex algorithms were not superior. (PMID:36317727)
- Updated considerations for ECG screening of young athletes address early repolarisation, left atrial abnormality, right bundle branch block, ST shifts and high and low QRS voltage. (PMID:40081638)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Basic systematic ECG interpretation (rate, rhythm, axis, intervals) and life-threatening arrhythmias are not covered by retrieved abstracts.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Culprit artery in inferior STEMI from ECG.
- Time targets from ECG to primary PCI.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with chest pain and ST elevation in II, III and aVF.

**Reviewer Gap**: Systematic ECG interpretation steps, STEMI criteria, high-risk arrhythmia recognition (VT, AF with pre-excitation, heart block) and posterior/right-sided leads are not in the retrieved abstracts; needs a general ECG source before approval.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 36056722**: *Guideline-Recommended Time Less Than 90 Minutes From ECG to Primary Percutaneous Coronary Intervention for ST-Segment-Elevation Myocardial Infarction Is Associated with Major Survival Benefits, Especially in Octogenarians: A Contemporary Report in 11 226 Patients from NORIC* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 36317727**: *Identifying the culprit artery via 12-lead electrocardiogram in inferior wall ST-segment elevation myocardial infarction: A meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39525334**: *Effect of Prehospital Digital Electrocardiogram Transmission on Revascularization Delays and Mortality in ST-Elevation Myocardial Infarction Patients: Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40081638**: *Proposed enhanced recommendations for interpretation of electrocardiographic screening of athletes* (2025) [guideline]

## [0606] Ectopic ACTH syndrome: small cell lung cancer, CRH stimulation, metyrapone treatment

**Cluster**: ectopic_acth | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
ACTH-dependent Cushing syndrome is split into pituitary Cushing disease and ectopic ACTH secretion using dynamic testing, imaging and inferior petrosal sinus sampling. Pheochromocytomas can rarely secrete ACTH or CRH.

### Core Teaching Points
- A positive ACTH response after the CRH test makes Cushing disease highly likely in a patient with ACTH-dependent Cushing syndrome. (PMID:36453141)
- Bilateral inferior petrosal sinus sampling offers very high sensitivity and specificity for localising ACTH pathology when performed to consensus technique. (PMID:42088331)
- Prolactin adjustment in BIPSS may help recognise Cushing disease but may not help identify ectopic ACTH syndrome. (PMID:36649781)
- Ectopic ACTH- or CRH-secreting pheochromocytoma should be considered in ACTH-dependent Cushing syndrome with an adrenal mass; outcomes appear favourable. (PMID:32710791)
- The 2008 Endocrine Society recommendations on whom to screen for Cushing syndrome remained valid in a 2022 reassessment, and long-term comorbidity surveillance is needed even after remission. (PMID:35730067, PMID:37536275)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Accuracy of desmopressin testing.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Differentiating pituitary from ectopic ACTH sources: CRH test, high-dose dexamethasone and IPSS.
- Ectopic source after negative pituitary MRI.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Rapidly progressive hypokalaemia, hyperglycaemia and hyperpigmentation in a smoker with a lung mass.

**Reviewer Gap**: Small cell lung cancer as the commonest ectopic source, metyrapone/ketoconazole control of hypercortisolism and the high-dose dexamethasone test are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 32710791**: *Ectopic ACTH- and/or CRH-Producing Pheochromocytomas* (2021) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 35730067**: *Whom Should We Screen for Cushing Syndrome? The Endocrine Society Practice Guideline Recommendations 2008 Revisited* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 36453141**: *Dynamic Testing for Differential Diagnosis of ACTH-Dependent Cushing Syndrome: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 36649781**: *Should We Use Prolactin Adjustment in Bilateral Inferior Petrosal Sinus Sampling to Diagnose Cushing Disease? A Joint Meta-Analysis of Head-to-Head Diagnostic Tests Accuracy Studies* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 37536275**: *Long-Term Consequences of Cushing Syndrome: A Systematic Literature Review* (2024) [systematic review]
- **PMID 42088331**: *Consensus Guidelines on Inferior Petrosal Sinus Sampling: A Guideline From the Society of Vascular and Interventional Neurology Guidelines and Practice Standards Committee* (2026) [guideline]

## [0607] ectopic pregnancy

**Cluster**: early_pregnancy | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
First-trimester bleeding is assessed with ultrasound and serial hCG to separate viable pregnancy, early pregnancy loss, pregnancy of unknown location and ectopic pregnancy. Current guidelines support individualised, patient-centred management.

### Core Teaching Points
- SOGC Guideline 414 covers management of pregnancy of unknown location and tubal and non-tubal ectopic pregnancies. (PMID:33453378)
- For intrauterine early pregnancy loss, SOGC Guideline 460 recommends patient-centred care, ideally in an early pregnancy assessment clinic, so patients can make informed decisions. (PMID:40349974)
- The Society of Family Planning defines resolution after aspiration for undesired PUL by hCG decline of more than 50% at 24 hours, 70% at 48 hours or 80% at about 72 hours. (PMID:40122324)
- The RCOG recurrent miscarriage guideline suggests micronised vaginal progesterone 400 mg twice daily at the time of bleeding until 16 weeks for women with recurrent miscarriage and bleeding in early pregnancy (Grade B). (PMID:37334488)
- The 2025 ACR Appropriateness Criteria address imaging of first-trimester vaginal bleeding. (PMID:41193042)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Evidence quality varies; many treatment recommendations are conditional.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Pregnancy of unknown location: serial hCG and follow-up.
- Progesterone in recurrent miscarriage with bleeding.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman at 6 weeks with unilateral pain and spotting, empty uterus on ultrasound and hCG 1,800.

**Reviewer Gap**: Methotrexate criteria, hCG discriminatory zone, red-flag symptoms of rupture and Rh immunoglobulin are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 33453378**: *Guideline No. 414: Management of Pregnancy of Unknown Location and Tubal and Nontubal Ectopic Pregnancies* (2021) [guideline]
- **PMID 37334488**: *Recurrent MiscarriageGreen-top Guideline No. 17* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 40122324**: *Society of Family Planning Clinical Recommendation: Management of undesired pregnancy of unknown location and abortion at less than 42 days of gestation* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40349974**: *Guideline No. 460: Diagnosis and Management of Intrauterine Early Pregnancy Loss* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41193042**: *ACR Appropriateness Criteria® First Trimester Vaginal Bleeding: 2025 Update* (2025) [guideline]

## [0609] eczema

**Cluster**: eczema | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Atopic dermatitis is a chronic inflammatory skin disease managed with emollients and topical anti-inflammatories, escalating to targeted systemic therapy (biologics and JAK inhibitors) for moderate to severe disease. Living guidelines now cover the newest agents.

### Core Teaching Points
- The EuroGuiDerm living guideline covers conventional immunosuppressants (azathioprine, ciclosporin, glucocorticosteroids, methotrexate, mycophenolate), biologics (dupilumab, lebrikizumab, nemolizumab, tralokinumab) and JAK inhibitors (abrocitinib, baricitinib, upadacitinib), with attention to paediatric, adolescent and pregnant patients. (PMID:40317496)
- The 2025 AAD focused update made strong recommendations for tapinarof cream, roflumilast cream, lebrikizumab and nemolizumab with concomitant topical therapy in adults. (PMID:40531067)
- Most trials of atopic dermatitis therapy are short, which limits conclusions about long-term comparative efficacy and safety. (PMID:40531067)
- The German-language S3 guideline Part 2 covers systemic treatment, including dupilumab, tralokinumab, JAK inhibitors, ciclosporin and systemic glucocorticoids. (PMID:38161245)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Long-term comparative safety of biologics versus JAK inhibitors.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Which classes of systemic therapy exist for moderate to severe atopic dermatitis.
- Topical non-steroid options (tapinarof, roflumilast) with strong recommendation in adults.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Adult with severe flexural eczema failing topical steroids and emollients: next step?

**Reviewer Gap**: The existing 'dermatitis' and 'eczema' DB topics are generic ai_generated entries; contact and seborrhoeic dermatitis are not covered here.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38161245**: *S3 guideline Atopic dermatitis: Part 2 - Systemic treatment* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 40317496**: *European Guideline (EuroGuiDerm) on atopic eczema: Living update* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40531067**: *Focused update: Guidelines of care for the management of atopic dermatitis in adults* (2025) [guideline]

## [0610] eGFR: Estimation and Limitations

**Cluster**: egfr | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
eGFR equations estimate filtration from creatinine and/or cystatin C; accuracy is limited in extremes of body composition and disease. Moving to race-free equations and adding cystatin C or measured GFR improves decisions.

### Core Teaching Points
- US pharmacy and medication-safety bodies advise moving from Cockcroft-Gault creatinine clearance to race-free eGFR equations for medication-related decisions, for more accurate and consistent assessment. (PMID:39552516)
- In patients with cancer, eGFR should incorporate both creatinine and cystatin C, and measured GFR with an exogenous filtration marker should be considered when eGFR gives borderline eligibility for therapy or trials. (PMID:38848131)
- Creatinine-based eGFR equations underestimate renal clearance in patients with sarcopenia or severe muscle wasting and overestimate clearance in muscular individuals; the 2021 CKD-EPI creatinine-cystatin C combined equation significantly improves diagnostic accuracy without using race variables. (PMID:39552516, PMID:38848131)
- Measured GFR using exogenous tracers (iohexol, 51Cr-EDTA, or iothalamate) remains the definitive reference standard in oncology for nephrotoxic chemotherapies (such as cisplatin and high-dose methotrexate) where narrow therapeutic indices preclude estimation errors. (PMID:38848131)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Impact of muscle mass, obesity and acute illness on creatinine-based eGFR is not covered in these sources.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Why cystatin C adds value in sarcopenia or cancer.
- Race-free CKD-EPI equations.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Cachectic cancer patient with creatinine-based eGFR that seems falsely reassuring.

**Reviewer Gap**: Limitations list (non-steady state, extremes of muscle mass, drugs blocking tubular creatinine secretion), CKD-EPI 2021 and KDIGO staging thresholds are not in the retrieved abstracts; thin page.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38848131**: *Assessment of GFR in Patients with Cancer: A Statement from the American Society of Onco-Nephrology* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39552516**: *Moving forward from Cockcroft-Gault creatinine clearance to race-free estimated glomerular filtration rate to improve medication-related decision-making in adults across healthcare settings: A consensus of the National Kidney Foundation Workgroup for Implementation of Race-Free eGFR-Based Medication-Related Decisions* (2025) [guideline]

## [0611] Electrical storm: refractory VT/VF management, stellate ganglion block

**Cluster**: electrical_storm | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Electrical storm needs urgent antiarrhythmic therapy, sedation and treatment of triggers; for refractory cases, sympathetic modulation (stellate ganglion block, cardiac sympathetic denervation, renal denervation) and catheter ablation are options.

### Core Teaching Points
- The EHRA 2024 consensus covers pathophysiology, presentation, diagnostic evaluation and acute and long-term management of electrical storm and clustered ventricular arrhythmias. (PMID:38584423)
- A systematic review found stellate ganglion blockade significantly decreased ventricular arrhythmia burden (12.4 to 1.04 episodes per day) in electrical storm, though larger prospective studies are needed; a later meta-analysis of observational data agrees. (PMID:29270467, PMID:39433834)
- Cardiac sympathetic denervation was associated with 60% VT non-recurrence at mean 15-month follow-up and fewer ICD shocks. (PMID:32460366)
- Renal denervation reduced ICD therapies in refractory ventricular arrhythmias and electrical storm, although randomised data are lacking. (PMID:33478701)
- In CPVT, ICD use is associated with high shock burden and complications, and adherence to guideline-directed adjuvant antiarrhythmic therapy is poor. (PMID:30063211)
- The 2022 ESC ventricular arrhythmia guideline added new recommendations for Brugada syndrome and primary electrical disease. (PMID:37102266)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Sympathetic modulation evidence is observational.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Beta-blockade and sedation as the cornerstone of electrical storm.
- Role of stellate ganglion block.

### Case Generation Hooks
- ICD recipient with five appropriate shocks in 24 hours.

**Reviewer Gap**: Immediate drug regimen (amiodarone, lidocaine, propranolol) and catheter ablation indications are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 29270467**: *Efficacy of Stellate Ganglion Blockade in Managing Electrical Storm: A Systematic Review* (2017) [systematic review]
- **PMID 30063211**: *Implantable cardioverter-defibrillator use in catecholaminergic polymorphic ventricular tachycardia: A systematic review* (2018) [systematic review]
- **PMID 32460366**: *Role of cardiac sympathetic denervation in ventricular tachycardia: A meta-analysis* (2020) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 33478701**: *Renal Denervation for the Management of Refractory Ventricular Arrhythmias: A Systematic Review* (2021) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 37102266**: *Spotlight on the 2022 ESC guideline management of ventricular arrhythmias and prevention of sudden cardiac death: 10 novel key aspects* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 38584423**: *Management of patients with an electrical storm or clustered ventricular arrhythmias: a clinical consensus statement of the European Heart Rhythm Association of the ESC-endorsed by the Asia-Pacific Heart Rhythm Society, Heart Rhythm Society, and Latin-American Heart Rhythm Society* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39433834**: *Efficacy of stellate ganglion block in treatment of electrical storm: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]

## [0612] electrolyte disturbances

**Cluster**: electrolytes | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
This topic is very broad. The retrieved sources deal with hyperkalaemia in patients who need RAAS inhibitors, and hyponatraemia work-up in cirrhosis. A narrower scope is needed before a useful page can be approved.

### Core Teaching Points
- Consensus panels in the US and Japan advise treating RAAS-inhibitor-induced hyperkalaemia in heart failure and CKD while maintaining optimal RAASi therapy, to preserve the cardiovascular benefit of these drugs. (PMID:39809248, PMID:40074356)
- In cirrhosis with hyponatraemia, the AGA expert review advises reviewing diuretic and medication history, electrolytes and kidney function, GI bleeding, and infection including diagnostic paracentesis. (PMID:41114681)
- Novel potassium-binding polymers (patiromer and sodium zirconium cyclosilicate, SZC) facilitate sustained maintenance of guideline-directed medical therapy (GDMT) with ACE inhibitors, ARBs, and mineralocorticoid receptor antagonists in patients with heart failure and CKD who develop hyperkalemia. (PMID:39809248, PMID:40074356)
- Hypervolemic hyponatremia in decompensated cirrhosis reflects non-osmotic hypersecretion of arginine vasopressin driven by severe effective arterial underfilling; fluid restriction (1,000-1,500 mL/day) is indicated when serum sodium drops below 125 mmol/L, avoiding rapid correction that risks osmotic demyelination syndrome. (PMID:41114681)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Treatment algorithms for each electrolyte are not covered.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Managing hyperkalaemia while keeping RAAS inhibitors.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Heart failure patient on spironolactone with potassium 5.9.

**Reviewer Gap**: Sodium, potassium, calcium and magnesium disorders each need their own source; consider replacing this topic with specific entries.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39809248**: *EMCREG-International Multidisciplinary Consensus Panel on Management of Hyperkalemia in Chronic Kidney Disease and Heart Failure* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40074356**: *Establishing Cross-Specialty Expert Consensus on the Optimal Management of Hyperkalemia in Patients With Heart Failure and Chronic Kidney Disease* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41114681**: *AGA Clinical Practice Update on the Management of Ascites, Volume Overload, and Hyponatremia in Cirrhosis: Expert Review* (2025) [guideline]

## [0613] Emergency contraception: mechanisms, timing and clinical guidance

**Cluster**: emergency_contraception | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Emergency contraception includes oral options and the copper IUD used within days of unprotected intercourse. Network meta-analysis found mifepristone associated with fewer pregnancies than levonorgestrel, and the SFP recommendation names research gaps on weight, repeated use and re-starting hormonal contraception.

### Core Teaching Points
- A Cochrane network meta-analysis found low-dose and mid-dose mifepristone probably associated with fewer pregnancies than levonorgestrel (RR 0.72 and 0.61). (PMID:30661244)
- The SFP clinical recommendation reviews indications, effectiveness, safety and side effects of EC methods, special populations and access barriers. (PMID:36693445)
- The SFP identifies research needs on LNG IUD effectiveness for EC, repeated use of ulipristal acetate in one cycle, the effect on ovulation when restarting regular hormonal contraception after ulipristal, and effective pill options by BMI or weight. (PMID:36693445)
- ACOG Committee Opinion 707 examines barriers to emergency contraception, emphasises increasing access and reviews limitations of efficacy in special populations. (PMID:28644339)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Effectiveness by body weight.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Most effective emergency contraception: copper IUD (not in abstracts).
- Ulipristal and starting regular hormonal contraception afterwards.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman presenting 72 hours after unprotected intercourse.

**Reviewer Gap**: Time windows, relative efficacy of copper IUD versus oral methods and drug interactions are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 28644339**: *Committee Opinion No 707: Access to Emergency Contraception* (2017) [guideline]
- **PMID 30661244**: *Interventions for emergency contraception* (2019) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 36693445**: *Society of Family Planning Clinical Recommendation: Emergency contraception* (2023) [guideline]

## [0614] empagliflozin cardiovascular outcomes type 2 diabetes EMPA-REG

**Cluster**: empagliflozin | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Empagliflozin reduces cardiovascular and kidney events across diabetes, heart failure and CKD populations, with a well-characterised safety profile. Kidney benefit does not depend on diabetes status or the early eGFR dip.

### Core Teaching Points
- An individual-participant meta-analysis of four large trials confirmed the long-term safety and tolerability of empagliflozin. (PMID:38771475)
- Empagliflozin's kidney benefits were evident irrespective of the size of the acute eGFR dip, diabetes status, heart failure status and primary cause of kidney disease. (PMID:41082889)
- SGLT2 inhibitors help delay progression of non-diabetic chronic kidney disease. (PMID:37046125)
- In type 2 diabetes, network meta-analysis suggested empagliflozin might be better than other SGLT2 inhibitors for all-cause mortality and cardiovascular events, and may be preferred in patients without CKD; these are indirect comparisons. (PMID:35370961, PMID:38293914)
- Empagliflozin and dapagliflozin may reduce the relative risk of sudden cardiac death in adjudicated trial data. (PMID:40972782)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Indirect comparisons between SGLT2 inhibitors; ad hoc RCTs needed.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Expected small eGFR dip after SGLT2 inhibitor initiation and why it should not prompt withdrawal.
- Benefit in non-diabetic CKD and heart failure.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Non-diabetic patient with CKD stage 3 and albuminuria: indication for empagliflozin.

**Reviewer Gap**: EMPA-REG OUTCOME headline figures (3-point MACE and cardiovascular death reduction), genital infection and ketoacidosis counselling are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35370961**: *Comparative Cardiovascular Outcomes of SGLT2 Inhibitors in Type 2 Diabetes Mellitus: A Network Meta-Analysis of Randomized Controlled Trials* (2022) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 37046125**: *The efficacy and safety of SGLT2 inhibitors in patients with non-diabetic chronic kidney disease: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38293914**: *Comparison of Effectiveness Among Different Sodium-Glucose Cotransoporter-2 Inhibitors According to Underlying Conditions: A Network Meta-Analysis of Randomized Controlled Trials* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38771475**: *Safety of Empagliflozin: An Individual Participant-Level Data Meta-Analysis from Four Large Trials* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40972782**: *Impact of empagliflozin and dapagliflozin on sudden cardiac death: A systematic review and meta-analysis of adjudicated randomized evidence* (2026) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41082889**: *Effects of empagliflozin on conventional and exploratory acute and chronic kidney outcomes: an individual participant-level meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
