## [0616] Empirical antibiotic selection in sepsis: source control, cultures before antibiotics, de-escalation

**Cluster**: sepsis_antibiotics | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Early appropriate empirical antimicrobials, cultures before antibiotics and prompt source control are the pillars of sepsis care. Most supporting recommendations rest on low-certainty evidence, so they are mostly weak recommendations or best practice statements.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ESCMID emergency-department antimicrobial stewardship guideline states most recommendations rest on very low or low certainty evidence, leading to weak recommendations or best practice statements. (PMID:39029872)
- A scoping review of source control in bloodstream infection in sepsis found inconsistent definitions and poor efficacy assessment, with catheter-source infections and candidaemia over-represented; standardised reporting is needed. (PMID:40658248)
- In neonates at risk of early-onset sepsis, antibiotics should be stopped as early as possible within the first 24 hours when sepsis is not confirmed, and therapy should be streamlined when infection is proven. (PMID:39417838)
- The Surviving Sepsis Campaign 2026 paediatric guideline highlights flexibility, early recognition, cautious intervention and long-term recovery, and recognises large evidence gaps. (PMID:42329352)
- The Surviving Sepsis Campaign 2021 adult guidelines remain the international reference for management of sepsis and septic shock. (PMID:34599691)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal timing of antibiotics in uncertain sepsis; benefits of source control are poorly quantified.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Blood cultures before antibiotics provided it does not delay therapy.
- Why antibiotics should be reviewed and streamlined at 24-48 hours.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Febrile hypotensive patient with abdominal pain: empirical regimen and source control.

**Reviewer Gap**: Specific empirical regimens by source, local-resistance adjustment, and time-to-antibiotic targets are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 34599691**: *Surviving sepsis campaign: international guidelines for management of sepsis and septic shock 2021* (2021) [guideline]
- **PMID 39029872**: *European society of clinical microbiology and infectious diseases guidelines for antimicrobial stewardship in emergency departments (endorsed by European association of hospital pharmacists)* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39417838**: *Management of neonates at risk of early onset sepsis: a probability-based approach and recent literature appraisal : Update of the Swiss national guideline of the Swiss Society of Neonatology and the Pediatric Infectious Disease Group Switzerland* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 40658248**: *Source control in bloodstream infections in patients with sepsis, septic shock, or requiring ICU admission: a scoping review with recommendations for standardizing research* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 42329352**: *Management of Sepsis and Septic Shock in Children: Surviving Sepsis Campaign International Guidelines 2026* (2026) [guideline]

---

## [0617] enalapril ACE inhibitor survival reduced left ventricular ejection fraction

**Cluster**: ace_inhibitor_hfref | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Enalapril established ACE inhibitors as foundational therapy for heart failure with reduced ejection fraction: CONSENSUS and SOLVD showed lower mortality and fewer heart failure hospitalisations, and SOLVD Prevention showed benefit even in asymptomatic left ventricular dysfunction.

### Core Teaching Points
- CONSENSUS concluded that adding enalapril to conventional therapy in severe congestive heart failure can reduce mortality and improve symptoms, the effect on mortality coming from fewer deaths from progression of heart failure. (PMID:2883575)
- SOLVD Treatment showed enalapril added to conventional therapy significantly reduced mortality and hospitalisations for heart failure in chronic heart failure with reduced ejection fraction. (PMID:2057034)
- SOLVD Prevention showed enalapril reduced the incidence of heart failure and related hospitalisations in asymptomatic left ventricular dysfunction, with a trend toward fewer cardiovascular deaths. (PMID:1463530)
- Contemporary HFrEF therapies (ARBs, ARNIs, SGLT2 inhibitors, ivabradine, hydralazine-nitrate and IV iron) improved health-related quality of life. (PMID:33634543)
- Guideline-recommended therapies since 2015 reduced mortality and morbidity except digoxin, which reduced only heart failure hospitalisation. (PMID:40835369)
- GDMT should not be inappropriately held during heart failure hospitalisation; discharge tools and post-discharge titration clinics help keep regimens optimised. (PMID:33309579)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Sequencing and combination of the four pillars of therapy is outside these sources.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Landmark trials establishing ACE inhibitors in HFrEF (CONSENSUS, SOLVD).
- Benefit of ACE inhibitor in asymptomatic LV dysfunction.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with new LVEF 30%, NYHA II: starting ACE inhibitor and monitoring renal function and potassium.

**Reviewer Gap**: Exact mortality figures, dosing and the switch to ARNI (PARADIGM-HF) are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 1463530**: *Effect of enalapril on mortality and the development of heart failure in asymptomatic patients with reduced left ventricular ejection fractions* (1992) [other]
- **PMID 2057034**: *Effect of enalapril on survival in patients with reduced left ventricular ejection fractions and congestive heart failure* (1991) [other]
- **PMID 2883575**: *Effects of enalapril on mortality in severe congestive heart failure. Results of the Cooperative North Scandinavian Enalapril Survival Study (CONSENSUS)* (1987) [other]
- **PMID 33309579**: *Heart Failure Hospitalization and Guideline-Directed Prescribing Patterns Among Heart Failure With Reduced Ejection Fraction Patients* (2021) [guideline]
- **PMID 33634543**: *Pharmacotherapy for heart failure with reduced ejection fraction and health-related quality of life: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40835369**: *Evidentiary Landscape of Heart Failure Therapies, Regulatory Decisions, and Translation Into Guidelines* (2025) [guideline]

## [0618] Endemic Mycoses

**Cluster**: endemic_mycoses | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Endemic mycoses such as histoplasmosis present as pulmonary, disseminated or mediastinal disease, and risk is higher in patients on TNF inhibitors or with HIV. The IDSA updated its histoplasmosis guidelines in 2025.

### Core Teaching Points
- IDSA issued 2025 histoplasmosis guideline updates in several parts, including treatment of mild or moderate disease, using GRADE methodology. (PMID:40637160, PMID:40667709, PMID:40625036)
- Severe histoplasmosis in children should be treated with liposomal amphotericin B followed by itraconazole; rituximab for fibrosing mediastinitis needs clinical trials. (PMID:42566694)
- The incidence of histoplasmosis in patients on TNF-alpha inhibitors may be considerably higher than classic estimates, especially in IBD, and clinicians should stay vigilant with regular monitoring. (PMID:38065857, PMID:40065425)
- In people with HIV in Europe, untreated histoplasmosis was associated with higher mortality. (PMID:38993488)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Burden in large endemic areas such as Central and South America is poorly studied.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Severe disseminated histoplasmosis: amphotericin B then itraconazole.
- Fungal infection risk with TNF inhibitors.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient on infliximab with fever, pancytopenia and hepatosplenomegaly after travel to the Ohio valley.

**Reviewer Gap**: Blastomycosis and coccidioidomycosis, antigen testing and the detailed IDSA recommendations are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38065857**: *Incidence of histoplasmosis in patients receiving TNF-alpha inhibitors: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38993488**: *Histoplasmosis in patients living with HIV in Europe: review of literature* (2024) [systematic review]
- **PMID 40065425**: *Fungal Infections Associated With TNF-Inhibitors: A 20-Year of a Systematic Review Fungal Infections and TNF-Inhibitors* (2025) [systematic review]
- **PMID 40625036**: *2025 Clinical Practice Guideline Update by the Infectious Diseases Society of America on Histoplasmosis: Treatment of Asymptomatic Histoplasma Pulmonary Nodules (Histoplasmomas) in Adults, Children, and Pregnant People* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 40637160**: *2025 Clinical Practice Guideline Update by the Infectious Diseases Society of America on Histoplasmosis: Treatment of Mild or Moderate Acute Pulmonary Histoplasmosis in Adults, Children, and Pregnant People* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 40667709**: *2025 Clinical Practice Guideline Update by the Infectious Diseases Society of America on Histoplasmosis: Treatment of Asymptomatic Histoplasma Pulmonary Nodules (Histoplasmomas) and Mild or Moderate Acute Pulmonary Histoplasmosis in Adults, Children, and Pregnant People* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42566694**: *Histoplasmosis in children: emerging insights and evolving guidelines* (2026) [guideline]

## [0619] Endobronchial ultrasound-guided transbronchial needle aspiration

**Cluster**: ebus | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
EBUS-TBNA is the standard minimally invasive test to diagnose and stage lung cancer, with high suitability for PD-L1 and sufficient yield for next-generation sequencing when enough passes are obtained.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2026 ERS/ESGE/ESTS guideline states endobronchial and oesophageal endosonography provide accurate, minimally invasive diagnosis and staging of lung cancer, and EBUS-TBNA has a high suitability rate for PD-L1 assessment. (PMID:42167778)
- The success of EBUS-TBNA sampling for next-generation sequencing rises with the number of passes. (PMID:35151114)
- Adding intranodal forceps biopsy to EBUS-TBNA increases diagnostic yield for intrathoracic adenopathy, with higher complication rates than TBNA alone but lower than surgical biopsy. (PMID:33485918)
- Mediastinal cryobiopsy (cryoEBUS) better defined lymphoma subtypes and allowed complete molecular characterisation in lung cancer, with no serious complications described. (PMID:37579981)
- ACCP guidance on EBUS-TBNA sample acquisition and handling finds evidence satisfactory in some areas but notes more research is needed. (PMID:39343294)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Standardisation of specimen handling and optimal sampling for biomarkers.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- EBUS-TBNA for N2/N3 staging before surgery.
- Passes needed for molecular testing.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with a right upper-lobe mass and enlarged subcarinal nodes: choice of sampling.

**Reviewer Gap**: Station sampling order, sensitivity for nodal staging and complications are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 33485918**: *Combined EBUS-IFB and EBUS-TBNA vs EBUS-TBNA Alone for Intrathoracic Adenopathy: A Meta-Analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 35151114**: *A systematic review and meta-analysis of the adequacy of endobronchial ultrasound transbronchial needle aspiration for next-generation sequencing in patients with non-small cell lung cancer* (2022) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 37579981**: *Is the diagnostic yield of mediastinal lymph node cryobiopsy (cryoEBUS) better for diagnosing mediastinal node involvement compared to endobronchial ultrasound-guided transbronchial needle aspiration (EBUS-TBNA)? A systematic review* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39343294**: *Acquisition and Handling of Endobronchial Ultrasound Transbronchial Needle Samples: An American College of Chest Physicians Clinical Practice Guideline* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 42167778**: *ERS/ESGE/ESTS clinical practice guidelines on endobronchial and oesophageal endosonography for the diagnosis and staging of lung cancer* (2026) [guideline]

## [0620] Endocarditis and prophylaxis

**Cluster**: endocarditis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Antibiotic prophylaxis before invasive dental procedures lowers endocarditis risk in high-risk individuals but not in low-risk people, consistent with AHA and ESC recommendations. Treatment follows the 2023 ESC guideline, which added early surgery for prosthetic valve endocarditis.

### Core Teaching Points
- A 2024 meta-analysis found antibiotic prophylaxis before invasive dental procedures was associated with lower infective endocarditis incidence in high-risk individuals, with no proven association in low or unknown risk groups, supporting AHA and ESC recommendations. (PMID:38581643)
- The 2023 ESC guideline includes a new class I recommendation for surgery in early (under 6 months) prosthetic valve endocarditis and guidance on device reimplantation after cardiac device infection. (PMID:41410976)
- Outpatient parenteral antibiotic therapy in selected endocarditis patients had low mortality and relapse rates but more readmissions and IV-line complications, so patient selection and monitoring matter. (PMID:40365768)
- An AHA advisory summarises percutaneous mechanical aspiration for right-sided infective endocarditis and provides consensus on patient selection. (PMID:42095279)
- A review of MRSA bacteraemia treatment covers available agents, knowledge gaps and recent developments. (PMID:42346648)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Population-level benefit of dental prophylaxis in lower-risk groups.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Who needs antibiotic prophylaxis before dental work (prosthetic valve, previous IE, certain congenital disease).
- Indications for early surgery.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with a mechanical aortic valve needing a dental extraction.

**Reviewer Gap**: The NICE position (no routine prophylaxis) differs from AHA/ESC and is not in the retrieved abstracts; the explicit list of high-risk cardiac lesions is also not stated.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38581643**: *Antibiotic Prophylaxis and Infective Endocarditis Incidence Following Invasive Dental Procedures: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40365768**: *Safety and Efficacy of Outpatient Parenteral Antibiotic Therapy (OPAT) in Patients With Infective Endocarditis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41410976**: *2023 European Society of Cardiology guidelines for the management of infective endocarditis : Statement of endorsement by the NVVC Full version* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42095279**: *Percutaneous Mechanical Aspiration in Patients With Right-Sided Infective Endocarditis: A Science Advisory From the American Heart Association* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42346648**: *Treatment of Methicillin-Resistant &lt;i&gt;Staphylococcus aureus&lt;/i&gt; (MRSA) Bacteremia-Guidelines and Recent Developments* (2026) [guideline]

---

## [0623] Endocrine causes of amenorrhoea: hypothalamic, pituitary, ovarian, thyroid - diagnostic workup

**Cluster**: amenorrhoea | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Evaluation of amenorrhoea follows a structured committee opinion, with hypothalamic causes such as the female athlete triad forming a major group that needs screening and a multidisciplinary approach.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2024 ASRM committee opinion on the current evaluation of amenorrhoea replaces the 2008 version and covers primary and secondary amenorrhoea. (PMID:38456861)
- The 2025 Female Athlete Triad Coalition consensus provides evidence-graded clinical guidelines for screening, diagnosis and treatment, extended to an adolescent model. (PMID:41474492, PMID:41474493)
- Functional hypothalamic amenorrhoea has important psychological factors, and longitudinal studies with clinical psychologists in assessment are recommended. (PMID:36777338)
- In adolescents, amenorrhoea distinguished PCOS from physiologic immaturity of the hypothalamic-pituitary-ovarian axis, and BMI was nearly one third higher in PCOS. (PMID:38818646)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal treatment of functional hypothalamic amenorrhoea.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Female athlete triad: low energy availability, menstrual dysfunction and low bone density.
- Distinguishing hypothalamic from PCOS amenorrhoea.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Distance runner with BMI 17 and 6 months of amenorrhoea.

**Reviewer Gap**: The stepwise work-up (pregnancy test, TSH, prolactin, FSH, progestin challenge) and pituitary and ovarian causes are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 36777338**: *Psychological factors in functional hypothalamic amenorrhea: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38456861**: *Current evaluation of amenorrhea: a committee opinion* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38818646**: *Menstrual pattern in polycystic ovary syndrome and hypothalamic-pituitary-ovarian axis immaturity in adolescents: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41474492**: *2025 Update to the Female Athlete Triad Coalition Consensus Statement Part 2: Clinical Guidelines for Screening, Diagnosis, Treatment, and Return to Play for Adolescents and Adults* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 41474493**: *2025 Update to the Female Athlete Triad Coalition Consensus Statement Part 1: State of the Science and Introduction of a New Adolescent Model* (2026) [guideline]

## [0624] Endocrine emergencies overview: DKA, HHS, adrenal crisis, thyroid storm - recognition and triage

**Cluster**: endocrine_emergencies | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Endocrine emergencies include hyperglycaemic crises, adrenal crisis, thyroid storm and pituitary apoplexy. Each needs immediate recognition, and recent consensus documents standardise their management.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2024 ADA-led consensus report on hyperglycaemic crises in adults gives new recommendations informed by a systematic review of publications since 2009. (PMID:39052901)
- ISPAD guidance supports subcutaneous insulin for uncomplicated mild to moderate DKA in young people when IV insulin is not an option. (PMID:32935435)
- Mortality in primary adrenal insufficiency is increased about 2.5-fold, and in congenital adrenal hyperplasia the main cause of death is adrenal crisis. (PMID:40966723)
- A 2026 joint consensus statement standardises the management of thyroid storm, including treatment of the underlying hyperthyroidism after stabilisation. (PMID:42554462)
- In pituitary apoplexy, patients without severe visual or neurological deficits can be managed expectantly, with management individualised to presenting severity. (PMID:39503840)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Thyroid storm and adrenal crisis protocols are not detailed in the retrieved abstracts.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Adrenal crisis is the commonest cause of death in adrenal insufficiency.
- When pituitary apoplexy needs surgery versus conservative care.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with Addison disease and gastroenteritis, hypotensive with hyponatraemia.

**Reviewer Gap**: Doses and sequence for hydrocortisone in adrenal crisis, and the thyroid storm regimen (thionamide, iodine, beta-blocker, steroid) are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 32935435**: *ISPAD Clinical Practice Consensus Guideline: Diabetic ketoacidosis in the time of COVID-19 and resource-limited settings-role of subcutaneous insulin* (2020) [guideline]
- **PMID 39052901**: *Hyperglycemic Crises in Adults With Diabetes: A Consensus Report* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39503840**: *The role of intervention timing and treatment modality in visual recovery following pituitary apoplexy: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40966723**: *Increased mortality in primary adrenal insufficiency: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 42554462**: *Management of thyroid emergencies: joint consensus statement on management of thyroid storm* (2026) [guideline]

## [0625] Endocrine hypertension: systematic approach to Conns, phaeochromocytoma, Cushings

**Cluster**: endocrine_hypertension | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Primary aldosteronism is the commonest endocrine cause of hypertension. Current guidance covers screening, adrenal vein sampling for lateralisation and mineralocorticoid-receptor antagonist treatment with renin monitoring; pheochromocytoma and paraganglioma management is increasingly genetics-led.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2025 Endocrine Society primary aldosteronism guideline suggests monitoring renin in patients on MRA therapy, and escalating therapy if hypertension persists while renin remains suppressed; MRAs with fewer androgen or progesterone receptor side effects may be preferred in some situations. (PMID:40658480)
- A systematic review supporting the guideline associated primary aldosteronism with higher risk of mortality, atrial fibrillation and stroke, though with very low certainty. (PMID:40658500)
- A simplified Primary Aldosteronism Severity Classification correlates with lateralised subtype and could grade recommendations for adrenal vein sampling before surgery. (PMID:40485198)
- Australian and New Zealand expert recommendations cover preparation, performance and interpretation of adrenal vein sampling. (PMID:39360599)
- The 2025 Japan Endocrine Society pheochromocytoma and paraganglioma guideline notes genetic testing guides management and mentions 177Lu-DOTATATE and molecularly targeted drugs for advanced disease. (PMID:41083371)
- The European Reference Network guideline on familial hyperaldosteronism applies to genetically proven cases. (PMID:38571460)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal screening thresholds and AVS selection are consensus-based.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Who to screen for primary aldosteronism and why adrenal vein sampling is needed.
- Genetic testing in phaeochromocytoma.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young patient with resistant hypertension and spontaneous hypokalaemia.

**Reviewer Gap**: Aldosterone-renin ratio cut-offs, saline suppression tests, drugs affecting the ratio and preoperative alpha-blockade for pheochromocytoma are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38571460**: *Familial hyperaldosteronism: an European Reference Network on Rare Endocrine Conditions clinical practice guideline* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39360599**: *Adrenal Vein Sampling for Primary Aldosteronism: Recommendations From the Australian and New Zealand Working Group* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40485198**: *Expert Consensus on the Primary Aldosteronism Severity Classification and its strategic application in indicating adrenal venous sampling* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40658480**: *Primary Aldosteronism: An Endocrine Society Clinical Practice Guideline* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40658500**: *A Systematic Review Supporting the Endocrine Society Clinical Practice Guideline on Management of Primary Aldosteronism* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41083371**: *Japan Endocrine Society Clinical Practice Guideline for the Diagnosis and Management of Pheochromocytoma and Paraganglioma 2025* (2026) [guideline]

## [0626] Endometrial Cancer

**Cluster**: endometrial_cancer | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Endometrial cancer management has been reshaped by the 2023 FIGO staging and by molecular classification and immunotherapy. Guidelines now integrate immunotherapy into systemic treatment, with Lynch syndrome prevention addressed separately.

### Core Teaching Points
- NCCN 2025 insights summarise the new FIGO 2023 staging system and updated systemic therapy recommendations for endometrial cancer. (PMID:40763788)
- The SEOM-GEICO 2025 guideline states treatment changed recently because of advances in immunotherapy and summarises evidence for diagnosis, treatment and follow-up. (PMID:40986254)
- The Polish 2025 update notes that options for patients previously treated with an immunotherapy combination are still under evaluation. (PMID:40558283)
- ESGO issued a consensus statement on endometrial cancer prevention, risk reduction and management of women with Lynch syndrome, including reproductive issues. (PMID:42090914)
- The SGO evidence-based review provides graded recommendations for endometrial cancer. (PMID:42048941)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Treatment after immunotherapy failure.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Role of molecular subtype (POLE, MMR, p53) in adjuvant decisions.
- Lynch syndrome and endometrial cancer risk.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Postmenopausal bleeding with endometrial thickness 12 mm: biopsy and subsequent steps.

**Reviewer Gap**: The four molecular classes, surgical staging details and adjuvant therapy thresholds are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 40558283**: *Updated Guidelines for the Diagnosis and Treatment of Endometrial Carcinoma: The Polish Society of Gynecological Oncology (2025v)* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40763788**: *NCCN Guidelines® Insights: Uterine Neoplasms, Version 3.2025* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40986254**: *SEOM-GEICO clinical guidelines on endometrial cancer (2025)* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 42048941**: *Endometrial cancer: A Society of Gynecologic Oncology evidence-based review and recommendations: Part I* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42090914**: *ESGO Consensus Statement on endometrial cancer prevention, risk reduction strategies, and management of women with Lynch syndrome* (2026) [guideline]

## [0627] Endoscopic Mucosal Resection and Submucosal Dissection

**Cluster**: emr_esd | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Endoscopic mucosal resection suits lesions removable en bloc, while endoscopic submucosal dissection gives higher en bloc and R0 resection rates for larger or suspicious lesions at the cost of time and, in the colon, more complications.

### Core Teaching Points
- ESGE states ESD or surgery should be chosen for suspected superficial invasive carcinoma that cannot be removed en bloc by standard polypectomy or EMR (strong recommendation, moderate-quality evidence). (PMID:38670139)
- In colorectal lesions ESD may suit malignant lesions despite higher rates of severe postoperative complications and longer operation time. (PMID:37768914)
- In Barrett's neoplasia ESD gave higher en bloc and R0 resection rates than EMR with comparable adverse events; both are used depending on lesion type, size and expertise. (PMID:38942058)
- The AGA 2024 guideline on endoscopic eradication therapy for Barrett's oesophagus emphasises shared decision-making. (PMID:38763697)
- ESGE recommends scheduled endoscopic surveillance after curative ESD with high-definition white-light and chromoendoscopy, biopsying only suspicious areas. (PMID:35523224)
- Traction-based ESD variants (tunnelling and traction) were effective and safe compared with conventional ESD, so technique should be tailored. (PMID:40562068)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Patient-important outcome differences between EMR and ESD.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Choosing EMR versus ESD by lesion size and invasion depth.
- Complications: perforation and delayed bleeding.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 3 cm laterally spreading colonic lesion with a suspicious surface pattern.

**Reviewer Gap**: Paris and Kudo classification, size cut-offs and stricture risk in the oesophagus are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35523224**: *Endoscopic submucosal dissection for superficial gastrointestinal lesions: European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE) Guideline - Update 2022* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 37768914**: *Comparing endoscopic mucosal resection with endoscopic submucosal dissection in colorectal adenoma and tumors: Meta-analysis and system review* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38670139**: *Colorectal polypectomy and endoscopic mucosal resection: European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE) Guideline - Update 2024* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38763697**: *AGA Clinical Practice Guideline on Endoscopic Eradication Therapy of Barrett's Esophagus and Related Neoplasia* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38942058**: *Endoscopic submucosal dissection and endoscopic mucosal resection for Barrett's-associated neoplasia: a systematic review and meta-analysis of the published literature* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40562068**: *Performance of three major techniques for endoscopic submucosal dissection: a systematic review and network meta-analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis]

## [0628] Endoscopic Ultrasound

**Cluster**: eus | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Endoscopic ultrasound is used for tissue sampling, staging and therapeutic drainage; guidance advises against routine antibiotic prophylaxis for tissue sampling of solid masses and suggests cholangioscopy-guided biopsy when evaluating bile duct strictures.

### Core Teaching Points
- ESGE does not recommend antibiotic prophylaxis before EUS-guided tissue sampling of solid masses or EUS-FNA of pancreatic cystic lesions. (PMID:40015316)
- In the diagnostic work-up of bile duct strictures, ESGE suggests cholangioscopy-guided biopsies in addition to standard ERCP modalities, with additional intraductal imaging used selectively. (PMID:39689874)
- A 2025 guideline on post-ERCP pancreatitis aims to reduce incidence through evidence-based practice, citing strong evidence for anti-inflammatory drugs and one other preventive measure. (PMID:40132896)
- An i-EUS consensus addresses indications, timing and technique for managing pancreatic fluid collections. (PMID:39048418)
- An international consensus on EUS-guided biliary drainage highlights the need for standardised terminology and further trials. (PMID:42429199)
- Practice guidelines for upper GI subepithelial lesions give 11 key recommendations on diagnosis, surveillance intervals, biopsy techniques and indications for resection. (PMID:41502317)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal role of EUS-guided drainage versus ERCP.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- No routine antibiotic prophylaxis for EUS-FNA of solid lesions.
- Work-up of an indeterminate biliary stricture.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Jaundice with a distal CBD stricture and non-diagnostic brushings.

**Reviewer Gap**: Needle choice, complications and staging indications are only partly covered by the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39048418**: *The i-EUS consensus on the management of pancreatic fluid collections - Part 1* (2024) [guideline]

## [0629] Endoscopy in the Anticoagulated Patient

**Cluster**: endoscopy_anticoag | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Periprocedural management of antithrombotics for endoscopy balances thrombosis against bleeding risk. Several society guidelines exist, but evidence for the timing of anticoagulant resumption is insufficient.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ACG-CAG guideline could not recommend for or against same-day resumption versus resumption 1 to 7 days after a procedure for patients on warfarin, DOACs or P2Y12 inhibitors because of insufficient evidence. (PMID:35297395)
- The BSG/ESGE guideline bases recommendations on the balance between thrombosis and haemorrhage risk, and its update adds a section on patients presenting with acute GI haemorrhage. (PMID:34362780)
- The International Consensus Group suggests PPI therapy for patients with previous ulcer bleeding who require antiplatelet or anticoagulant therapy for cardiovascular prophylaxis. (PMID:31634917)
- A joint Asian-Pacific guideline offers guidance for patients on antithrombotics undergoing emergency or elective GI endoscopy. (PMID:29331946)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Timing of anticoagulant resumption is not settled.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Procedure bleeding risk categories drive interruption.
- PPI for secondary prophylaxis after ulcer bleeding on antithrombotics.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient on apixaban needing a polypectomy: interruption and resumption plan.

**Reviewer Gap**: High versus low risk procedure lists and drug-specific hold times are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 29331946**: *Management of patients on antithrombotic agents undergoing emergency and elective endoscopy: joint Asian Pacific Association of Gastroenterology (APAGE) and Asian Pacific Society for Digestive Endoscopy (APSDE) practice guidelines* (2018) [guideline]
- **PMID 31634917**: *Management of Nonvariceal Upper Gastrointestinal Bleeding: Guideline Recommendations From the International Consensus Group* (2019) [guideline]
- **PMID 34362780**: *Endoscopy in patients on antiplatelet or anticoagulant therapy: British Society of Gastroenterology (BSG) and European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE) guideline update* (2021) [guideline]
- **PMID 35297395**: *American College of Gastroenterology-Canadian Association of Gastroenterology Clinical Practice Guideline: Management of Anticoagulants and Antiplatelets During Acute Gastrointestinal Bleeding and the Periendoscopic Period* (2022) [guideline]

## [0630] endovascular thrombectomy time to treatment ischemic stroke meta-analysis

**Cluster**: thrombectomy | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Endovascular thrombectomy improves functional outcomes and survival for large vessel occlusion stroke, and its indications now extend to large core infarcts and late windows, though evidence in those groups is less certain.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found endovascular thrombectomy associated with improved long-term functional outcomes and reduced mortality in acute large vessel occlusion stroke. (PMID:39777553)
- The 2026 AHA/ASA guideline for early management of acute ischaemic stroke reflects significant advances including an updated approach to thrombolysis contraindications, and highlights gaps. (PMID:41582814)
- A meta-analysis of randomised trials in very large strokes with ASPECTS 0-2 challenges use of ASPECTS alone for treatment selection; patient-level meta-analysis would strengthen the evidence. (PMID:38687364)
- Evidence for thrombectomy beyond 24 hours is limited in certainty, with higher symptomatic intracranial haemorrhage risk, so randomised trials are essential. (PMID:40970285)
- In posterior circulation occlusion, prior intravenous thrombolysis was associated with better 90-day outcomes and lower mortality without more haemorrhage (class III evidence). (PMID:41252660)
- Thrombectomy in patients with concomitant intracranial haemorrhage carries poor functional outcomes and high mortality, so careful selection is needed. (PMID:39893927)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Selection for large core and ultra-late presentations.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Thrombectomy window and imaging selection for large vessel occlusion.
- Bridging thrombolysis versus direct thrombectomy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with right M1 occlusion 10 hours after last seen well.

**Reviewer Gap**: The time-to-treatment relationship (benefit per minute), NIHSS/ASPECTS thresholds and DAWN/DEFUSE-3 data are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38687364**: *Endovascular Thrombectomy for Large Ischemic Strokes with ASPECTS 0-2: a Meta-analysis of Randomized Controlled Trials* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39777553**: *Long-term outcome of endovascular thrombectomy in patients with acute ischemic stroke: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39893927**: *Endovascular thrombectomy for large vessel occlusion in acute ischemic stroke patients with concomitant intracranial hemorrhage* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40970285**: *Endovascular Thrombectomy Versus Best Medical Therapy for Large Vessel Occlusion Stroke Beyond 24 Hours: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41252660**: *Bridging or Direct Thrombectomy in Posterior Circulation Large-Vessel Occlusion Stroke: Analysis of Binational Registries and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41582814**: *2026 Guideline for the Early Management of Patients With Acute Ischemic Stroke: A Guideline From the American Heart Association/American Stroke Association* (2026) [guideline]

## [0631] Enteral Nutrition in Clinical Practice

**Cluster**: enteral_nutrition | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
In critically ill patients with a functioning gut, early enteral nutrition is preferred to early parenteral nutrition for infection control and recovery, with monitoring for GI complications.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis supports prioritising early enteral nutrition over early parenteral nutrition in critically ill patients with functional gastrointestinal systems, to improve infection control and recovery, with monitoring of GI complications. (PMID:39796444)
- The Japanese Critical Care Nutrition Guideline 2024 provides recommendations for nutritional treatment of critically ill patients and will be revised in 4 to 6 years. (PMID:40119480)
- A clinical nutrition guideline for GI diseases recommends starting nutritional support within 72 hours of admission when oral intake covers less than 60% of protein-calorie requirements, and 30-35 kcal/kg/day in cirrhosis. (PMID:40035369)
- The SCCM/ASHP guideline provides consensus recommendations for preventing stress-related upper GI bleeding in critically ill patients. (PMID:39007578)
- A Chinese guideline gives 58 recommendations on medical nutrition therapy for adults with gastrointestinal dysfunction. (PMID:39477800)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal calorie and protein targets in the acute phase are not covered in the retrieved abstracts.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Enteral over parenteral nutrition when the gut works.
- Timing of feeding in ICU patients.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Day-2 ventilated patient with ileus: feeding route decisions.

**Reviewer Gap**: Refeeding risk, contraindications, post-pyloric feeding and gastric residual volumes are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39007578**: *Society of Critical Care Medicine and American Society of Health-System Pharmacists Guideline for the Prevention of Stress-Related Gastrointestinal Bleeding in Critically Ill Adults* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39477800**: *[Guideline for medical nutritional treatment of adult patients with gastrointestinal dysfunction (2025 edition)]* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 39796444**: *Comparison of Early Enteral Nutrition Versus Early Parenteral Nutrition in Critically Ill Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40035369**: *Clinical nutrition in gastrointestinal diseases: an up-to-date clinical practice guideline* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40119480**: *The Japanese Critical Care Nutrition Guideline 2024* (2025) [guideline]

## [0632] Enteropathic Arthritis

**Cluster**: enteropathic_arthritis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Joint disease in inflammatory bowel disease includes peripheral and axial spondyloarthritis. Management is joint between gastroenterology and rheumatology, with drug choice chosen to treat both gut and joint disease.

### Core Teaching Points
- An Italian IG-IBD and SIR guideline gives 34 statements and four therapeutic algorithms for management of IBD-associated spondyloarthritis, as a reference for gastroenterologists and rheumatologists. (PMID:38521214)
- The 2022 French Society for Rheumatology recommendations cover everyday management of spondyloarthritis, including psoriatic arthritis, using the predominant clinical phenotype concept. (PMID:35038574)
- The EXTRA consensus defines endpoints to evaluate extraintestinal manifestations in IBD trials. (PMID:35051383)
- Some JAK inhibitors, particularly peficitinib, baricitinib and tofacitinib, are associated with higher herpes zoster risk in immune-mediated inflammatory diseases. (PMID:37614317)
- A BMJ rapid recommendation addressed proactive therapeutic drug monitoring of biologic drugs in IBD and inflammatory arthritis based on a systematic review of 10 trials with 2,383 participants. (PMID:39467592)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Evidence for individual agents against both gut and joint inflammation is limited in these abstracts.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Type 1 versus type 2 peripheral arthropathy of IBD (not in abstracts).
- JAK inhibitor zoster risk.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Crohn disease patient with inflammatory back pain and asymmetric knee swelling.

**Reviewer Gap**: Peripheral type 1 and 2 definitions, axial disease, and drug choices (NSAIDs worsen colitis, TNF inhibitor first line) are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35038574**: *2022 French Society for Rheumatology (SFR) recommendations on the everyday management of patients with spondyloarthritis, including psoriatic arthritis* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 35051383**: *Endpoints for extraintestinal manifestations in inflammatory bowel disease trials: the EXTRA consensus from the International Organization for the Study of Inflammatory Bowel Diseases* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 37614317**: *Risk of herpes zoster associated with JAK inhibitors in immune-mediated inflammatory diseases: a systematic review and network meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38521214**: *The management of patients with inflammatory bowel disease-associated spondyloarthritis: Italian Group for the Study of Inflammatory Bowel Disease (IG-IBD) and Italian Society of Rheumatology (SIR) recommendations based on a pseudo-Delphi consensus* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39467592**: *Proactive therapeutic drug monitoring of biologic drugs in adult patients with inflammatory bowel disease, inflammatory arthritis, or psoriasis: a clinical practice guideline* (2024) [guideline]

## [0634] Entrapment neuropathies: carpal tunnel, cubital tunnel, peroneal nerve compression

**Cluster**: entrapment_neuropathy | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Carpal tunnel syndrome is highly prevalent and is managed with splinting, injections and surgical release. Evidence compares corticosteroid injection with surgery and newer injectates such as dextrose and platelet-rich plasma.

### Core Teaching Points
- The prevalence of carpal tunnel syndrome is notably high, highlighting the need for effective surgical management strategies. (PMID:39672798)
- A Cochrane review found surgery improved nerve conduction (distal motor latency) more than local corticosteroid injection, with low-certainty evidence and uncertain effects on quality of life and adverse events. (PMID:39206746)
- An injection-therapy network meta-analysis suggested platelet-rich plasma as the leading option, with dextrose and corticosteroid as alternatives. (PMID:38753671)
- Ultrasound-guided hydrodissection with platelet-rich plasma showed greater long-term symptom improvement, with a 5 cc volume giving the greatest benefit, but protocols by severity need study. (PMID:39894044)
- The AAOS/ASSH 2025 clinical practice guideline summarises management of carpal tunnel syndrome and highlights evidence gaps. (PMID:39637428)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Network meta-analysis rankings rest on small, heterogeneous trials.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Steroid injection versus surgical release in carpal tunnel syndrome.
- Nerve conduction studies and when to operate.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Night-time paraesthesia in the thumb to ring finger of a pregnant woman.

**Reviewer Gap**: Cubital tunnel and common peroneal neuropathy (named in the topic), clinical tests and indications for surgery are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38753671**: *Injection therapy for carpal tunnel syndrome: A systematic review and network meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39206746**: *Local corticosteroid injection versus surgery for carpal tunnel syndrome* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39637428**: *American Academy of Orthopaedic Surgeons/ASSH Clinical Practice Guideline Summary Management of Carpal Tunnel Syndrome* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 39672798**: *Global and Regional Prevalence of Carpal Tunnel Syndrome: A Meta-Analysis Based on a Systematic Review* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39894044**: *Ultrasound-Guided Nerve Hydrodissection for the Management of Carpal Tunnel Syndrome: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]

## [0635] Environmental emergencies: heat stroke, severe hypothermia, drowning

**Cluster**: environmental_emergencies | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Heat stroke, severe hypothermia and drowning each have current society guidance (SCCM, ELSO, Wilderness Medical Society, ERC). The retrieved abstracts are largely administrative, so this page lists the guidance but cannot state the recommendations.

### Core Teaching Points
- The Society of Critical Care Medicine published 2025 guidelines for the treatment of heat stroke, with recommendations to be considered with the patient's clinical status and available resources. (PMID:39982186)
- ELSO published a 2025 narrative guideline on the use of ECMO for accidental hypothermia. (PMID:41128452)
- The Wilderness Medical Society 2024 update is the second update of its practice guidelines for treatment and prevention of drowning. (PMID:38379489)
- The ERC 2025 First Aid guidelines cover prevention and management of life-threatening conditions that could progress to cardiac arrest. (PMID:41117568)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Specific cooling methods, rewarming strategies and drowning resuscitation steps are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Rapid cooling by cold-water immersion in exertional heat stroke (not in abstracts).
- ECMO rewarming for hypothermic cardiac arrest.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Marathon runner collapsed with core temperature 41.5 C and confusion.

**Reviewer Gap**: The actual recommendations, thresholds and cooling or rewarming targets are not in the abstracts; the page needs the full texts (SCCM heat stroke, ELSO, WMS) before approval.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38379489**: *Wilderness Medical Society Clinical Practice Guidelines for the Treatment and Prevention of Drowning: 2024 Update* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39982186**: *Society of Critical Care Medicine Guidelines for the Treatment of Heat Stroke* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41117568**: *European Resuscitation Council Guidelines 2025 First Aid* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41128452**: *ELSO 2025 Narrative Guideline on the Use of ECMO for Accidental Hypothermia* (2025) [guideline]

## [0636] Eosinophilia Differential Diagnosis

**Cluster**: eosinophilia | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Eosinophilia is approached by separating secondary causes (infection, drugs, allergy) from primary clonal disease, with updated classification for primary eosinophilic disorders.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2024 WHO and International Consensus Classification update on eosinophilic disorders covers diagnosis and risk stratification, and notes targeted therapies such as benralizumab, depemokimab and tyrosine kinase inhibitors for MLN-eo-TK are under active investigation. (PMID:38551368)
- UK guidelines for eosinophilia in returning travellers and migrants updated the list of pathogens, expanded the fungal infection section and added guidance on off-license and rarely used drugs. (PMID:39537036)
- DRESS is a drug cause of eosinophilia, and a review provides a stepwise management tool for diagnosis, treatment and follow-up. (PMID:39325061)
- A 2024 Chinese guideline updates the 2017 consensus on diagnosis and treatment of eosinophilic disorders. (PMID:38527831)
- EGPA is covered by a separate evidence-based diagnosis and management guideline. (PMID:37161084)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Thresholds for hypereosinophilia and organ-damage criteria are not in the abstracts.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Travel history and strongyloides screening before steroids.
- Primary versus secondary eosinophilia.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Migrant with eosinophil count 3.2 and no symptoms.

**Reviewer Gap**: Definition of eosinophilia and hypereosinophilic syndrome, a stepwise work-up and the contraindication of steroids without strongyloides exclusion are not stated in the abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37161084**: *Evidence-Based Guideline for the diagnosis and management of eosinophilic granulomatosis with polyangiitis* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 38527831**: *[Guideline of the diagnosis and treatment of eosinophilic disorders (2024)]* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38551368**: *World Health Organization and International Consensus Classification of eosinophilic disorders: 2024 update on diagnosis, risk stratification, and management* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39325061**: *Demystifying drug reaction with eosinophilia and systemic symptoms (DRESS): a review of the literature and guidelines for management* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39537036**: *UK guidelines for the investigation and management of eosinophilia in returning travellers and migrants* (2025) [guideline]

## [0637] Eosinophilic esophagitis topical steroid and elimination diet

**Cluster**: eoe | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Eosinophilic oesophagitis is a chronic immune-mediated disease with inflammatory and fibrostenotic components. PPIs induce remission in about half of patients, and treatment response must be monitored histologically over time.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ACG 2025 guideline advises assessing treatment response and following patients on maintenance therapy, with attention to both the inflammatory and fibrostenotic aspects of EoE. (PMID:39745304)
- PPI therapy induced clinicopathological remission in almost half of paediatric and adult EoE patients, response was higher in Japan, and sustained remission was common on tapering doses. (PMID:40089255)
- H. pylori exposure was inversely associated with EoE. (PMID:39991954)
- The EoETALY consensus covers definition, clinical presentation and diagnosis, and current treatment and monitoring. (PMID:38423918, PMID:38521670)
- An Italian paediatric guideline covers EoE in children and adolescents. (PMID:40702503)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Relative role of diet, topical steroids and biologics is not detailed in the abstracts.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- PPI-responsive esophageal eosinophilia is part of the EoE spectrum.
- Monitoring with endoscopy and biopsy after therapy change.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young man with solid food dysphagia and a food bolus impaction.

**Reviewer Gap**: Topical steroids, empirical elimination diets and dupilumab (named in topic 637) are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38423918**: *The 1st EoETALY Consensus on the Diagnosis and Management of Eosinophilic Esophagitis - Definition, Clinical Presentation and Diagnosis* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38521670**: *The 1st EoETALY Consensus on the Diagnosis and Management of Eosinophilic Esophagitis-Current Treatment and Monitoring* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39745304**: *ACG Clinical Guideline: Diagnosis and Management of Eosinophilic Esophagitis* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 39991954**: *Meta-Analysis: Inverse Association Between Helicobacter pylori Infection and Eosinophilic Oesophagitis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40089255**: *Proton Pump Inhibitors for Inducing and Maintaining Remission in Eosinophilic Esophagitis: An Updated Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40702503**: *Eosinophilic esophagitis in children and adolescents: a clinical practice guideline* (2025) [guideline]

## [0638] Eosinophilic Granulomatosis with Polyangiitis

**Cluster**: egpa | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Eosinophilic granulomatosis with polyangiitis is treated with corticosteroids plus steroid-sparing agents for severe disease, and anti-IL-5/IL-5R biologics are increasingly integrated to reduce steroid exposure.

### Core Teaching Points
- A systematic review concluded that anti-IL-5/IL-5 receptor therapies should be integrated into EGPA treatment strategies to improve outcomes and reduce long-term steroid side effects. (PMID:40766318)
- A Chinese multidisciplinary consensus suggests corticosteroids combined with rituximab, methotrexate, azathioprine or mepolizumab to induce remission in severe EGPA, with steroids tapered to the minimum effective dose. (PMID:40300867)
- An evidence-based guideline exists for the diagnosis and management of EGPA, and the 2025 BSR recommendations address ANCA-associated vasculitis. (PMID:37161084, PMID:40499922)
- In Japan an evidence-practice gap in EGPA management was found, suggesting broader guideline dissemination is needed. (PMID:40626915)
- Eosinophilic complications during dupilumab therapy are extremely rare and mostly develop in the first months. (PMID:40305840)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Place of rituximab and cyclophosphamide versus biologics.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- EGPA features: asthma, eosinophilia and vasculitis; ANCA positivity in a minority.
- Mepolizumab as steroid-sparing therapy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Adult-onset asthma, sinusitis, eosinophilia and mononeuritis multiplex.

**Reviewer Gap**: Diagnostic criteria, the five-factor score and the dose of mepolizumab used in EGPA are not stated in the abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37161084**: *Evidence-Based Guideline for the diagnosis and management of eosinophilic granulomatosis with polyangiitis* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 40300867**: *[Multidisciplinary expert consensus on diagnosis and treatment of eosinophilic granulomatosis with polyangiitis (2025 Edition)]* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40305840**: *Eosinophilic complications during dupilumab therapy for type 2 diseases: a systematic review* (2025) [systematic review]
- **PMID 40499922**: *The 2025 British Society for Rheumatology management recommendations for ANCA-associated vasculitis* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40626915**: *Concordance between treatment guideline and daily practice in the management of eosinophilic granulomatosis with polyangiitis: a cross-sectional web-questionnaire survey* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40766318**: *Anti-IL5/IL-5 receptor therapies for eosinophilic granulomatosis with polyangiitis: an updated Systematic Review* (2025) [meta-analysis]

## [0640] epilepsy

**Cluster**: epilepsy_asm | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Antiseizure medication choice depends on seizure type, sex and pregnancy potential, and HLA risk. Folic acid before and during pregnancy is advised for people with epilepsy on ASMs, and functional seizures require different management.

### Core Teaching Points
- The AAN practice guideline advises at least 0.4 mg folic acid daily preconceptionally and during pregnancy for people with epilepsy of childbearing potential treated with an ASM, to reduce neural tube defects and possibly improve neurodevelopment. (PMID:38748979)
- The NICE epilepsy guideline ties valproate prescribing in people who could become pregnant to fulfilment of the Pregnancy Prevention Programme conditions. (PMID:35700280)
- For patients with functional seizures and no other indication, the AAN guideline advises tapering off antiseizure medications. (PMID:41370742)
- A UK guideline covers HLA genotype testing for carbamazepine, oxcarbazepine and eslicarbazepine. (PMID:42002301)
- A clinical practice guideline reviews the evidence and recommendations for third-generation antiseizure medications. (PMID:41270420)
- Specialised epilepsy centre guidelines cover monitoring units, surgery, neuroimaging, neuropsychology, genetics and outpatient care. (PMID:38306606)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Comparative efficacy of newer ASMs by syndrome.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Valproate in women of childbearing potential.
- HLA-B*15:02 screening before carbamazepine in at-risk ancestry.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman with generalised epilepsy planning pregnancy on valproate.

**Reviewer Gap**: First seizure work-up (EEG, MRI, driving advice) and drug selection by syndrome are not in the retrieved abstracts.

## [0641] Epilepsy antiseizure medication selection

**Cluster**: epilepsy_asm | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Antiseizure medication choice depends on seizure type, sex and pregnancy potential, and HLA risk. Folic acid before and during pregnancy is advised for people with epilepsy on ASMs, and functional seizures require different management.

### Core Teaching Points
- The AAN practice guideline advises at least 0.4 mg folic acid daily preconceptionally and during pregnancy for people with epilepsy of childbearing potential treated with an ASM, to reduce neural tube defects and possibly improve neurodevelopment. (PMID:38748979)
- The NICE epilepsy guideline ties valproate prescribing in people who could become pregnant to fulfilment of the Pregnancy Prevention Programme conditions. (PMID:35700280)
- For patients with functional seizures and no other indication, the AAN guideline advises tapering off antiseizure medications. (PMID:41370742)
- A UK guideline covers HLA genotype testing for carbamazepine, oxcarbazepine and eslicarbazepine. (PMID:42002301)
- A clinical practice guideline reviews the evidence and recommendations for third-generation antiseizure medications. (PMID:41270420)
- Specialised epilepsy centre guidelines cover monitoring units, surgery, neuroimaging, neuropsychology, genetics and outpatient care. (PMID:38306606)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Comparative efficacy of newer ASMs by syndrome.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Valproate in women of childbearing potential.
- HLA-B*15:02 screening before carbamazepine in at-risk ancestry.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman with generalised epilepsy planning pregnancy on valproate.

**Reviewer Gap**: First seizure work-up (EEG, MRI, driving advice) and drug selection by syndrome are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35700280**: *Epilepsies in children, young people and adults* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 38306606**: *Guidelines for Specialized Epilepsy Centers: Executive Summary of the Report of the National Association of Epilepsy Centers Guideline Panel* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38748979**: *Teratogenesis, Perinatal, and Neurodevelopmental Outcomes After In Utero Exposure to Antiseizure Medication: Practice Guideline From the AAN, AES, and SMFM* (2024) [guideline]

- **PMID 41370742**: *Management of Functional Seizures Practice Guideline Executive Summary: Report of the AAN Guidelines Subcommittee* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42002301**: *HLA genotype testing for carbamazepine, oxcarbazepine and eslicarbazepine: A guideline developed by the UK Centre of Excellence in Regulatory Science and Innovation in Pharmacogenomics (CERSI-PGx)* (2026) [guideline]

## [0642] Epistaxis: assessment and management

**Cluster**: epistaxis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Epistaxis is managed in a stepwise way from vasoconstrictors and packing to cautery and, for refractory cases, arterial ligation or embolisation; imaging is reserved for severe, recurrent or posterior bleeding.

### Core Teaching Points
- The AAO-HNS guideline covers management with vasoconstrictors, nasal packing and cautery, and for complex epistaxis endoscopic arterial ligation and interventional radiology. (PMID:31910111)
- Imaging in epistaxis is indicated mainly in severe, recurrent or posterior bleeding to localise the source and guide treatment. (PMID:41697295)
- In hereditary haemorrhagic telangiectasia, a network meta-analysis found timolol, tranexamic acid, tamoxifen and estriol effective for epistaxis, with adverse event concerns for tranexamic acid, tacrolimus, propranolol and oestrogen. (PMID:37995018)
- An expert consensus statement addresses sclerotherapy for HHT-related epistaxis. (PMID:40773189)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Evidence for HHT agents rests on small trials.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- First-aid for anterior epistaxis: lean forward and pinch the soft part of the nose.
- When to suspect HHT.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Anticoagulated patient with recurrent anterior epistaxis.

**Reviewer Gap**: First-aid steps, packing types and the role of tranexamic acid in non-HHT epistaxis are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 31910111**: *Clinical Practice Guideline: Nosebleed (Epistaxis)* (2020) [guideline]
- **PMID 37995018**: *Treatments of Epistaxis in Hereditary Hemorrhagic Telangiectasia: Systematic Review and Network Meta-Analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40773189**: *Consensus on Sclerotherapy for Patients With Hereditary Hemorrhagic Telangiectasia-Related Epistaxis* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41697295**: *ESR Essentials: imaging in nasal obstruction and epistaxis-practice recommendations by the European Society of Head and Neck Radiology* (2026) [guideline]

## [0643] ERCP indications and complications

**Cluster**: ercp | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
ERCP is now mainly therapeutic, and its main hazard is post-ERCP pancreatitis. Risk is predicted by patient and papilla factors, and prevention rests on pharmacological measures supported by strong evidence.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2023 clinical practice guidelines for post-ERCP pancreatitis state that anti-inflammatory drug prophylaxis and one other measure are supported by strong evidence for reducing incidence. (PMID:40132896)
- A comprehensive meta-analysis of predictors of post-ERCP pancreatitis provides pooled estimates of risk factors that could feed a prediction model to guide consent and prevention. (PMID:39694210)
- Certain papilla morphologies are associated with higher rates of difficult cannulation and post-ERCP pancreatitis. (PMID:38538734)
- Adverse events of ERCP remain common despite improved techniques, which should inform consent and use of risk-mitigation strategies. (PMID:39515394)
- Paediatric ERCP appears to be performed safely with success rates similar to adults. (PMID:37996785)
- World Endoscopy Organization guidelines cover biliary cannulation and sphincterotomy techniques, alternatives to ERCP when it fails (percutaneous and EUS-guided) and access in altered anatomy. (PMID:40518920)
- ESGE suggests cholangioscopy-guided biopsies in addition to standard ERCP modalities for the work-up of bile duct strictures. (PMID:39689874)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- The exact preventive measures are not named in the retrieved abstract tail.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Rectal NSAID prophylaxis and pancreatic stent placement to prevent post-ERCP pancreatitis (not in abstracts).
- Papilla morphology and difficult cannulation.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young woman with suspected sphincter of Oddi dysfunction booked for ERCP: pre-procedure risk assessment.

**Reviewer Gap**: Indications, antibiotic prophylaxis and rectal indomethacin dosing are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37996785**: *Indications, success, and adverse event rates of pediatric endoscopic retrograde cholangiopancreatography (ERCP): a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38538734**: *Morphology of the papilla can predict procedural safety and efficacy of ERCP-a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39515394**: *Adverse Events Associated With Endoscopic Retrograde Cholangiopancreatography: Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39689874**: *Diagnostic work-up of bile duct strictures: European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE) Guideline* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 39694210**: *Predictors of Post-endoscopic Retrograde Cholangiopancreatography Pancreatitis: A Comprehensive Systematic Review and Meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40132896**: *Clinical Practice Guidelines for post-ERCP pancreatitis 2023* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40518920**: *World Endoscopy Organization guidelines on endoscopic retrograde cholangiopancreatography biliary cannulation and sphincterotomy techniques* (2025) [guideline]

## [0644] erysipelas

**Cluster**: erysipelas | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Erysipelas and cellulitis are frequently misdiagnosed: many apparent cases are non-infectious mimics, leading to unnecessary admission and antibiotic use. Obesity is a risk factor.

### Core Teaching Points
- Systematic reviews found cellulitis is commonly misdiagnosed, most alternative diagnoses are non-infectious, and misdiagnosis drives unnecessary hospital admissions and antibiotic overuse. (PMID:36189619, PMID:37231210)
- Cellulitis is significantly associated with obesity, so maintaining a healthy BMI may be indicated. (PMID:39163102)
- A German S1 guideline provides a differential diagnosis of acute and chronic redness of the lower legs, with detailed features for seven diagnoses and a table of more than 40 others. (PMID:35758562)
- A systematic review of cellulitis guidelines examined the role of non-pharmacological management in preventing recurrence. (PMID:40530782)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal prevention of recurrence.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Cellulitis mimics: venous stasis dermatitis, lipodermatosclerosis, contact dermatitis.
- Risk factors: obesity and oedema.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Bilateral red legs in an obese patient labelled cellulitis three times.

**Reviewer Gap**: Erysipelas-specific features (sharp demarcation, streptococcal aetiology), first-line penicillin treatment and duration, and recurrence prophylaxis are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35758562**: *S1 guideline: Differential diagnosis of acute and chronic redness of the lower legs* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 36189619**: *Prevalence of misdiagnosis of cellulitis: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 37231210**: *Misdiagnosis of Uncomplicated Cellulitis: a Systematic Review and Meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39163102**: *Association of Cellulitis With Obesity: Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40530782**: *A Systematic Review of Cellulitis Guidelines: The Role of Non-Pharmacological Management in Preventing Recurrence* (2025) [guideline]

## [0645] ESBL Enterobacterales bloodstream infection therapy

**Cluster**: esbl | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
For ESBL-producing Enterobacterales bloodstream infection, carbapenems remain the reference therapy: the MERINO randomised trial found piperacillin-tazobactam failed to show non-inferiority to meropenem on 30-day mortality, although a later observational synthesis found no difference after adjustment.

### Core Teaching Points
- In the MERINO trial of E coli or K pneumoniae bloodstream infection with ceftriaxone resistance, definitive piperacillin-tazobactam did not achieve non-inferior 30-day mortality compared with meropenem, and the authors did not support its use in this setting. (PMID:30208454)
- A later meta-analysis found that after adjustment for confounders mortality and clinical outcomes did not significantly differ between piperacillin-tazobactam and carbapenems for ESBL bloodstream infections. (PMID:39173739)
- A Brazilian guideline organises recommendations for multidrug-resistant Gram-negative infections by pathogen and site, including bloodstream infection. (PMID:41130038)
- Decolonisation strategies have short-term benefit for ESBL-E and carbapenem-resistant Enterobacterales carriage but long-term effect is unclear. (PMID:39420082)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Role of piperacillin-tazobactam after clinical stabilisation, and of newer beta-lactam-inhibitor combinations.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Carbapenem versus piperacillin-tazobactam for ESBL bacteraemia (MERINO).
- Decolonisation is not routinely effective long term.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Urosepsis with ceftriaxone-resistant E coli bacteraemia.

**Reviewer Gap**: Oral step-down options, duration and carbapenem-sparing strategies are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 30208454**: *Effect of Piperacillin-Tazobactam vs Meropenem on 30-Day Mortality for Patients With E coli or Klebsiella pneumoniae Bloodstream Infection and Ceftriaxone Resistance: A Randomized Clinical Trial* (2018) [other]
- **PMID 39173739**: *Piperacillin-tazobactam vs. carbapenems for treating hospitalized patients with ESBL-producing Enterobacterales bloodstream infections: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39420082**: *Decolonization strategies for ESBL-producing or carbapenem-resistant Enterobacterales carriage: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41130038**: *Guideline for antimicrobial treatment of multidrug-resistant Gram-negative infections: practice recommendations of the Brazilian Society of Infectious Diseases* (2025) [guideline]

## [0646] esophageal cancer neoadjuvant chemoradiation

**Cluster**: oesophageal_cancer | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Preoperative chemoradiotherapy (CROSS) improved survival in resectable oesophageal and junctional cancer, and neoadjuvant immunotherapy combinations are now under study. Pathologic response should not be treated as a surrogate for survival.

### Core Teaching Points
- The CROSS trial concluded that preoperative chemoradiotherapy improved survival in potentially curable oesophageal or junctional cancer, with an R0 resection rate of 92% versus 69% for surgery alone and acceptable adverse events. (PMID:22646630)
- In locally advanced disease, neoadjuvant chemoradiotherapy versus chemotherapy increased tumour regression and survival but also postoperative mortality and grade 3 or worse adverse events; squamous carcinoma benefited more than adenocarcinoma. (PMID:38241577)
- Pathologic complete response is higher in squamous cell carcinoma than adenocarcinoma, but pathological response lacks trial-level surrogacy for overall survival. (PMID:38457244, PMID:37400616)
- Neoadjuvant immunochemoradiation enhanced pathologic response in squamous carcinoma with possibly acceptable safety, supporting further trials. (PMID:40319926)
- The German guideline stratifies palliative systemic therapy by histology and PD-L1 or HER2 expression. (PMID:38284661)
- Circulating tumour DNA after neoadjuvant therapy and oesophagectomy may stratify risk but needs standardisation. (PMID:40793951)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Immunotherapy sequencing; surrogate endpoints.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- CROSS regimen (carboplatin, paclitaxel, radiotherapy) and survival benefit.
- Histology differences in response to chemoradiation.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Fit patient with T3N1 distal oesophageal adenocarcinoma: neoadjuvant options.

**Reviewer Gap**: Definitive chemoradiotherapy versus surgery in squamous cancer, FLOT in adenocarcinoma, and radiotherapy dosing are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 22646630**: *Preoperative chemoradiotherapy for esophageal or junctional cancer* (2012) [other]
- **PMID 37400616**: *Validity of Using Pathological Response as a Surrogate for Overall Survival in Neoadjuvant Studies for Esophageal Cancer: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38241577**: *Efficacy and safety of neoadjuvant chemoradiotherapy versus neoadjuvant chemotherapy in locally advanced esophageal cancer: An updated meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38284661**: *Updated German guideline on diagnosis and treatment of squamous cell carcinoma and adenocarcinoma of the esophagus* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38457244**: *Pathologic complete response in patients with esophageal cancer receiving neoadjuvant chemotherapy or chemoradiation: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40319926**: *Neoadjuvant Immunochemoradiation Therapy Versus Chemoradiation Therapy in Esophageal Cancer: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Reconstructed Individual Patient Data* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40793951**: *Circulating tumor DNA as a biomarker for progression and survival in esophageal cancer after neoadjuvant therapy and esophagectomy: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]

## [0647] Essential Thrombocythaemia

**Cluster**: essential_thrombocythaemia | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Essential thrombocythaemia is a JAK2, CALR or MPL-driven myeloproliferative neoplasm whose management centres on thrombosis prevention and risk-adapted cytoreduction. Retrieval did not return the hydroxyurea versus anagrelide trials named in the topic.

### Core Teaching Points
- JAK2-positive patients with ET have a higher incidence of arterial and venous thromboses than JAK2-negative patients. (PMID:37350087)
- Interferon therapy gave high haematological and molecular response rates in PV and ET and was associated with a favourable prognosis. (PMID:34091876)
- The European LeukemiaNet proposed genotype-tailored recommendations for CALR-mutated essential thrombocythaemia. (PMID:34450103)
- NCCN 2026 insights on myeloproliferative neoplasms mainly update myelofibrosis, and a 2026 Chinese guideline covers adult ET. (PMID:42425157, PMID:42409729)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Place of interferon relative to hydroxyurea.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Thrombosis risk stratification by JAK2 status and age.
- Aspirin and cytoreduction in high-risk ET.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 60-year-old with platelets 1,000 x10^9/L, JAK2 V617F and a prior TIA.

**Reviewer Gap**: Hydroxyurea versus anagrelide evidence (PT-1, ANAHYDRET), IPSET-thrombosis scoring and diagnostic criteria are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 34091876**: *Clinical outcomes of interferon therapy for polycythemia vera and essential thrombocythemia: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 34450103**: *Unmet clinical needs in the management of CALR-mutated essential thrombocythaemia: a consensus-based proposal from the European LeukemiaNet* (2021) [guideline]
- **PMID 37350087**: *Incidence of Thrombosis at Different Sites During the Follow-Up Period in Essential Thrombocythemia: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 42409729**: *[Chinese guidelines on the diagnosis and treatment of adult essential thrombocythemia (2026)]* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42425157**: *NCCN Guidelines® Insights: Myeloproliferative Neoplasms, Version 2.2026* (2026) [guideline]

## [0649] Essential tremor: medical management (beta-blockers, primidone) vs MR-guided FUS

**Cluster**: essential_tremor | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Essential tremor is treated first with drugs, and refractory cases can have MR-guided focused ultrasound thalamotomy, deep brain stimulation or gamma knife radiosurgery. MRgFUS efficacy is good but may wane over time.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found strong evidence for the efficacy and safety of unilateral MRgFUS in refractory essential tremor for tremor severity and quality of life, with acceptable adverse events. (PMID:39217634)
- Another meta-analysis suggests the MRgFUS treatment effect decreases over time, which needs further investigation. (PMID:40243386)
- MRgFUS was effective in patients with a low skull density ratio, with sustained tremor reduction up to one year comparable to higher ratios. (PMID:40617709)
- Gamma knife radiosurgery appears an effective and safe option for essential tremor. (PMID:39562373)
- Short pulse width DBS induced more tremor reduction in essential tremor than conventional pulse width. (PMID:38160999)
- Available evidence does not support a causal association between beta-adrenoreceptor modulators and Parkinson disease; significant associations are likely explained by protopathic bias and confounding. (PMID:40200766)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Durability of ablative treatments.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Refractory ET: MRgFUS thalamotomy versus DBS.
- Propranolol is not a cause of Parkinson disease.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with disabling hand tremor despite propranolol and primidone.

**Reviewer Gap**: Drug therapy (propranolol, primidone, topiramate) efficacy and adverse effects are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38160999**: *Deep brain stimulation with short versus conventional pulse width in Parkinson's disease and essential tremor: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39217634**: *Magnetic resonance-guided focused ultrasound in the treatment of refractory essential tremor: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39562373**: *Efficacy and safety of stereotactic radiosurgery with gamma knife machine in patients with essential tremor: a systematic review and single-arm meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40200766**: *Association Between β-Adrenoreceptor Agonists and Antagonists and Parkinson's Disease: Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40243386**: *Efficacy and Safety of Magnetic Resonance-Guided Focused Ultrasound Thalamotomy in Essential Tremor: A Systematic Review and Metanalysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40617709**: *Magnetic resonance-guided focused ultrasound for essential tremor patients with low skull density ratio: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis]
