## [0651] Ethics in the ICU: goals of care, withholding and withdrawing treatment

**Cluster**: icu_ethics | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
End-of-life care in the ICU should use shared decision-making, early palliative care and structured family communication. The 2024 ESICM guideline is the principal evidence-based source, supported by regional consensus on withdrawal, sedation and bereavement.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2024 ESICM guideline recommends shared decision-making strategies, suggests early integration of palliative care with standardised symptom assessment for patients at high risk of dying, and advocates communication training for staff and printed communication aids for families. (PMID:39361081)
- The same ESICM guideline highlights structured family conferences, culturally sensitive interventions, conflict-management protocols and strategies to prevent burnout among professionals. (PMID:39361081)
- A Scandinavian Delphi consensus defined domains including extubation and termination of mechanical ventilation, continuous sedation at the end of life, indicators for specialist palliative consultation, bereavement care and debriefing. (PMID:40066825)
- A meta-synthesis found that culture, religion, family and clinicians' perceptions influence continuation of futile life-sustaining treatment and that nurses' roles in ensuring comfort matter. (PMID:38050047)
- A German recommendation states end-of-life treatment focuses on alleviating symptoms, the team should anticipate and plan palliative measures, and a family-centred approach needs psychosocial and spiritual support. (PMID:40879752)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best approach to prognostication and futility conflicts between teams and families.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Withdrawal of life-sustaining treatment is an ethically equivalent decision to withholding.
- Family conferences improve satisfaction.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Day 14 ICU patient with multi-organ failure and no prospect of recovery; family disagrees about withdrawal.

**Reviewer Gap**: Legal frameworks (best interests, capacity), formal prognostic tools and NICE or GMC guidance are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38050047**: *Futile life-sustaining treatment in the intensive care unit - nurse and physician experiences: meta-synthesis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39361081**: *European Society of Intensive Care Medicine guidelines on end of life and palliative care in the intensive care unit* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 40066825**: *Consensus statements on end-of-life care in ICU - A Scandinavian multidisciplinary Delphi study* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40879752**: *[Treatment and support for adults at the end of life in intensive care. A recommendation by the Ethics Section of DIVI and the German Society for Palliative Medicine. : Part 2: Therapeutic measures and support at the end of life in intensive care]* (2025) [guideline]

## [0652] EULAR recommendations 2022 for RA management: updated treat-to-target algorithm

**Cluster**: eular_ra | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
The 2022 EULAR update keeps treat-to-target management of rheumatoid arthritis with methotrexate-based therapy and escalation to biologic or targeted synthetic DMARDs, with glucocorticoids used short term. JAK inhibitor safety (malignancy, MACE, VTE) shapes drug choice.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2022 EULAR update was informed by systematic reviews on efficacy and safety of synthetic and biological DMARDs and on glucocorticoids. (PMID:36368906, PMID:36376026, PMID:36410794)
- Safety reviews confirmed the profile of biologic DMARDs and raised the question whether the difference in malignancy, MACE and VTE between tofacitinib and TNF inhibitors applies to other JAK inhibitors. (PMID:36376026)
- In trials most patients could stop glucocorticoids within 12 to 24 months, and well-known glucocorticoid risks were confirmed. (PMID:36410794)
- Treatment guidance advises a treat-to-target strategy aiming for low disease activity or remission, with frequent monitoring and escalation if methotrexate alone is inadequate. (PMID:35953230)
- In difficult-to-treat RA, ultrasound may add value in assessing inflammatory activity, including in patients with obesity or fibromyalgia. (PMID:33514671)
- Dietary exposures show at most small benefits in rheumatic and musculoskeletal disease. (PMID:35654458)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- JAK inhibitor class safety beyond tofacitinib.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Treat-to-target principle and frequent monitoring.
- Safety cautions for JAK inhibitors in patients with cardiovascular risk.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with RA not at target after 3 months of methotrexate at optimum dose.

**Reviewer Gap**: The detailed 2022 recommendation wording (phases I-III, tapering rules) is not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 33514671**: *Diagnostic issues in difficult-to-treat rheumatoid arthritis: a systematic literature review informing the EULAR recommendations for the management of difficult-to-treat rheumatoid arthritis* (2021) [guideline]
- **PMID 35654458**: *Effects of diet on the outcomes of rheumatic and musculoskeletal diseases (RMDs): systematic review and meta-analyses informing the 2021 EULAR recommendations for lifestyle improvements in people with RMDs* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 35953230**: *Treatment Guidelines in Rheumatoid Arthritis* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 36368906**: *Efficacy of synthetic and biological DMARDs: a systematic literature review informing the 2022 update of the EULAR recommendations for the management of rheumatoid arthritis* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 36376026**: *Safety of synthetic and biological DMARDs: a systematic literature review informing the 2022 update of the EULAR recommendations for the management of rheumatoid arthritis* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 36410794**: *Efficacy, duration of use and safety of glucocorticoids: a systematic literature review informing the 2022 update of the EULAR recommendations for the management of rheumatoid arthritis* (2023) [guideline]

## [0653] evidence for tight glycemic control in the ICU

**Cluster**: tight_glycaemic_control | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Tight glucose control in the ICU does not improve outcomes and increases severe hypoglycaemia. The 2024 SCCM guideline recommends against intensive targets, and moderate targets with safe insulin protocols are preferred.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2024 SCCM guideline recommends avoiding intensive glucose targets and minimising hypoglycaemia in critically ill children and adults. (PMID:38240484)
- A patient-level meta-analysis of intensive glucose control in critically ill adults found no subgroup in which intensive control was beneficial, with excess severe hypoglycaemia. (PMID:38864749)
- In septic ICU patients, intensive and liberal glucose control had comparable effects on outcomes, but intensive control carried a higher risk of severe hypoglycaemia. (PMID:38796958)
- An updated 2026 comparison suggests a range of 110-140 mg/dL (6.1-7.8 mmol/L) may be acceptable in select centres where hypoglycaemia risk is negligible and optimised protocols are used. (PMID:42262517)
- Perioperative intensive glucose control in diabetic surgical patients reduced some complications but increased hypoglycaemia. (PMID:34509960)
- In non-ICU hospitalised patients with diabetes, adding continuous glucose monitoring to point-of-care testing improved glycaemic control and reduced hypoglycaemia. (PMID:39798897)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal target within the moderate range.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- NICE-SUGAR lesson: intensive control increases hypoglycaemia and mortality risk.
- Typical ICU target around 140-180 mg/dL (not in abstracts).

### Case Generation Hooks
- Septic ICU patient with glucose 11 mmol/L: target and insulin infusion protocol.

**Reviewer Gap**: The explicit upper threshold to start insulin and the 140-180 mg/dL target are not in the retrieved abstracts; NICE-SUGAR and Leuven trials were not retrieved.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 34509960**: *Intensive glucose control during the perioperative period for diabetic patients undergoing surgery: An updated systematic review and meta-analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38240484**: *Society of Critical Care Medicine Guidelines on Glycemic Control for Critically Ill Children and Adults 2024* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38796958**: *Intensive or liberal glucose control in intensive care units for septic patients? A meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38864749**: *A Patient-Level Meta-Analysis of Intensive Glucose Control in Critically Ill Adults* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39798897**: *Use of continuous glucose monitoring and point-of-care glucose testing in hospitalized patients with diabetes mellitus in non-intensive care unit settings: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 42262517**: *Comparison of Intensive Versus Conventional Glycemic Control Targets: An Updated Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of the 2024 Society of Critical Care Medicine Guidelines on Glycemic Control for Critically Ill Adults* (2026) [guideline]

## [0654] evidence for treatments in achalasia

**Cluster**: achalasia | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Achalasia treatment options are pneumatic dilation, laparoscopic Heller myotomy with fundoplication and peroral endoscopic myotomy. POEM is preferred for type III, with either POEM or Heller reasonable for types I and II, and GERD is the main downside of POEM.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2024 SAGES update conditionally recommends either POEM or Heller myotomy with fundoplication for achalasia subtypes I and II, and prefers POEM for subtype III. (PMID:40399617)
- A meta-analysis found POEM and Heller myotomy both effective and safe, with POEM better for clinical success, operative time, hospital stay and postoperative pain and a trend to lower recurrence. (PMID:38775883)
- Long-term outcomes show POEM has efficacy equivalent to Heller myotomy but possibly a higher risk of postoperative GERD, and randomised trials with longer follow-up are needed. (PMID:40624423, PMID:36260203)
- AGA advice supports using functional lumen imaging probe during myotomy to guide adequacy of lower oesophageal sphincter disruption. (PMID:40679455)
- The ASGE guideline compares the four achalasia therapies on effectiveness, adverse effects and cost. (PMID:31839408)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Need for routine post-POEM contrast study is unclear.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Choose POEM for spastic (type III) achalasia.
- Post-POEM reflux and PPI surveillance.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young adult with dysphagia to solids and liquids, 'bird beak' on barium swallow.

**Reviewer Gap**: Chicago classification, manometric diagnosis, pseudoachalasia and botulinum toxin are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 31839408**: *ASGE guideline on the management of achalasia* (2020) [guideline]
- **PMID 36260203**: *Mid-Term and Long-Term Outcomes of Peroral Endoscopic Myotomy for the Treatment of Achalasia: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38775883**: *Achalasia: laparoscopic Heller myotomy with fundoplication versus peroral endoscopic myotomy-a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40399617**: *2024 Update to SAGES guidelines for the use of peroral endoscopic myotomy (POEM) in the treatment of achalasia* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40624423**: *Long-term outcomes after per-oral endoscopic myotomy versus laparoscopic Heller myotomy in the treatment of achalasia: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40679455**: *AGA Clinical Practice Update on Incorporating Functional Lumen Imaging Probe Into Esophageal Clinical Practice: Expert Review* (2025) [guideline]

## [0655] evidence on postmenopausal hormone therapy and cognition

**Cluster**: hrt_cognition | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Menopausal hormone therapy is not indicated for preventing cognitive decline or dementia. Observational and trial meta-analyses are inconsistent, with possible time-dependent effects according to timing and formulation.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis of menopausal hormone therapy and dementia found non-significant increases in risk for estrogen-progestin (RR 1.32, 0.98-1.79) and estrogen-only therapy (RR 1.07, 1.00-1.14), supporting renewed research on midlife estrogen for risk reduction. (PMID:37937120)
- A meta-analysis of cognition found time-dependent effects of hormone therapy, varying by formulation and timing of initiation. (PMID:38501109)
- A time-response analysis found the association between hormone therapy and Alzheimer disease shifts after five years, adding weight to the critical window hypothesis. (PMID:32057896)
- The USPSTF recommends against combined estrogen-progestin and against estrogen alone (after hysterectomy) for primary prevention of chronic conditions in postmenopausal women (grade D). (PMID:29234814)
- A critical appraisal of menopause guidelines found dementia and cardiometabolic disease prevention were not treatment indications for hormone therapy. (PMID:38336466)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Whether early initiation near menopause alters dementia risk.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Hormone therapy is for vasomotor symptoms, not for dementia prevention.
- The timing hypothesis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 58-year-old asks whether hormone therapy will protect her memory.

**Reviewer Gap**: WHI-MS trial results and formulation-specific data are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 29234814**: *Hormone Therapy for the Primary Prevention of Chronic Conditions in Postmenopausal Women: US Preventive Services Task Force Recommendation Statement* (2017) [guideline]
- **PMID 32057896**: *Postmenopausal hormone therapy and Alzheimer's disease, dementia, and Parkinson's disease: A systematic review and time-response meta-analysis* (2020) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 37937120**: *Systematic review and meta-analysis of the effects of menopause hormone therapy on risk of Alzheimer's disease and dementia* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38336466**: *A systematic review and critical appraisal of menopause guidelines* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38501109**: *Systematic review and meta-analysis of the effects of menopause hormone therapy on cognition* (2024) [meta-analysis]

## [0656] evidence on salt reduction and cardiovascular outcomes

**Cluster**: salt_reduction | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Reducing salt intake lowers blood pressure and cardiovascular risk. Behavioural programmes, salt substitution and multicomponent interventions work, and reduction gives dose-dependent blood pressure effects even in treated hypertension.

### Core Teaching Points
- An umbrella review concluded that high salt intake increases cardiovascular risk while moderate reduction provides protective benefit without adverse lipid effects. (PMID:41243115)
- Pooled evidence suggests a dose-response relationship between sodium reduction and blood pressure in treated hypertensives, influenced by the class of antihypertensive. (PMID:39236753)
- Salt substitution and nutrition education are effective strategies to lower blood pressure, and multicomponent approaches may be more effective. (PMID:36477548)
- Behavioural salt-reduction interventions lowered salt intake and blood pressure, but work best combined with other salt-reduction strategies. (PMID:38143483)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Hard cardiovascular outcome trials of salt reduction are limited.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Non-pharmacologic blood pressure lowering: sodium restriction, DASH diet, weight loss.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Newly diagnosed hypertensive adult with high sodium diet.

**Reviewer Gap**: Recommended intake thresholds (WHO under 5 g/day) are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 36477548**: *Effect of salt reduction interventions in lowering blood pressure: A comprehensive systematic review and meta-analysis of controlled clinical trials* (2022) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38143483**: *Effects of Behavioral Interventions for Salt Reduction on Blood Pressure and Urinary Sodium Excretion: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Randomized Controlled Trials* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39236753**: *Blood Pressure-Lowering Medications, Sodium Reduction, and Blood Pressure* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41243115**: *Dietary salt intake and cardiovascular outcomes: an umbrella review of meta-analyses and dose-response evidence* (2025) [meta-analysis]

## [0657] evidence summary of interventions for long COVID

**Cluster**: long_covid | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Long COVID has no proven disease-modifying drug. Management is supportive and rehabilitative, with exercise showing benefit for fatigue, breathlessness and low mood, and consensus guidance under active development.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found exercise improved long COVID symptoms including fatigue, dyspnoea and depression, with benefit from supervised programmes. (PMID:39218998)
- The ORCHESTRA Delphi consensus offers a tailored approach to diagnosis and management of post-COVID-19 condition in vulnerable populations, while noting that diagnosis, management and prevention are still under discussion. (PMID:40252805)
- Korea and regional expert groups have issued clinical practice guidance on assessment, management and rehabilitation of post-acute sequelae of COVID-19. (PMID:41486438, PMID:40842528)
- Core outcome sets exist for children and young people, and EURONET-SOMA proposes core psychosocial outcome domains in adults to compare interventions. (PMID:38359962, PMID:39934846)
- Symptom clustering analysis identifies subtypes and trajectories of post-COVID condition that may help early warning and targeted intervention. (PMID:40680300)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- No effective pharmacological treatment is established in the retrieved sources.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Graded exercise caution in post-exertional symptom exacerbation (not in abstracts).
- Long COVID is a clinical diagnosis after exclusion of alternatives.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with fatigue and cognitive fog three months after COVID-19.

**Reviewer Gap**: Evidence on specific drugs (for example antivirals, metformin) is not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38359962**: *Core outcome measurement set for research and clinical practice in post-COVID-19 condition (long COVID) in children and young people: an international Delphi consensus study "PC-COS Children"* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39218998**: *The effectiveness of exercise in alleviating long COVID symptoms: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39934846**: *Assessment of psychosocial aspects in adults in post-COVID-19 condition: the EURONET-SOMA recommendations on core outcome domains for clinical and research use* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40252805**: *ORCHESTRA Delphi consensus: diagnostic and therapeutic management of post-COVID-19 condition in vulnerable populations* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40680300**: *Symptom Trajectories and Clinical Subtypes in Post-COVID-19 Condition: Systematic Review and Clustering Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40842528**: *Consensus based recommendations for the management of post-COVID long-term sequelae (Long COVID): a regional perspective* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41486438**: *Clinical Practice Guideline Recommendations for Post-Acute Sequelae of COVID-19* (2025) [guideline]

## [0658] Ewing Sarcoma

**Cluster**: ewing | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Ewing sarcoma requires upfront multidisciplinary planning in specialised centres, with multimodal chemotherapy and local control. Relapsed disease follows consensus recommendations on systemic and local therapies.

### Core Teaching Points
- The Spanish GEIS guideline states Ewing sarcoma treatment should be done in specialised centres with plans designed upfront by a multidisciplinary tumour board. (PMID:39158802)
- NCCN bone cancer guidelines provide a consensus framework for workup, management and surveillance of local and recurrent or metastatic disease. (PMID:40203873)
- For relapsed Ewing sarcoma, consensus recommendations support systemic therapy choices and weigh disease burden, progression-free interval, life expectancy, toxicity and quality of life for local or metastasis-directed therapy, using shared decision-making. (PMID:39182183, PMID:40251761)
- COG consensus provides biopsy guidance for Ewing sarcoma and osteosarcoma to harmonise tissue collection. (PMID:39729709)
- A systematic review of clinical presentation of bone tumours in children and young people highlights the need to improve diagnostic speed. (PMID:39909718)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Limited randomised evidence in relapse.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Plan biopsy through the sarcoma centre before any resection.
- t(11;22) EWSR1-FLI1 translocation (not in abstracts).

### Case Generation Hooks
- Teenager with progressive femoral pain, fever and raised LDH.

**Reviewer Gap**: Chemotherapy regimens (VDC/IE), radiotherapy and genetic hallmark are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39158802**: *Clinical practice guidelines for the treatment of Ewing sarcoma (Spanish Sarcoma Research Group-GEIS)* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 39182183**: *Consensus recommendations for systemic therapies in the management of relapsed Ewing sarcoma: A report from the National Ewing Sarcoma Tumor Board* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39729709**: *Optimizing Ewing Sarcoma and Osteosarcoma Biopsy Acquisition: A Children's Oncology Group Bone Tumor Committee Consensus Statement* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39909718**: *Clinical presentation of bone tumours in children and young people: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]

- **PMID 40251761**: *Consensus recommendations regarding local and metastasis-directed therapies in the management of relapsed/recurrent Ewing sarcoma* (2025) [guideline]

## [0659] Extra-articular RA: nodules, vasculitis, ILD, Felty syndrome, ocular involvement

**Cluster**: ra_extra_articular | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
The best-covered extra-articular manifestation of rheumatoid arthritis in the retrieved literature is interstitial lung disease. Guidelines address screening, biomarkers and treatment, with low-certainty evidence in many areas.

### Core Teaching Points
- ERS/EULAR 2026 guidelines give evidence-based recommendations for each connective tissue disease with ILD, noting low certainty or absent evidence in many areas. (PMID:40912974, PMID:40907995)
- A joint statement recommends screening for early ILD detection in rheumatic diseases, and an expert consensus covers screening, diagnosis and treatment of RA-ILD. (PMID:38148269, PMID:42248165)
- Italian rheumatology recommendations on RA-ILD treatment were informed by a review that highlighted safety of methotrexate and supported abatacept, rituximab and nintedanib as promising options. (PMID:40876558, PMID:41247141)
- A meta-analysis of biomarkers of RA-ILD found markers useful for diagnosis and prognosis but needing prospective validation. (PMID:39534599)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal immunosuppression in RA-ILD.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Screening high-risk RA patients for ILD.
- Methotrexate pneumonitis misconceptions.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Older male smoker with seropositive RA and new dry cough and crackles.

**Reviewer Gap**: Rheumatoid nodules, vasculitis, Felty syndrome and ocular involvement named in the topic are not covered by the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38148269**: *Early detection of interstitial lung disease in rheumatic diseases: A joint statement from the Portuguese Pulmonology Society, the Portuguese Rheumatology Society, and the Portuguese Radiology and Nuclear Medicine Society* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 39534599**: *Biomarkers of rheumatoid arthritis-associated interstitial lung disease: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40876558**: *Updated systematic literature review and meta-analysis to inform the Italian Society of Rheumatology Recommendations on the treatment of rheumatoid arthritis-associated interstitial lung disease* (2025) [guideline]

## [0660] Extubation failure: predictors, post-extubation stridor, NIV rescue

**Cluster**: extubation_failure | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Prophylactic non-invasive respiratory support after extubation (high-flow, CPAP or BiPAP) reduces extubation failure in at-risk patients, but is not effective as rescue after failure has occurred.

### Core Teaching Points
- A network meta-analysis found non-invasive respiratory support applied preventively after extubation reduced reintubation, but not when applied as rescue. (PMID:34825256)
- Compared with conventional oxygen therapy, HFNC, CPAP and BiPAP lowered extubation and treatment failure with modest increases in abdominal distension and nasal injury; CPAP had the lowest failure rates. (PMID:37273226)
- ESA/ESICM suggests NIPPV or CPAP immediately after extubation for hypoxaemic patients at risk of respiratory failure after abdominal surgery. (PMID:32157356)
- The ERS guideline provides conditional recommendations on high-flow nasal cannula in acute respiratory failure. (PMID:34649974)
- Predictors of extubation outcome are inconsistent, and robust multiparametric scores or AI algorithms need testing. (PMID:34782003)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best single device and patient selection.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Prophylactic NIV or HFNC in high-risk extubation (age over 65, cardiac or lung disease).
- Post-extubation stridor (not in abstracts).

### Case Generation Hooks
- COPD patient extubated after 5 days of ventilation, high risk for reintubation.

**Reviewer Gap**: Cuff-leak testing, steroids for post-extubation stridor and spontaneous breathing trial criteria are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 32157356**: *Noninvasive respiratory support in the hypoxaemic peri-operative/periprocedural patient: a joint ESA/ESICM guideline* (2020) [guideline]
- **PMID 34649974**: *ERS clinical practice guidelines: high-flow nasal cannula in acute respiratory failure* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 34782003**: *Prediction of extubation outcome in critically ill patients: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 34825256**: *Noninvasive respiratory support following extubation in critically ill adults: a systematic review and network meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 37273226**: *Association of Extubation Failure Rates With High-Flow Nasal Cannula, Continuous Positive Airway Pressure, and Bilevel Positive Airway Pressure vs Conventional Oxygen Therapy in Infants and Young Children: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-Analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]

## [0661] ezetimibe added to statin therapy acute coronary syndrome

**Cluster**: ezetimibe_acs | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Adding ezetimibe to statin after an acute coronary syndrome lowered LDL cholesterol further and improved cardiovascular outcomes in IMPROVE-IT, supporting up-front combination therapy in high-risk patients; early PCSK9 inhibitor use in ACS also lowers events.

### Core Teaching Points
- IMPROVE-IT found that ezetimibe added to simvastatin after ACS gave incremental LDL-C lowering (versus 1.8 mmol/L with simvastatin alone) and improved cardiovascular outcomes; lowering LDL-C below previous targets provided additional benefit. (PMID:26039521)
- A meta-analysis supports lipid-lowering combination therapy with statin plus ezetimibe as early as possible, preferably up-front, for high- and very-high-risk patients to reach LDL-C goals and reduce cardiovascular events and mortality. (PMID:40126455)
- A meta-analysis comparing high-intensity statin with lower-intensity statin plus ezetimibe found that greater LDL-C reduction in the combination group did not translate into fewer MACE. (PMID:39135464)
- An alternative LDL-lowering strategy gave comparable 3-year efficacy to high-intensity statins in ASCVD, with lower LDL-C, less new-onset diabetes and less intolerance. (PMID:39565634)
- Early PCSK9 inhibitor initiation in ACS reduced MACE and improved lipid profiles, with promising but unproven stroke and mortality signals. (PMID:39080549)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Whether combination of moderate statin plus ezetimibe equals high-intensity statin for hard outcomes.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Next step if LDL-C remains above goal after ACS on maximal statin: add ezetimibe, then PCSK9 inhibitor.
- IMPROVE-IT: lower is better.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Post-NSTEMI patient with LDL-C 2.1 mmol/L four weeks after starting atorvastatin 80 mg.

**Reviewer Gap**: Absolute event reduction in IMPROVE-IT, and the exact LDL-C targets in 2025 ESC/EAS guidance, are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 26039521**: *Ezetimibe Added to Statin Therapy after Acute Coronary Syndromes* (2015) [other]
- **PMID 39080549**: *Early administration of proprotein convertase subtilisin/kexin type 9 (PCSK9) inhibitors in patients with acute coronary syndrome: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39135464**: *Safety and Effectiveness of High-Intensity Statins Versus Low/Moderate-Intensity Statins Plus Ezetimibe in Patients With Atherosclerotic Cardiovascular Disease for Reaching LDL-C Goals: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39565634**: *Alternative LDL Cholesterol-Lowering Strategy vs High-Intensity Statins in Atherosclerotic Cardiovascular Disease: A Systematic Review and Individual Patient Data Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40126455**: *Impact of Lipid-Lowering Combination Therapy With Statins and Ezetimibe vs Statin Monotherapy on the Reduction of Cardiovascular Outcomes: A Meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]

## [0662] Fabry Disease

**Cluster**: fabry | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Fabry disease is an X-linked lysosomal storage disease with renal, cardiac and neurological involvement. Cardiac MRI predicts outcome, kidney disease cohorts should be screened, and female carriers need follow-up.

### Core Teaching Points
- Late gadolinium enhancement showed the strongest association with adverse prognosis among imaging predictors in Fabry cardiomyopathy. (PMID:39840688)
- Fabry disease is more prevalent in CKD than recognised; an expert consensus recommends screening, diagnosis and a multidisciplinary approach in chronic kidney disease. (PMID:39358116, PMID:37918171)
- Disease involvement in females is lower but this should not lead to inadequate follow-up, and variants of uncertain significance need clarification. (PMID:37918171)
- GLA variant classification materially changes the estimated prevalence of Fabry disease, so variants need periodic reassessment. (PMID:38047356)
- A 2026 evidence-based clinical practice guideline addresses Fabry nephropathy through 11 key questions, and Italian consensus statements address neurological management and outcome measures. (PMID:42263771, PMID:42032696)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Effect of enzyme replacement on late gadolinium enhancement.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Fabry as a cause of unexplained left ventricular hypertrophy and proteinuria in young adults.
- Screening of at-risk kidney patients.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young man with acroparaesthesia, angiokeratomas, proteinuria and LVH.

**Reviewer Gap**: Enzyme replacement versus migalastat, alpha-Gal A level and genetic confirmation are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37918171**: *Prevalence of Fabry disease in patients with chronic kidney disease: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38047356**: *Impact of <i>GLA</i> Variant Classification on the Estimated Prevalence of Fabry Disease: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Screening Studies* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39358116**: *Insights of Fabry disease: Expert consensus approach for screening, diagnosis, and multidisciplinary management in chronic kidney disease* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 39840688**: *Imaging predictors of adverse prognosis in Fabry disease cardiomyopathy: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 42032696**: *Neurological management and outcome measures in Fabry disease: consensus statements from the Italian Fabry disease neurological working group* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42263771**: *Executive summary of evidence-based clinical practice guideline for Fabry nephropathy* (2026) [guideline]

## [0663] Factor XI Deficiency and Rare Factor Deficiencies

**Cluster**: rare_factor_deficiency | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Rare bleeding disorders such as factor XI and factor VII deficiency have poor correlation between laboratory levels and bleeding, so management is individualised. Evidence retrieved is limited to pregnancy, reproductive issues and acquired haemophilia.

### Core Teaching Points
- In pregnant women with factor XI deficiency, available laboratory assays are not useful to distinguish bleeding-prone patients from those who are not, and alternative testing strategies are needed. (PMID:32437625)
- Bleeding in women with congenital factor VII deficiency is heterogeneous because treatment guidelines are lacking, and harmonising severity classification may help. (PMID:36073900)
- DOACs are attractive for atrial fibrillation in factor VII deficiency because they do not rely on tests affected by the defect and act independently of FVII. (PMID:31069850)
- Italian consensus and a global expert consensus provide guidance on diagnosis, management and evaluation of bleeds in acquired haemophilia A. (PMID:35175184, PMID:31517435)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Predicting bleeding in FXI deficiency.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Poor correlation between FXI level and bleeding tendency.
- Prolonged aPTT with normal PT.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Pregnant woman with FXI 25% and previous post-dental bleeding.

**Reviewer Gap**: Replacement options (FFP, FXI concentrate, tranexamic acid, rFVIIa), inheritance patterns and perioperative management are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 31069850**: *Direct oral anticoagulants for atrial fibrillation in patients with congenital factor VII deficiency* (2019) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 31517435**: *Clinical evaluation of bleeds and response to haemostatic treatment in patients with acquired haemophilia: A global expert consensus statement* (2019) [guideline]
- **PMID 32437625**: *The clinical management of factor XI deficiency in pregnant women* (2020) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 35175184**: *Acquired haemophilia A: Italian Consensus Recommendations on diagnosis, general management and treatment of bleeding* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 36073900**: *Reproductive health and hemostatic issues in women and girls with congenital factor VII deficiency: A systematic review* (2022) [systematic review]

## [0664] Faecal Incontinence

**Cluster**: faecal_incontinence | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Faecal incontinence affects about 1 in 12 adults, more often women and older people. The 2022 joint European guideline gives 45 recommendations on classification, diagnosis and management in adults.

### Core Teaching Points
- The UEG/ESCP/ESNM/ESPCG guideline contains 45 recommendations on classification, diagnosis and management of faecal incontinence in adults. (PMID:35303758)
- About 1 in 12 adults worldwide has faecal incontinence, with higher prevalence in women (9.1%) than men (7.4%) and in older people. (PMID:37734583)
- ACOG notes faecal incontinence affects 7-15% of community-dwelling women, that pregnancy, childbirth, obstetric anal sphincter injury and pelvic floor dysfunction are risk factors, and gives guidance on screening, evaluation and management. (PMID:30913197)
- Many nerve stimulation trials for faecal incontinence lack sham controls, so high placebo response rates limit interpretation of reported success. (PMID:31889364)
- An international Delphi consensus has developed a core outcome set for faecal incontinence research. (PMID:40516560)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Comparative effectiveness of sacral neuromodulation and other procedures.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Obstetric anal sphincter injury as a cause.
- First-line management: conservative measures, stool consistency and pelvic floor training.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman 10 years after a third-degree tear with urge incontinence of stool.

**Reviewer Gap**: Specific treatment steps (loperamide, biofeedback, sacral neuromodulation, sphincteroplasty) are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 30913197**: *ACOG Practice Bulletin No. 210: Fecal Incontinence* (2019) [guideline]
- **PMID 31889364**: *Placebo Response Rates in Electrical Nerve Stimulation Trials for Fecal Incontinence and Constipation: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2020) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 35303758**: *Guideline for the diagnosis and treatment of Faecal Incontinence-A UEG/ESCP/ESNM/ESPCG collaboration* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 37734583**: *Global Prevalence of Fecal Incontinence in Community-Dwelling Adults: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40516560**: *Faecal incontinence core outcome set: an international Delphi consensus exercise among patients, health-care professionals, and researchers* (2025) [guideline]

## [0665] Faecal Microbiota Transplant

**Cluster**: fmt_cdiff | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Faecal microbiota transplantation is more effective than standard antibiotics for recurrent C difficile infection and appears similarly safe and effective in mildly to moderately immunocompromised patients.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found FMT more effective than standard antibiotic therapy for resolution and reducing recurrence in recurrent C difficile infection. (PMID:41081988)
- In mildly to moderately immunocompromised patients, FMT for recurrent CDI had safety and effectiveness comparable to immunocompetent cohorts, with a serious adverse event rate of 10.1%. (PMID:40759389)
- The AGA 2026 update states clinicians should not perform routine test of cure after treatment of C difficile infection, and that psyllium fibre can bulk stools after recovery. (PMID:42383946)
- The AGA update for CDI in IBD addresses patients with severe colitis or systemic toxicity such as more than six bowel movements daily, severe pain, leukocytosis or haemodynamic instability. (PMID:42138670)
- The Danish national guideline covers treatment of CDI and FMT organisation, including donor recruitment and screening, laboratory preparation and follow-up. (PMID:34261379)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Regulatory framework and long-term safety of FMT.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- FMT after two or more recurrences.
- No test of cure after successful treatment.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Third recurrence of C difficile after vancomycin taper.

**Reviewer Gap**: First-line agents (vancomycin, fidaxomicin), bezlotoxumab and the route of FMT delivery are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 34261379**: *Danish national guideline for the treatment of <i>Clostridioides difficile</i> infection and use of faecal microbiota transplantation (FMT)* (2021) [guideline]
- **PMID 40759389**: *Effectiveness and Safety of Fecal Microbiota Transplantation for Recurrent Clostridioides difficile Infection in Immunocompromised Patients* (2026) [systematic review]
- **PMID 41081988**: *Safety and efficacy of fecal microbiota transplantation versus antibiotics for treating clostridioides difficile infection: systematic review and meta-analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 42138670**: *AGA Clinical Practice Update on Management of Clostridioides difficile Infection in Inflammatory Bowel Disease: Expert Review* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42383946**: *AGA Clinical Practice Update on Management of Clostridioides difficile Infection in Adults: Expert Review* (2026) [guideline]

## [0666] failure to thrive

**Cluster**: failure_to_thrive | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Faltering weight in children is assessed by growth trajectory rather than a single measurement, and the 2026 clinical practice guideline aims to standardise diagnosis and reduce unnecessary testing.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2026 clinical practice guideline on faltering weight aims to reduce confusion about diagnostic criteria, improve diagnostic accuracy, decrease overuse of laboratory tests and imaging, and enhance health service use. (PMID:41833317)
- Oral nutritional supplements are supported for children with or at risk of faltering growth, with an association with fewer infections, though dosage needs study. (PMID:37406354)
- The word 'faltering' is preferred to 'failure' because it implies a temporary problem and is less pejorative. (PMID:42049591)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best supplement dose and duration.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Diagnose faltering growth from falling centile crossing, not one weight.
- Limit routine investigations when growth is faltering without red flags.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Infant crossing two centiles downward between 4 and 8 months.

**Reviewer Gap**: Causes by age (inadequate intake, malabsorption, coeliac disease, cystic fibrosis) and red flags for organic disease are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37406354**: *Effect of oral nutritional supplements on outcomes in children presenting with, or at risk of, faltering growth in clinical settings: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41833317**: *Clinical Practice Guideline for Diagnosis and Management of Faltering Weight* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42049591**: *Expert recommendations for preterm neonates' growth goals: Considerations for clinicians* (2026) [guideline]

## [0667] Falls assessment and prevention in older adults

**Cluster**: falls | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Falls prevention in older adults relies on exercise, medication review and multifactorial interventions; guideline implementation is weak, and evidence for institutional measures such as low-floor beds and alarms is graded very low.

### Core Teaching Points
- A hospital and nursing-home fall prevention guideline graded the certainty of evidence as very low for low-floor beds, alarm sensor systems, medication review and staff education, calling for sound intervention studies. (PMID:35262271)
- Dance interventions may be an alternative to strength and balance training for preventing falls, without robust cost-effectiveness evidence. (PMID:38776214)
- Virtual reality interventions improved balance and postural control in older adults. (PMID:40533914)
- Implementing fall prevention guidelines in health services can change and sometimes sustain professionals' behaviour, but it is uncertain whether implementation reduces falls. (PMID:41134532)
- Fall rates in community-dwelling older Europeans remain persistently high, so implementation of existing knowledge is needed. (PMID:40479612)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Which institutional measures reduce falls.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Exercise (strength and balance) is the best-supported fall prevention intervention.
- Medication review for fall-risk-increasing drugs.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 80-year-old with a fall at home on four medications including a sedative and an antihypertensive.

**Reviewer Gap**: Multifactorial risk assessment content, vitamin D and orthostatic hypotension are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35262271**: *Fall prevention in hospitals and nursing homes: Clinical practice guideline* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 38776214**: *Effectiveness of dance interventions for falls prevention in older adults: systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40479612**: *Epidemiology of falls in community-dwelling older adults in Europe: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40533914**: *Virtual Reality Intervention for Fall Prevention in Older Adults: A Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41134532**: *Falling short on implementation of fall prevention guidelines in health services: a systematic review with meta-analysis* (2025) [guideline]

## [0668] Familial hypercholesterolaemia: LDL-C targets, statins, PCSK9 inhibitors, cascade testing

**Cluster**: familial_hypercholesterolaemia | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Familial hypercholesterolaemia is under-detected, and early detection and optimal treatment from childhood improve life expectancy. PCSK9 inhibitors add substantial LDL-C lowering for patients not controlled on standard therapy; apheresis serves homozygous disease.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2026 EAS consensus states early detection and optimal treatment in children and adolescents with FH are crucial to improving life expectancy, and discusses transition to adult care. (PMID:42179051)
- Alirocumab and evolocumab reduced LDL-C (high-certainty evidence) and apolipoprotein B in FH, with no change in treatment-emergent adverse events or neurocognitive events versus placebo. (PMID:36852818)
- PCSK9-targeting therapies are effective in heterozygous FH patients inadequately controlled on standard therapy and are generally well tolerated. (PMID:40841123)
- Expert recommendations cover indication, methods, goals and follow-up of lipoprotein apheresis in children with homozygous FH, and the role of Lp(a) and liver transplantation. (PMID:38598969)
- The 2026 National Lipid Association update provides strategies to improve identification and care of people with FH. (PMID:41741298)
- Guidelines for dyslipidaemia in childhood are discrepant, and consensus on early identification of FH is needed. (PMID:41238902)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal screening strategy in children.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Cascade screening of relatives.
- LDL apheresis for homozygous FH.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Man aged 38 with LDL-C 8.5 mmol/L, tendon xanthomas and a father who died of MI at 45.

**Reviewer Gap**: Numerical LDL-C targets, Dutch Lipid Clinic criteria and statin dose recommendations are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 36852818**: *Efficacy and Safety of Alirocumab and Evolocumab as Proprotein Convertase Subtilisin/Kexin Type 9 (PCSK9) Inhibitors in Familial Hypercholesterolemia: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38598969**: *Clinical practice recommendations on lipoprotein apheresis for children with homozygous familial hypercholesterolaemia: An expert consensus statement from ERKNet and ESPN* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 40841123**: *PCSK9 targeting therapies for familial hypercholesterolaemia: a meta-analysis of efficacy on lipid biomarkers and safety in adults and children across 23 RCTs* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41238902**: *Current guidelines on screening, diagnosis and management of dyslipidaemia in childhood: a systematic review* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 41741298**: *Update on familial hypercholesterolemia: An expert clinical consensus from the National Lipid Association* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42179051**: *Familial hypercholesterolaemia in children and adolescents: a European Atherosclerosis Society consensus statement* (2026) [guideline]

## [0669] Familial hypocalciuric hypercalcaemia: CaSR mutation, benign course, avoid parathyroidectomy

**Cluster**: fhh | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Familial hypocalciuric hypercalcaemia is a benign CaSR disorder that mimics primary hyperparathyroidism. Low or low-normal urinary calcium in an asymptomatic hypercalcaemic patient should prompt consideration of FHH, which avoids unnecessary parathyroid surgery.

### Core Teaching Points
- FHH should be considered when low or low-normal urinary calcium is found in a typically asymptomatic hypercalcaemic patient; the calcimimetic cinacalcet has been used for hypercalcaemia in certain symptomatic cases. (PMID:30449544)
- Calcimimetic and calcilytic drugs, as positive and negative allosteric CaSR modulators, are of potential benefit in FHH and autosomal dominant hypocalcaemia. (PMID:27647839)
- A European expert consensus covers practical management of parathyroid disorders in adults and in pregnancy. (PMID:34863037)
- A paediatric review presents a clinical, biochemical and genetic approach to investigating hypercalcaemia in children. (PMID:28914984)
- CASRdb is a public database of disease-associated calcium-sensing receptor variants. (PMID:39484850)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- The exact urinary calcium/creatinine clearance ratio cut-off is not stated in the abstracts.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Low calcium:creatinine clearance ratio distinguishes FHH from primary hyperparathyroidism.
- FHH needs no parathyroidectomy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Asymptomatic patient with mildly raised calcium, inappropriately normal PTH and a relative with similar results.

**Reviewer Gap**: Calcium/creatinine clearance ratio threshold (below about 0.01), inheritance and neonatal severe hyperparathyroidism are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 27647839**: *Disorders of the calcium-sensing receptor and partner proteins: insights into the molecular basis of calcium homeostasis* (2016) [review]
- **PMID 28914984**: *Hypercalcemic Disorders in Children* (2017) [review]
- **PMID 30449544**: *Familial hypocalciuric hypercalcemia and related disorders* (2018) [review]
- **PMID 34863037**: *European Expert Consensus on Practical Management of Specific Aspects of Parathyroid Disorders in Adults and in Pregnancy: Recommendations of the ESE Educational Program of Parathyroid Disorders* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 39484850**: *CASRdb: A Publicly Accessible Comprehensive Database for Disease-Associated Calcium-Sensing Receptor Variants* (2025) [systematic review]

## [0670] Familial Mediterranean Fever

**Cluster**: fmf | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Familial Mediterranean fever is treated with lifelong colchicine, which is safe in pregnancy and lactation, and anti-IL-1 agents for colchicine-resistant or intolerant patients. The 2024 EULAR/PReS recommendations are the current reference.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2024 EULAR/PReS endorsed update gives recommendations for treating and monitoring FMF, supported by evidence and expert opinion. (PMID:40234174)
- Colchicine has a good risk-benefit profile regarding fertility, pregnancy and lactation, although higher-quality studies on male fertility are desirable. (PMID:40023732)
- Anti-IL-1 agents were effective with a low adverse event profile in paediatric and adult FMF, and are options for patients who do not respond to colchicine. (PMID:37769252, PMID:40562663)
- IL-1 inhibitors remain first choice for colchicine-resistant or intolerant FMF, while tocilizumab might be considered in selected patients with AA amyloidosis resistant to colchicine and IL-1 inhibitors. (PMID:38950176)
- Carotid intima-media thickness findings point to premature atherosclerosis in FMF, so targeting inter-critical low-grade inflammation may help limit cardiovascular risk. (PMID:35933450)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Place of IL-6 blockade.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Colchicine prevents AA amyloidosis in FMF.
- Anakinra or canakinumab for colchicine resistance.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young patient of Mediterranean origin with recurrent 2-day fevers, peritonitis-like pain and elevated CRP.

**Reviewer Gap**: MEFV genotype interpretation, diagnostic criteria and colchicine dosing are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35933450**: *Intima media thickness of carotid arteries in familial Mediterranean fever: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 37769252**: *Efficacy and safety of anti-interleukin-1 treatment in familial Mediterranean fever patients: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38950176**: *Could tocilizumab be used in familial Mediterranean fever? A systematic review* (2025) [systematic review]
- **PMID 40023732**: *Safety of colchicine on fertility, pregnancy, and lactation: a systematic review and meta-analysis informing the EULAR/PReS recommendations for familial Mediterranean fever* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40234174**: *EULAR/PReS endorsed recommendations for the management of familial Mediterranean fever (FMF): 2024 update* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40562663**: *Efficacy and safety of treatments in familial Mediterranean fever and its complications: a systematic review informing the EULAR/PReS recommendations for familial Mediterranean fever* (2025) [guideline]

## [0672] Fanconi Anaemia and Dyskeratosis Congenita

**Cluster**: bone_marrow_failure | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Fanconi anaemia is a multisystem inherited bone marrow failure syndrome with cancer predisposition. Lifelong surveillance, including strict screening for oral squamous cell carcinoma, is essential. Dyskeratosis congenita evidence was not retrieved.

### Core Teaching Points
- Patients with Fanconi anaemia need strict surveillance for oral squamous cell carcinoma, even without oral potentially malignant disorders, for early detection and lower mortality. (PMID:38309198)
- Dental and craniofacial anomalies are part of the Fanconi anaemia phenotype, supporting expansion and standardisation of diagnostic criteria. (PMID:39568270)
- A systematised review of longitudinal manifestations of Fanconi anaemia identifies knowledge gaps in organ-system care. (PMID:39107201)
- A Chinese consensus (2022) standardises diagnosis and treatment of Fanconi anaemia, and French cytogenetics guidelines recommend cytogenetic diagnosis and follow-up in bone marrow failure syndromes. (PMID:36660784, PMID:38016422)
- A predisposition syndrome should be suspected at diagnosis of childhood leukaemia because it affects family exploration and genetic counselling. (PMID:38267311)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal timing of haematopoietic transplantation is not covered.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Diepoxybutane or mitomycin C chromosomal breakage test for diagnosis (not in abstracts).
- Cancer predisposition in Fanconi anaemia.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Child with short stature, thumb anomaly and progressive pancytopenia.

**Reviewer Gap**: Telomere biology disorders (dyskeratosis congenita), chromosomal breakage testing and transplant conditioning are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 36660784**: *[Chinese expert consensus on the diagnosis and treatment of Fanconi anemia (version 2022)]* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 38016422**: *Cytogenetics in the management of bone marrow failure syndromes: Guidelines from the Groupe Francophone de Cytogénétique Hématologique (GFCH)* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 38267311**: *[Suspicion of constitutional abnormality at diagnosis of childhood leukemia: Update of the leukemia committee of the French Society of Childhood Cancers]* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38309198**: *Oral cancer and oral potentially malignant disorders in patients with Fanconi anemia - A systematic review* (2024) [systematic review]
- **PMID 39107201**: *Longitudinal clinical manifestations of Fanconi anemia: A systematized review* (2024) [systematic review]
- **PMID 39568270**: *Dental and Craniofacial Anomalies in Fanconi Anemia: A Systematic Review and Additional 46 Reports* (2025) [systematic review]

## [0673] febrile neutropenia

**Cluster**: febrile_neutropenia | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Febrile neutropenia is an oncological emergency: start broad-spectrum antibiotics promptly, reassess if there is no response, and stop antibiotics in stable afebrile patients after the intended course regardless of neutrophil count.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ASCO/IDSA guideline advises that outpatients not responding to initial empirical broad-spectrum antibiotics be re-evaluated and considered for inpatient treatment. (PMID:29461916)
- A 2026 guideline for patients with haematological malignancy or after HSCT supports stopping antibiotics regardless of neutrophil count in afebrile, stable patients after completing the intended course, and gives strategies for resistant Gram-negative infections. (PMID:41314221)
- In haematological malignancy, Pseudomonas aeruginosa bacteraemia after infection is frequently resistant to guideline-recommended antibiotics, leading to inappropriate empirical therapy and increased mortality. (PMID:37367629)
- Consensus guidelines advise that routine antibacterial prophylaxis in neutropenia often brings resistance and adverse effects that outweigh reduced infection incidence, with no mortality benefit. (PMID:41521409)
- Primary G-CSF prophylaxis during breast cancer chemotherapy reduces febrile neutropenia incidence, with unclear effect on overall survival. (PMID:38900215)
- A local audit found low concordance of febrile neutropenia antibiotic prescribing with guidelines. (PMID:36714288)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal duration and de-escalation strategy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Time-to-antibiotic target in febrile neutropenia.
- Who is low risk (MASCC/CISNE) and eligible for outpatient therapy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Day 10 after chemotherapy, temperature 38.6 C, neutrophils 0.2.

**Reviewer Gap**: The time target (within 60 minutes), standard anti-pseudomonal beta-lactam choices and antifungal triggers are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 29461916**: *Outpatient Management of Fever and Neutropenia in Adults Treated for Malignancy: American Society of Clinical Oncology and Infectious Diseases Society of America Clinical Practice Guideline Update* (2018) [guideline]
- **PMID 36714288**: *Going local: Evaluating guideline adherence and appropriateness of antibiotic prescribing in patients with febrile neutropenia at an academic teaching hospital* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 37367629**: *High Rate of Inappropriate Antibiotics in Patients with Hematologic Malignancies and Pseudomonas aeruginosa Bacteremia following International Guideline Recommendations* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 38900215**: *Effectiveness and safety of primary prophylaxis with G-CSF during chemotherapy for invasive breast cancer: a systematic review and meta-analysis from Clinical Practice Guidelines for the Use of G-CSF 2022* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 41314221**: *Empirical and targeted antimicrobial therapy in patients with febrile neutropenia and haematological malignancy or after haematopoietic cell transplantation: recommendations from the 10th European Conference on Infections in Leukaemia* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 41521409**: *Consensus guidelines for antibacterial prophylaxis in patients with neutropenia* (2025) [guideline]

## [0674] Febrile Non-Haemolytic and Allergic Transfusion Reactions

**Cluster**: transfusion_reactions | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Transfusion reaction incidence varies by product and population. Febrile non-haemolytic and allergic reactions are the commonest, and reaction rates are comparable between plasma types for allergic reactions and TACO.

### Core Teaching Points
- A review by an international panel summarises the pathophysiology, treatment and management of each category of transfusion reaction, using evidence-based recommendations where available. (PMID:27083327)
- TRALI is less frequent with male-only plasma than mixed-sex plasma, while allergic reactions, TACO and anaphylaxis do not differ between plasma types. (PMID:28766723)
- Transfusion reaction incidence differs between children and adults, and guidelines specific to children are necessary. (PMID:35758531)
- The evidence for pre-storage versus post-storage leukoreduction filters remains fragile. (PMID:39054473)
- Technology use in transfusion reduces human error and associated complications. (PMID:36106812)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best leukoreduction strategy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Stop the transfusion and check the unit and patient identity for any reaction.
- Premedication does not reliably prevent FNHTR.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Fever of 38.8 C and rigors 30 minutes into a red cell unit.

**Reviewer Gap**: Definitions and the specific management of febrile non-haemolytic and allergic reactions are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 27083327**: *Transfusion reactions: prevention, diagnosis, and treatment* (2016) [review]
- **PMID 28766723**: *Comparing transfusion reaction rates for various plasma types: a systematic review and meta-analysis/regression* (2017) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 35758531**: *Comparison of transfusion reactions in children and adults: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 36106812**: *Ways To Enhance Blood Transfusion Safety: A Systematic Review* (2022) [systematic review]
- **PMID 39054473**: *Effect of post-storage filters vs. pre-storage filters for leukoreduction of blood components on clinical outcomes: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]

## [0676] Fertility Preservation in Cancer

**Cluster**: fertility_preservation | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Fertility preservation should be raised before gonadotoxic cancer treatment through prompt referral to a multidisciplinary team. ASCO 2025 advises clinicians to advocate for access and coverage.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2025 ASCO update stresses prompt access to a multidisciplinary fertility preservation team and advocacy for coverage of services. (PMID:40106739)
- Guidance from the Canadian Fertility and Andrology Society and FIGO covers medical and surgical technologies and referral for fertility preservation in cancer. (PMID:38458057, PMID:40739998, PMID:40854469)
- Ovarian cortical tissue intended for re-implantation should undergo histopathology, augmented by immunohistochemistry, PCR or xenograft according to metastatic risk, and tissue harbouring malignant cells is usually withheld. (PMID:41705155)
- The premature ovarian insufficiency guideline updated recommendations on genetic testing, estrogen regimens, combined oral contraceptive and testosterone therapy. (PMID:39652037)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Safety of tissue re-implantation in haematological cancers.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Oocyte or embryo cryopreservation before chemotherapy.
- Referral before starting treatment.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 28-year-old with breast cancer awaiting chemotherapy and asking about future fertility.

**Reviewer Gap**: GnRH agonist for ovarian protection, male sperm banking and paediatric options are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38458057**: *Fertility preservation in patients undergoing gonadotoxic treatments: a Canadian Fertility and Andrology Society clinical practice guideline* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39652037**: *Evidence-based guideline: Premature Ovarian Insufficiency* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40106739**: *Fertility Preservation in People With Cancer: ASCO Guideline Update* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40739998**: *Cancer and fertility management: FIGO best practice advice* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40854469**: *Fertility preservation for cancer: referral guidelines, treatment options, and specific considerations* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41705155**: *Clinical practice guideline for female fertility preservation* (2025) [guideline]

## [0677] Fever and neutropenia empiric antibiotic strategy

**Cluster**: febrile_neutropenia | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Febrile neutropenia is an oncological emergency: start broad-spectrum antibiotics promptly, reassess if there is no response, and stop antibiotics in stable afebrile patients after the intended course regardless of neutrophil count.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ASCO/IDSA guideline advises that outpatients not responding to initial empirical broad-spectrum antibiotics be re-evaluated and considered for inpatient treatment. (PMID:29461916)
- A 2026 guideline for patients with haematological malignancy or after HSCT supports stopping antibiotics regardless of neutrophil count in afebrile, stable patients after completing the intended course, and gives strategies for resistant Gram-negative infections. (PMID:41314221)
- In haematological malignancy, Pseudomonas aeruginosa bacteraemia after infection is frequently resistant to guideline-recommended antibiotics, leading to inappropriate empirical therapy and increased mortality. (PMID:37367629)
- Consensus guidelines advise that routine antibacterial prophylaxis in neutropenia often brings resistance and adverse effects that outweigh reduced infection incidence, with no mortality benefit. (PMID:41521409)
- Primary G-CSF prophylaxis during breast cancer chemotherapy reduces febrile neutropenia incidence, with unclear effect on overall survival. (PMID:38900215)
- A local audit found low concordance of febrile neutropenia antibiotic prescribing with guidelines. (PMID:36714288)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal duration and de-escalation strategy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Time-to-antibiotic target in febrile neutropenia.
- Who is low risk (MASCC/CISNE) and eligible for outpatient therapy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Day 10 after chemotherapy, temperature 38.6 C, neutrophils 0.2.

**Reviewer Gap**: The time target (within 60 minutes), standard anti-pseudomonal beta-lactam choices and antifungal triggers are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 29461916**: *Outpatient Management of Fever and Neutropenia in Adults Treated for Malignancy: American Society of Clinical Oncology and Infectious Diseases Society of America Clinical Practice Guideline Update* (2018) [guideline]
- **PMID 36714288**: *Going local: Evaluating guideline adherence and appropriateness of antibiotic prescribing in patients with febrile neutropenia at an academic teaching hospital* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 37367629**: *High Rate of Inappropriate Antibiotics in Patients with Hematologic Malignancies and Pseudomonas aeruginosa Bacteremia following International Guideline Recommendations* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 38900215**: *Effectiveness and safety of primary prophylaxis with G-CSF during chemotherapy for invasive breast cancer: a systematic review and meta-analysis from Clinical Practice Guidelines for the Use of G-CSF 2022* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 41314221**: *Empirical and targeted antimicrobial therapy in patients with febrile neutropenia and haematological malignancy or after haematopoietic cell transplantation: recommendations from the 10th European Conference on Infections in Leukaemia* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 41521409**: *Consensus guidelines for antibacterial prophylaxis in patients with neutropenia* (2025) [guideline]

## [0678] fever in children

**Cluster**: fever_children | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Fever in children is mainly managed symptomatically after excluding serious bacterial infection. The 2026 outpatient guideline recommends a symptom-oriented approach and parent education to avoid unnecessary antipyretics.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2026 clinical practice guideline on outpatient management of fever in children and adolescents sets a differentiated symptom-oriented approach and emphasises parent education to avoid unnecessary antipyretic drugs. (PMID:41733151)
- A meta-analysis evaluated procalcitonin and C-reactive protein for predicting invasive and serious bacterial infection in young febrile infants. (PMID:38499017)
- The 2025 IDSA guidance on group A streptococcal pharyngitis suggests a clinical scoring system to identify patients with low probability and reduce unnecessary testing. (PMID:41343363)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Thresholds for biomarker-based rule-out in infants.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Febrile infant under 3 months needs full sepsis evaluation.
- Antipyretics treat comfort rather than preventing febrile seizures.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Six-week-old infant with temperature of 38.4 C.

**Reviewer Gap**: Age-based risk stratification, NICE traffic-light system and red flags are not in the retrieved abstracts; this page is thin.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38499017**: *Diagnostic test accuracy of procalcitonin and C-reactive protein for predicting invasive and serious bacterial infections in young febrile infants: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41343363**: *2025 Clinical Practice Guideline Update by the Infectious Diseases Society of America on Group A Streptococcal Pharyngitis: Risk Assessment Using Clinical Scoring Systems in Children and Adults* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 41733151**: *Clinical Practice Guideline: The Outpatient Management of Fever in Children and Adolescents* (2026) [guideline]

## [0679] fever in returning traveller

**Cluster**: returning_traveller | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
In a febrile returning traveller malaria is the first diagnosis to exclude: it accounted for about one fifth of febrile cases in a systematic review, followed by other common tropical infections. Travel history, geography and incubation period guide the rest of the work-up.

### Core Teaching Points
- A systematic review of aetiology of fever in returning travellers and migrants found malaria accounted for one-fifth of febrile cases, supporting rapid malaria testing followed by other rapid tests for common tropical diseases. (PMID:33146395)
- The same review highlights high variability between studies and the need for multi-centre studies and clinical decision support algorithms to develop evidence-based guidelines. (PMID:33146395)
- Lassa fever, a rodent-borne viral haemorrhagic fever, has been reported in travellers returning from West Africa and non-endemic countries. (PMID:30666924)
- Dengue is a growing problem with a Dengvaxia vaccine and new tools, and the earlier dengue page covers warning signs for severe disease. (PMID:35543085)
- Risk factors for typhoid fever have been systematically reviewed to guide control in high-risk communities. (PMID:40875987)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- No consensus algorithm was retrieved.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Malaria must be excluded in any febrile returning traveller from an endemic area.
- Incubation period helps narrow the differential.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient returning from West Africa with fever and headache 10 days after arrival.

**Reviewer Gap**: Regional differential diagnosis by geography, incubation tables, isolation precautions for haemorrhagic fevers and malaria treatment are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 30666924**: *Lassa Fever in Travelers from West Africa, 1969-2016* (2019) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 33146395**: *Aetiology of fever in returning travellers and migrants: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2020) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 35543085**: *Dengue: A Growing Problem With New Interventions* (2022) [review]
- **PMID 40875987**: *Risk Factors for Typhoid Fever: Systematic Review* (2025) [systematic review]

---

## [0681] Fever management in critically ill patients

**Cluster**: fever_icu | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
New fever in an ICU patient needs a structured infectious and non-infectious evaluation. Treating fever itself with antipyretics has not been shown to change mortality or serious adverse events.

### Core Teaching Points
- The SCCM/IDSA 2023 guideline on evaluating new fever in adult ICU patients identifies research needs including noninvasive core temperature measurement, diagnostic imaging, molecular microbiology and biomarkers. (PMID:37902340)
- A systematic review found fever therapy in febrile adults does not seem to affect the risk of death or serious adverse events (very low-certainty evidence). (PMID:35820685)
- After cardiac arrest, ERC-ESICM advises temperature control to achieve normothermia in patients with mild hypothermia after ROSC, and recommends against prehospital cooling with rapid large-volume cold IV fluids. (PMID:35131119)
- ESICM/NACCS consensus documents give best-practice recommendations for targeted temperature control after traumatic brain injury and after intracerebral haemorrhage, subarachnoid haemorrhage or ischaemic stroke. (PMID:38769582, PMID:37225535)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- When fever control improves outcomes in brain-injured patients versus general ICU patients.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Fever in the ICU is not automatically infective: drug fever, thrombosis, transfusion.
- Temperature targets after cardiac arrest.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Post-operative ICU patient on day 5 with new fever of 38.9 C.

**Reviewer Gap**: The specific diagnostic algorithm and antibiotic withholding rules in the SCCM/IDSA guideline are not in the retrieved abstract.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35131119**: *ERC-ESICM guidelines on temperature control after cardiac arrest in adults* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 35820685**: *Fever therapy in febrile adults: systematic review with meta-analyses and trial sequential analyses* (2022) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 37225535**: *Targeted temperature management in patients with intracerebral haemorrhage, subarachnoid haemorrhage, or acute ischaemic stroke: updated consensus guideline recommendations by the Neuroprotective Therapy Consensus Review (NTCR) group* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 37902340**: *Society of Critical Care Medicine and the Infectious Diseases Society of America Guidelines for Evaluating New Fever in Adult Patients in the ICU* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 38769582**: *Targeted temperature control following traumatic brain injury: ESICM/NACCS best practice consensus recommendations* (2024) [guideline]

## [0682] Fever of unknown origin FUO: classical definition, systematic investigation, common causes

**Cluster**: fuo | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Fever of unknown origin is solved faster with a structured diagnostic protocol that includes local disease prevalence. FDG PET-CT adds diagnostic value but has lower specificity than CT, and rheumatic disease is a frequent final diagnosis.

### Core Teaching Points
- Structured diagnostic protocols achieved a significantly better diagnosis rate than non-structured evaluations, and local disease prevalence should be considered. (PMID:35653154)
- FDG PET-CT contributed to the diagnosis in about 75% of FUO and inflammation of unknown origin cases, with superior sensitivity but inferior specificity compared with CT. (PMID:38499136)
- Rheumatic disorders are a common cause of FUO and IUO, so rheumatologists should be involved. (PMID:35868032)
- A modified Delphi panel recommended updated definitions of fever and inflammation of unknown origin and similar evaluation strategies for both. (PMID:38966848)
- Metagenomic next-generation sequencing has good specificity and diagnostic odds ratio for identifying causative pathogens in FUO, though optimal use needs study. (PMID:39059148)
- In children, FDG PET is a promising approach in the diagnostic work-up of FUO. (PMID:35348809)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Where mNGS and PET-CT sit in the sequence of tests.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Classic FUO definition: fever over 38.3 C for more than 3 weeks with no diagnosis after appropriate evaluation (not in abstracts).
- Common causes: infection, inflammatory disease, malignancy.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Four weeks of fever with raised CRP and negative cultures and routine imaging.

**Reviewer Gap**: The classical definition, the standard initial work-up list and the main cause categories by percentages are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35348809**: *Quantifying the contribution of &lt;sup&gt;18&lt;/sup&gt;F-FDG PET to the diagnostic assessment of pediatric patients with fever of unknown origin: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 35653154**: *Prospective Studies Comparing Structured vs Nonstructured Diagnostic Protocol Evaluations Among Patients With Fever of Unknown Origin: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 35868032**: *Rheumatic disorders among patients with fever of unknown origin: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38499136**: *The Contribution of 18F FDG PET-CT for the Investigation of Fever of Unknown Origin and Inflammation of Unknown Origin* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38966848**: *Recommendations for Updating Fever and Inflammation of Unknown Origin From a Modified Delphi Consensus Panel* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39059148**: *Metagenomic next-generation sequencing in patients with fever of unknown origin: A comprehensive systematic literature review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
