## [0683] Fibromyalgia: 2016 ACR diagnostic criteria, central sensitisation, non-pharmacological management

**Cluster**: fibromyalgia | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Fibromyalgia is managed with patient education, graded aerobic and strengthening exercise and non-drug therapies first; drugs are secondary. Exercise type should be chosen with the patient for long-term adherence.

### Core Teaching Points
- Aerobic exercise reduces pain in fibromyalgia, and exercise types such as walking and swimming should be chosen with the patient to ensure adherence. (PMID:39805734)
- In a network meta-analysis aquatic exercise was most effective for short-term pain in women with fibromyalgia, and resistance training for long-term pain. (PMID:40319533)
- Chinese non-pharmacological guidelines support interventions that enhance physical function with long-term benefit, while TMS and nutritional supplements show promise but low-certainty evidence. (PMID:40500865)
- For sleep problems in fibromyalgia, certainty is low overall, but some forms of exercise training appear more likely to improve sleep quality. (PMID:39930945)
- Physicians commonly endorse exercise, non-opioid analgesics, NSAIDs, antidepressants and physiotherapy for fibromyalgia, with considerable practice variation. (PMID:39093781)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Drug evidence is weaker than that for exercise.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- First-line fibromyalgia management is education and exercise.
- Opioids are not recommended (not in abstracts).

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman with widespread pain, fatigue and unrefreshing sleep, normal inflammatory markers.

**Reviewer Gap**: The 2016 ACR diagnostic criteria (widespread pain index and symptom severity), central sensitisation and the pharmacological options (duloxetine, pregabalin, amitriptyline) are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39093781**: *Physicians' knowledge, attitudes, and practices regarding fibromyalgia: A systematic review and meta-analysis of cross-sectional studies* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39805734**: *Aerobic Exercise Prescription for Pain Reduction in Fibromyalgia: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39930945**: *Effects of Pharmacologic and Nonpharmacologic Interventions for the Management of Sleep Problems in People With Fibromyalgia: Systematic Review and Network Meta-Analysis of Randomized Controlled Trials* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40319533**: *The most effective therapeutic exercises for pain intensity in women with fibromyalgia: A systematic review and network meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40500865**: *Guidelines on Treating Fibromyalgia With Nonpharmacological Therapies in China* (2025) [guideline]

---

## [0686] Fibrosing Mediastinitis

**Cluster**: fibrosing_mediastinitis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Fibrosing mediastinitis is a rare, progressive fibrotic response, often linked to histoplasmosis or IgG4-related disease, that compresses mediastinal vessels and airways. The evidence is mostly case series and reviews.

### Core Teaching Points
- Reviews describe pulmonary hypertension caused by fibrosing mediastinitis and its diagnostic and treatment modalities, and catheter-based treatment of pulmonary vein stenosis from FM has been systematically reviewed with unclear long-term results. (PMID:36338410, PMID:31737523)
- IgG4-related fibrosing mediastinitis can be diagnosed with CT-guided percutaneous needle biopsy. (PMID:29851832)
- Clinical trials are needed to assess rituximab for fibrosing mediastinitis in histoplasmosis. (PMID:42566694)
- A review of acute mediastinitis, mediastinal granuloma and chronic fibrosing mediastinitis covers epidemiology, etiology, presentation, treatment and prognosis. (PMID:34176697)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- No proven medical therapy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Histoplasmosis as the commonest cause in endemic areas.
- Complications: SVC syndrome, pulmonary vein or artery stenosis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young adult from the Ohio valley with progressive dyspnoea and mediastinal calcified nodes.

**Reviewer Gap**: Imaging signs, antifungal and steroid roles and stenting indications are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 29851832**: *IgG4-related fibrosing mediastinitis diagnosed with computed tomography-guided percutaneous needle biopsy: Two case reports and a review of the literature* (2018) [review]
- **PMID 31737523**: *Balloon angioplasty or stent implantation for pulmonary vein stenosis caused by fibrosing mediastinitis: a systematic review* (2019) [systematic review]
- **PMID 34176697**: *Acute mediastinitis, mediastinal granuloma, and chronic fibrosing mediastinitis: A review* (2022) [review]
- **PMID 36338410**: *Pulmonary Hypertension Caused by Fibrosing Mediastinitis* (2022) [review]
- **PMID 42566694**: *Histoplasmosis in children: emerging insights and evolving guidelines* (2026) [guideline]

## [0687] first seizure work-up

**Cluster**: epilepsy_asm | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Antiseizure medication choice depends on seizure type, sex and pregnancy potential, and HLA risk. Folic acid before and during pregnancy is advised for people with epilepsy on ASMs, and functional seizures require different management.

### Core Teaching Points
- The AAN practice guideline advises at least 0.4 mg folic acid daily preconceptionally and during pregnancy for people with epilepsy of childbearing potential treated with an ASM, to reduce neural tube defects and possibly improve neurodevelopment. (PMID:38748979)
- The NICE epilepsy guideline ties valproate prescribing in people who could become pregnant to fulfilment of the Pregnancy Prevention Programme conditions. (PMID:35700280)
- For patients with functional seizures and no other indication, the AAN guideline advises tapering off antiseizure medications. (PMID:41370742)
- A UK guideline covers HLA genotype testing for carbamazepine, oxcarbazepine and eslicarbazepine. (PMID:42002301)
- A clinical practice guideline reviews the evidence and recommendations for third-generation antiseizure medications. (PMID:41270420)
- Specialised epilepsy centre guidelines cover monitoring units, surgery, neuroimaging, neuropsychology, genetics and outpatient care. (PMID:38306606)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Comparative efficacy of newer ASMs by syndrome.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Valproate in women of childbearing potential.
- HLA-B*15:02 screening before carbamazepine in at-risk ancestry.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman with generalised epilepsy planning pregnancy on valproate.

**Reviewer Gap**: First seizure work-up (EEG, MRI, driving advice) and drug selection by syndrome are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35700280**: *Epilepsies in children, young people and adults* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 38306606**: *Guidelines for Specialized Epilepsy Centers: Executive Summary of the Report of the National Association of Epilepsy Centers Guideline Panel* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38748979**: *Teratogenesis, Perinatal, and Neurodevelopmental Outcomes After In Utero Exposure to Antiseizure Medication: Practice Guideline From the AAN, AES, and SMFM* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 41270420**: *Clinical practice guidelines for the administration of third-generation anti-seizure medications* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 41370742**: *Management of Functional Seizures Practice Guideline Executive Summary: Report of the AAN Guidelines Subcommittee* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42002301**: *HLA genotype testing for carbamazepine, oxcarbazepine and eslicarbazepine: A guideline developed by the UK Centre of Excellence in Regulatory Science and Innovation in Pharmacogenomics (CERSI-PGx)* (2026) [guideline]

## [0688] Flexible Bronchoscopy

**Cluster**: bronchoscopy | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Flexible bronchoscopy needs standardised bleeding grading, appropriate sedation and monitoring, and procedure choice matched to the lesion. Guideline quality and evidence for sedation monitoring are limited.

### Core Teaching Points
- The Nashville Delphi consensus standardised definitions of bleeding after transbronchial lung biopsy, a scale that can also apply to endobronchial biopsy and EBUS needle aspiration. (PMID:32067944)
- A systematic review of guidelines on analgosedation, monitoring and recovery for flexible bronchoscopy found low to moderate evidence, with capnometry and recovery time lacking evidence and lung-diseased patients under-represented. (PMID:34112130)
- Few clinical guidelines in adult diagnostic flexible bronchoscopy meet AGREE II quality standards. (PMID:39552889)
- A South African consensus supports transbronchial lung cryobiopsy for ILD in suitable patients where surgical lung biopsy is an alternative. (PMID:41255426)
- Guidelines and recommendations for sedation during flexible bronchoscopy differ, and international training and practice standards are needed. (PMID:40835396)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal sedation monitoring.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Grading of post-biopsy bleeding.
- Complications of transbronchial biopsy: pneumothorax and bleeding.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with diffuse interstitial lung disease being considered for transbronchial cryobiopsy.

**Reviewer Gap**: Indications, contraindications, anticoagulation handling and BAL interpretation are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 32067944**: *Standardized Definitions of Bleeding After Transbronchial Lung Biopsy: A Delphi Consensus Statement From the Nashville Working Group* (2020) [guideline]
- **PMID 34112130**: *Guidelines on analgosedation, monitoring, and recovery time for flexible bronchoscopy: a systematic review* (2021) [guideline]
- **PMID 39552889**: *Clinical practice guidelines in adult diagnostic flexible bronchoscopy: systematic review of the literature and quality appraisal with AGREE II* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 40835396**: *Comparative analysis of guidelines and recommendations for sedation during flexible bronchoscopy: a narrative review* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41255426**: *South African Thoracic Society consensus statement on transbronchial lung cryobiopsy for interstitial lung disease* (2025) [guideline]

## [0689] Flow Cytometry in Haematology

**Cluster**: flow_cytometry | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Flow cytometry underpins immunophenotypic diagnosis and measurable residual disease testing in haematological malignancy; standardised panels and technical standards improve reproducibility. Retrieval for this topic was thin.

### Core Teaching Points
- A practical approach to panel design, validation and interpretation for evaluating T-cell neoplasms by flow cytometry aligns with CLSI H62 guidelines to standardise assessment across settings. (PMID:41122063)
- Consensus recommendations standardise technical aspects of flow cytometric measurable residual disease assessment in B-cell acute lymphoblastic leukaemia to improve sensitivity, reproducibility and comparability. (PMID:42549678)
- Multiparameter flow cytometry differentiates precursor B-cell acute lymphoblastic leukemia (CD19+, CD10+, CD34+, TdT+, surface Ig negative) from mature B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphomas (surface light chain kappa/lambda restriction) within hours of bone marrow aspirate collection. (PMID:42549678)
- In mature T-cell and NK-cell lymphomas, immunophenotypic aberrancies—such as pan-T-cell antigen loss (CD3, CD5, CD7), CD4/CD8 co-expression or double negativity, and altered T-cell receptor beta chain constant region (TRBC1) expression—confirm monoclonality. (PMID:41122063)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Panel composition for specific diseases is not covered.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Flow-based MRD informs treatment decisions in B-ALL.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Peripheral blood lymphocytosis: flow cytometry to separate clonal from reactive.

**Reviewer Gap**: Standard antigens for lineage assignment, CLL and myeloma panels and pre-analytical requirements are not in the retrieved abstracts; this page is minimal.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 41122063**: *A practical approach to panel design, validation, and interpretation for the evaluation of T-cell neoplasms by flow cytometry* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 42549678**: *Consensus recommendations for technical standardization of flow cytometric measurable residual disease assessment in B-cell acute lymphoblastic leukemia: A report from The Cytometry Society, India* (2026) [guideline]

---

## [0690] Fluid Assessment and Management in Renal Disease

**Cluster**: renal_fluid | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Fluid assessment in renal disease balances volume overload against intradialytic hypotension. The retrieved evidence is limited to dialysate sodium and hydrosaline overload consensus; a general nephrology fluid source is needed.

### Core Teaching Points
- Low dialysate sodium probably increases intradialytic hypotension (RR 1.58) and cramps in chronic haemodialysis, based on moderate-certainty evidence. (PMID:39498822)
- A Spanish consensus (SEMI/SEC/S.E.N.) addresses the approach to hydrosaline overload in acute heart failure in inpatient and outpatient settings. (PMID:38432324)
- The AGA expert review on volume overload and hyponatraemia in cirrhosis recommends reviewing diet and medication history, electrolytes and kidney function, GI bleeding and infection including diagnostic paracentesis. (PMID:41114681)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Bedside volume assessment methods are not covered.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Dialysate sodium prescription and intradialytic hypotension.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Haemodialysis patient with recurrent intradialytic hypotension and high interdialytic weight gains.

**Reviewer Gap**: Clinical volume assessment, bioimpedance, ultrafiltration targets and diuretic strategy in CKD are not in the retrieved abstracts; page needs a nephrology source.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38432324**: *Consensus on the approach to hydrosaline overload in acute heart failure. SEMI/SEC/S.E.N. recommendations* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39498822**: *Low dialysate sodium levels for chronic haemodialysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41114681**: *AGA Clinical Practice Update on the Management of Ascites, Volume Overload, and Hyponatremia in Cirrhosis: Expert Review* (2025) [guideline]

## [0691] Fluid responsiveness assessment: passive leg raise, pulse pressure variation

**Cluster**: fluid_responsiveness | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Dynamic tests such as the passive leg raise, end-expiratory occlusion and tidal volume challenge are reliable predictors of fluid responsiveness. Pulse and stroke volume variation depend on ventilation conditions, so no single maneuver is clearly best.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis found no data supporting any single variability-based or dynamic maneuver as best; the passive leg raising test, end-expiratory occlusion test and tidal volume challenge are also reliable. (PMID:39217370)
- Pulse pressure and stroke volume variation predict response in ventilated patients at tidal volumes of 8 mL/kg or less, but technical and clinical variables influence their performance. (PMID:33555488, PMID:37730622)
- In the operating room the reliability of pulse pressure variation is affected by high tidal volumes and choice of colloids for the fluid challenge. (PMID:37940953)
- Change in end-tidal CO2 during passive leg raising performed moderately in predicting fluid responsiveness in ventilated patients. (PMID:35031070)
- In children, only the peak velocity variation was a reliable POCUS predictor, and evidence for guiding fluid therapy in spontaneously breathing children is lacking. (PMID:36222022)
- The 2025 ESICM guidelines give 50 recommendations on diagnosis of shock and haemodynamic monitoring. (PMID:41236566)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best test in spontaneously breathing patients and arrhythmias.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Passive leg raise as a reversible 'self-fluid bolus' test.
- Limitations of pulse pressure variation: spontaneous breathing, arrhythmia, low tidal volume.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Hypotensive ventilated septic patient: decide whether to give more fluid.

**Reviewer Gap**: Cut-off values (for example PPV above 12%) are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 33555488**: *Predictors of fluid responsiveness in critically ill patients mechanically ventilated at low tidal volumes: systematic review and meta-analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 35031070**: *Value of variation of end-tidal carbon dioxide for predicting fluid responsiveness during the passive leg raising test in patients with mechanical ventilation: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 36222022**: *Point-of-care ultrasonography to predict fluid responsiveness in children: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 37730622**: *Variables influencing the prediction of fluid responsiveness: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 37940953**: *Reliability of pulse pressure and stroke volume variation in assessing fluid responsiveness in the operating room: a metanalysis and a metaregression* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39217370**: *Assessment of fluid responsiveness using pulse pressure variation, stroke volume variation, plethysmographic variability index, central venous pressure, and inferior vena cava variation in patients undergoing mechanical ventilation: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41236566**: *ESICM guidelines on circulatory shock and hemodynamic monitoring 2025* (2025) [guideline]

## [0692] fluid resuscitation in children with septic shock

**Cluster**: paeds_septic_shock_fluids | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
In paediatric septic shock fluid resuscitation is balanced against fluid overload. Balanced crystalloids reduce acute kidney injury and hyperchloraemia compared with saline, though mortality benefit is unproven.

### Core Teaching Points
- More than 10% fluid overload 24 hours after ICU admission was associated with higher mortality, more mechanical ventilation and longer stay in children with sepsis. (PMID:37989355)
- Balanced salt solutions versus normal saline in paediatric septic shock did not reduce mortality but reduced AKI, need for renal replacement therapy and hyperchloraemia. (PMID:39988960, PMID:38916738)
- One meta-analysis found hospital length of stay was longer with balanced solutions than saline, so results across studies are not uniform. (PMID:39891147)
- Paediatric sepsis summaries state that antibiotics, fluid resuscitation and vasoactive medications are the most important interventions, and that protocols and bundles reduce mortality. (PMID:37252329)
- The Surviving Sepsis Campaign 2026 paediatric guideline highlights flexibility, early recognition and cautious intervention and acknowledges large evidence gaps. (PMID:42329352)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Bolus volume and rate in different settings, particularly without intensive care.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Fluid overload threshold and its association with mortality.
- Balanced crystalloid reduces AKI and hyperchloraemia.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Four-year-old with septic shock after 40 mL/kg fluid and rising oxygen requirement.

**Reviewer Gap**: Bolus volumes (for example 10-20 mL/kg) and when to start vasoactive drugs are not stated in the retrieved abstracts; FEAST trial was not retrieved.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37252329**: *Pediatric Sepsis: a Summary of Current Definitions and Management Recommendations* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 37989355**: *Association between fluid overload and mortality in children with sepsis: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38916738**: *Fluid resuscitation in children with severe infection and septic shock: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39891147**: *Balanced crystalloid versus saline for resuscitation in pediatric septic shock: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39988960**: *Balanced Salt Solution Versus Normal Saline as Resuscitation Fluid in Pediatric Septic Shock: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Randomized Controlled Trials* (2026) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 42329352**: *Management of Sepsis and Septic Shock in Children: Surviving Sepsis Campaign International Guidelines 2026* (2026) [guideline]

## [0693] Fluid resuscitation type in sepsis and critical illness

**Cluster**: sepsis_fluid_type | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
The ESICM 2024 guideline gives eleven recommendations on resuscitation fluid choice. Balanced crystalloids are preferred in most patients but not in traumatic brain injury, and routine albumin for sepsis survival is not recommended.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ESICM guideline on fluid therapy in adult critically ill patients (Part 1) provides eleven recommendations on resuscitation fluid choice. (PMID:38771364)
- Balanced crystalloids reduced the risk of death in non-TBI patients but increased it in traumatic brain injury in a meta-analysis versus normal saline. (PMID:35436929)
- An updated meta-analysis concluded that general use of human albumin to improve survival in septic patients cannot be recommended, though a possible survival benefit (RR 0.90) in septic shock was noted. (PMID:39969316)
- A meta-analysis of albumin concentrations suggested 4-5% and 20% albumin may improve survival compared with crystalloid in septic shock, but more RCTs are required. (PMID:37078161)
- A review and practical guideline on albumin names rational uses, including hypoalbuminaemia not responding to other treatments. (PMID:38607390)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Albumin benefit in septic shock; fluid choice in TBI.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Preferred resuscitation fluid in sepsis: balanced crystalloid.
- Exception: use caution with balanced fluids in TBI.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Septic shock patient needing large-volume resuscitation: choose fluid and consider albumin.

**Reviewer Gap**: The direction of the hypertonic crystalloid and starch/gelatin recommendations is not stated in the retrieved abstract.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35436929**: *Fluid resuscitation with balanced crystalloids versus normal saline in critically ill patients: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 37078161**: *Different Concentrations of Albumin Versus Crystalloid in Patients with Sepsis and Septic Shock: A Meta-Analysis of Randomized Clinical Trials* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38607390**: *Albumin: a comprehensive review and practical guideline for clinical use* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38771364**: *European Society of Intensive Care Medicine clinical practice guideline on fluid therapy in adult critically ill patients. Part 1: the choice of resuscitation fluids* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39969316**: *Human albumin for adults with sepsis: An updated systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2024) [meta-analysis]

## [0694] Focal Segmental Glomerulosclerosis

**Cluster**: fsgs | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
FSGS includes primary, genetic and secondary forms, so genetic testing and correct classification guide therapy. Steroid-resistant disease may respond to calcineurin inhibitors combined with steroids, and recurrence after transplant is common.

### Core Teaching Points
- A network meta-analysis of nine immunosuppressive agents in adult primary FSGS found ciclosporin plus steroids may be the best choice for improving remission rate in steroid-resistant disease, with leflunomide or mycophenolate plus steroids as steroid-sparing alternatives. (PMID:39663153)
- Clinical recommendations propose genetic testing in adults with FSGS for correct classification and therapeutic planning. (PMID:39952830)
- Recurrent FSGS after paediatric kidney transplantation is relatively common; age at onset was associated with recurrence while living related donors were not a risk factor. (PMID:37725070)
- LDL apheresis has been reported for recurrent FSGS in paediatric transplant recipients with variable protocols and outcomes. (PMID:41670656)
- Anti-nephrin antibodies are positive in a relatively high proportion of podocytopathies and relate to clinical severity and recurrence. (PMID:41518631)
- The CARI commentary on the KDIGO glomerular disease guideline notes new medications awaiting approval. (PMID:40955731)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Role of sparsentan and SGLT2 inhibitors is not covered in these abstracts.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Genetic FSGS does not respond to immunosuppression.
- Recurrence of primary FSGS after transplantation.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Adult with nephrotic syndrome and biopsy showing FSGS without secondary causes.

**Reviewer Gap**: Sparsentan, RAS blockade and supportive therapy named in topic 703 are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37725070**: *Incidence, risk factors, and outcomes of recurrent focal segmental glomerulosclerosis in pediatric kidney transplant recipients: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39663153**: *Efficacy and safety of nine immunosuppressive agents for primary focal segmental glomerulosclerosis in adults: a pairwise and network meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39952830**: *Indications for genetic testing in adults with focal segmental glomerulosclerosis* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40955731**: *CARI Guidelines Commentary on the KDIGO Clinical Practice Guideline for the Management of Glomerular Diseases* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41518631**: *Association between Anti-Nephrin Antibodies and Podocytopathies: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41670656**: *Low-density lipoprotein apheresis for recurrent focal segmental glomerulosclerosis in pediatric kidney transplant recipients: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis]

## [0695] folate deficiency

**Cluster**: folate_deficiency | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Folate deficiency is linked to inflammatory bowel disease, bariatric surgery and pregnancy loss, and assessing status requires method-appropriate cut-offs. Retrieved evidence is limited, so this page is minimal.

### Core Teaching Points
- Meta-analysis suggests inflammatory bowel disease is linked with lower folate and vitamin B12 levels, and supplementation may improve nutritional status. (PMID:28406440)
- Folic acid supplementation may reduce colorectal cancer risk in IBD, though prospective studies are required. (PMID:26905603)
- Roux-en-Y gastric bypass carries a higher risk of anaemia and micronutrient deficiency than sleeve gastrectomy, requiring more surveillance. (PMID:35048495)
- Vitamin B12 deficiency and raised homocysteine are independent risk factors for early pregnancy loss, especially in combination. (PMID:34134041)
- Global folate status in women of reproductive age should be assessed using the microbiologic assay with assay-matched cutoffs to inform neural tube defect prevention. (PMID:30239016)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Causality in IBD.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Macrocytic anaemia: B12 versus folate deficiency.
- Folic acid before B12 replacement can mask neurological B12 deficiency (not in abstracts).

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with Crohn disease and macrocytosis.

**Reviewer Gap**: Causes, clinical features, blood film findings and treatment doses are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 26905603**: *Folic Acid Supplementation May Reduce Colorectal Cancer Risk in Patients With Inflammatory Bowel Disease: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2017) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 28406440**: *Associations between Folate and Vitamin B12 Levels and Inflammatory Bowel Disease: A Meta-Analysis* (2017) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 30239016**: *Global folate status in women of reproductive age: a systematic review with emphasis on methodological issues* (2018) [systematic review]
- **PMID 34134041**: *Hyperhomocysteinemia and low vitamin B12 are associated with the risk of early pregnancy loss: A clinical study and meta-analyses* (2021) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 35048495**: *Comparative risk of anemia and related micronutrient deficiencies after Roux-en-Y gastric bypass and sleeve gastrectomy in patients with obesity: An updated meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2022) [meta-analysis]

## [0696] Follicular Lymphoma

**Cluster**: follicular_lymphoma | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Follicular lymphoma management is evolving with new immunotherapy and cellular therapies. The 2024 International Follicular Lymphoma Workshop addressed first-line treatment, early progression (POD24) and redefining cure.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2024 International Follicular Lymphoma Scientific Workshop consensus updates biology, first-line treatment, the role of progression of disease within 24 months, trial design and redefining cure. (PMID:41337699)
- Expert consensus supports CAR-T therapy as a viable option for relapsed or refractory FL, with attention to patient selection and adherence. (PMID:40985996)
- A consortium produced 15 consensus statements on haematopoietic cell transplantation and cellular therapies in FL; bispecific antibodies were out of scope. (PMID:38972511)
- For response assessment the 5-point Deauville score should be used, and biopsies may be needed to exclude marrow involvement for staging. (PMID:39747586)
- National guidelines from Australasia and Spain (GELTAMO 2025) cover diagnosis, management and follow-up, and NCCN 2025 highlights updates for FL, MCL and DLBCL. (PMID:39099075, PMID:41681868, PMID:41067277)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Role of bispecific antibodies earlier in the course.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- POD24 as a prognostic marker.
- Watch-and-wait for low-tumour-burden FL (not in abstracts).

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with FL relapsing within 18 months of chemoimmunotherapy.

**Reviewer Gap**: Obinutuzumab-based and lenalidomide-rituximab regimens (GALLIUM, RELEVANCE) named in topic 697 and the GELF criteria are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38972511**: *Clinical Practice Recommendations for Hematopoietic Cell Transplantation and Cellular Therapies in Follicular Lymphoma: A Collaborative Effort on Behalf of the American Society for Transplantation and Cellular Therapy and the European Society for Blood and Marrow Transplantation* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39099075**: *Diagnosis, management and follow-up of follicular lymphoma: a consensus practice statement from the Australasian Lymphoma Alliance* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39747586**: *ESR Essentials: imaging of lymphoma-practice recommendations by the European Society of Oncologic Imaging* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40985996**: *A SWOT-Consensus for CAR-T in Follicular Lymphoma: Fine Tuning of Patient Journey and Selection* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41067277**: *NCCN Guidelines® Insights: B-Cell Lymphomas 3.2025* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41337699**: *Consensus recommendations from the 2024 International Follicular Lymphoma Scientific Workshop* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 41681868**: *Guidelines for Diagnosis, Treatment, and Follow-Up of Patients with Follicular Lymphoma-Spanish Lymphoma Group (GELTAMO) 2025* (2026) [guideline]

## [0698] Fractional Exhaled Nitric Oxide

**Cluster**: feno | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
FeNO measures type 2 airway inflammation. Management guided by FeNO reduces asthma exacerbations in adults, and testing is best done with standardised procedures and quality control.

### Core Teaching Points
- FeNO-guided management, alone or combined with symptom scores, decreased asthma exacerbations in adults compared with conventional management. (PMID:39689589)
- A Chinese expert consensus standardises the FeNO testing process, quality control and clinical application for diagnosis and treatment of airway disease. (PMID:42760505)
- Bronchodilator reversibility was associated with a lower risk of severe asthma attacks in a patient-level analysis of risk factors for attacks. (PMID:40215991)
- FeNO is elevated in eosinophilic chronic rhinosinusitis, with potential as a noninvasive diagnostic tool, and rises in acute exacerbations of COPD, returning to baseline in stability. (PMID:41521835, PMID:41318323)
- Personal air pollution exposure, disease and allergy status and medication use influence FeNO interpretation. (PMID:38386269)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Cut-offs vary by population.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Raised FeNO predicts steroid response in asthma.
- Factors lowering FeNO: smoking, steroids.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with chronic cough and normal spirometry: FeNO result in diagnosis.

**Reviewer Gap**: Numerical thresholds (for example above 50 ppb high) and recommendations in diagnostic algorithms are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38386269**: *The Association Between Personal Air Pollution Exposures and Fractional Exhaled Nitric Oxide (FeNO): A Systematic Review* (2024) [systematic review]
- **PMID 39689589**: *Assessing the utility of fractional exhaled nitric oxide-guided management in adult patients with asthma: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40215991**: *Inflammatory and clinical risk factors for asthma attacks (ORACLE2): a patient-level meta-analysis of control groups of 22 randomised trials* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41318323**: *Fractional exhaled nitric oxide in acute exacerbations of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease: A meta-analysis with meta-regressions* (2026) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41521835**: *Fractional Exhaled Nitric Oxide for Diagnosing Eosinophilic Chronic Rhinosinusitis: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 42760505**: *Chinese Expert Consensus on the Testing Process, Quality Control and Clinical Application of Fractional Exhaled Nitric Oxide (2025)* (2026) [guideline]

## [0699] fracture risk assessment

**Cluster**: fracture_risk | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Fracture risk assessment combines clinical risk factors (via FRAX) and bone density, and guidelines recommend identifying skeletal fragility to reduce fractures. Obesity effects on fracture risk are being incorporated into FRAX.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2024 Australian guideline recommends FRAX for risk stratification, clarifies what constitutes imminent or very high fracture risk, and supports early osteoanabolic therapy. (PMID:40134107)
- The 2023 Canadian guideline stresses that identification and treatment of skeletal fragility reduces fractures and preserves mobility and independence. (PMID:37816527)
- A German guideline notes that 71% of persons with osteoporosis in Germany remain untreated. (PMID:39803994)
- A meta-analysis of BMI and fracture risk found additional risk in the highest BMI categories after adjusting for femoral neck BMD, greater in men than women, to inform the next FRAX version. (PMID:40795319)
- ACR and ECTS guidelines address prevention and treatment of glucocorticoid-induced osteoporosis, including fracture risk assessment. (PMID:37845798, PMID:39556468)
- The 2024 UK clinical guideline covers prevention and treatment of osteoporosis and is endorsed by IOF and ESCEO. (PMID:40921943)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Intervention thresholds differ between national guidelines.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Treat after a fragility fracture regardless of T-score thresholds.
- Interpretation of FRAX with glucocorticoid use.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 68-year-old woman with a wrist fracture and T-score -2.1.

**Reviewer Gap**: Specific FRAX thresholds and DXA T-score definitions are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37816527**: *Clinical practice guideline for management of osteoporosis and fracture prevention in Canada: 2023 update* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 37845798**: *2022 American College of Rheumatology Guideline for the Prevention and Treatment of Glucocorticoid-Induced Osteoporosis* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 39556468**: *Prevention and treatment of glucocorticoid-induced osteoporosis in adults: recommendations from the European Calcified Tissue Society* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39803994**: *Clinical Practice Guideline: The Diagnosis and Treatment of Osteoporosis* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40134107**: *2024 Royal Australian College of General Practitioners and Healthy Bones Australia guideline for osteoporosis management and fracture prevention in postmenopausal women and men over 50 years of age* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40795319**: *Body mass index and subsequent fracture risk: a meta-analysis to update FRAX* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40921943**: *The 2024 UK clinical guideline for the prevention and treatment of osteoporosis* (2025) [guideline]

## [0700] Frailty and comprehensive geriatric assessment

**Cluster**: frailty | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Frailty assessment feeds into comprehensive geriatric assessment (CGA), which is evidence-based in hospital settings but with limited and context-dependent trial evidence. Frailty screening is also embedded in perioperative guidance.

### Core Teaching Points
- A systematic review found comprehensive geriatric assessment for older adults with frailty in hospital settings effective, but the evidence was limited. (PMID:39146609)
- Outpatient CGA effects depended on context, and robust conclusions are difficult because of different practices and measurement tools. (PMID:40490724)
- The German clinical practice guideline on CGA in the hospital guides personalised geriatric medicine, with complex interventional studies needed in other settings. (PMID:39873689)
- The 2025 ESPEN surgery guideline adds recommendations for frailty assessment, sarcopenia diagnosis and prehabilitation. (PMID:40957230)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Which screening tool is best.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Frailty predicts adverse outcomes after surgery.
- CGA is a multidisciplinary process.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 82-year-old with weight loss, slow gait speed and a planned hip replacement.

**Reviewer Gap**: Frailty tools (Clinical Frailty Scale, Fried phenotype) and their cut-offs are not in the retrieved abstracts; this page is thin.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39146609**: *The effectiveness of the comprehensive geriatric assessment for older adults with frailty in hospital settings: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39873689**: *Clinical Practice Guideline: Comprehensive Geriatric Assessment in the Hospital* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40490724**: *The effectiveness of comprehensive geriatric assessment intervention for older people in outpatient setting: a systematic review / meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40957230**: *ESPEN guideline on clinical nutrition in surgery - Update 2025* (2025) [guideline]

## [0702] Frontotemporal dementia: behavioural variant, semantic dementia, PNFA

**Cluster**: ftd | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Frontotemporal dementia comprises behavioural, language and right temporal variants, and is often diagnosed late. Imaging with FDG-PET supports diagnosis, and consensus criteria are refining clinical recognition.

### Core Teaching Points
- A 2025 international consensus defines the clinical profile and nomenclature of frontotemporal dementia with right temporal predominance to improve diagnostic precision. (PMID:41387629)
- FDG-PET outperformed ASL-MRI in diagnosing FTD, though ASL-MRI performed well enough to justify further study as a quicker, more convenient alternative. (PMID:39134374)
- Time to diagnosis in dementia is long, and specialist services are likely key for young-onset dementia. (PMID:40716451)
- Pooled estimates of FTD incidence and prevalence provide a basis for public health planning. (PMID:40920400)
- Social cognition assessment may have diagnostic value in dementia assessment, and empathy appears preserved in Alzheimer dementia. (PMID:40145360)
- Mutations in ALS and FTD genes have differential effects on behavioural and cognitive changes, supporting genotype-informed care. (PMID:40649976)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal diagnostic biomarkers.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Behavioural variant FTD: disinhibition, apathy, loss of empathy and hyperorality.
- Gene associations: MAPT, GRN, C9orf72 (not in abstracts).

### Case Generation Hooks
- 58-year-old with new disinhibition, compulsive eating and loss of empathy with preserved memory.

**Reviewer Gap**: Core diagnostic criteria for bvFTD, semantic and nonfluent variants and typical imaging patterns are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39134374**: *Diagnostic Performance of ASL-MRI and FDG-PET in Frontotemporal Dementia: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40145360**: *Social cognition in mild cognitive impairment and dementia: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40649976**: *The Differential Effects of Genetic Mutations in ALS and FTD Genes on Behavioural and Cognitive Changes: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40716451**: *Time to Diagnosis in Dementia: A Systematic Review With Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40920400**: *Incidence and Prevalence of Frontotemporal Dementia: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41387629**: *Clinical recognition of frontotemporal dementia with right temporal predominance: a consensus statement from the International Working Group* (2025) [guideline]

## [0703] FSGS sparsentan and supportive therapy

**Cluster**: fsgs | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
FSGS includes primary, genetic and secondary forms, so genetic testing and correct classification guide therapy. Steroid-resistant disease may respond to calcineurin inhibitors combined with steroids, and recurrence after transplant is common.

### Core Teaching Points
- A network meta-analysis of nine immunosuppressive agents in adult primary FSGS found ciclosporin plus steroids may be the best choice for improving remission rate in steroid-resistant disease, with leflunomide or mycophenolate plus steroids as steroid-sparing alternatives. (PMID:39663153)
- Clinical recommendations propose genetic testing in adults with FSGS for correct classification and therapeutic planning. (PMID:39952830)
- Recurrent FSGS after paediatric kidney transplantation is relatively common; age at onset was associated with recurrence while living related donors were not a risk factor. (PMID:37725070)
- LDL apheresis has been reported for recurrent FSGS in paediatric transplant recipients with variable protocols and outcomes. (PMID:41670656)
- Anti-nephrin antibodies are positive in a relatively high proportion of podocytopathies and relate to clinical severity and recurrence. (PMID:41518631)
- The CARI commentary on the KDIGO glomerular disease guideline notes new medications awaiting approval. (PMID:40955731)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Role of sparsentan and SGLT2 inhibitors is not covered in these abstracts.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Genetic FSGS does not respond to immunosuppression.
- Recurrence of primary FSGS after transplantation.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Adult with nephrotic syndrome and biopsy showing FSGS without secondary causes.

**Reviewer Gap**: Sparsentan, RAS blockade and supportive therapy named in topic 703 are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37725070**: *Incidence, risk factors, and outcomes of recurrent focal segmental glomerulosclerosis in pediatric kidney transplant recipients: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39663153**: *Efficacy and safety of nine immunosuppressive agents for primary focal segmental glomerulosclerosis in adults: a pairwise and network meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39952830**: *Indications for genetic testing in adults with focal segmental glomerulosclerosis* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40955731**: *CARI Guidelines Commentary on the KDIGO Clinical Practice Guideline for the Management of Glomerular Diseases* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41518631**: *Association between Anti-Nephrin Antibodies and Podocytopathies: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41670656**: *Low-density lipoprotein apheresis for recurrent focal segmental glomerulosclerosis in pediatric kidney transplant recipients: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis]

## [0704] Functional Dyspepsia

**Cluster**: dyspepsia | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Uninvestigated dyspepsia without alarm features is managed by H. pylori test-and-treat and acid suppression; functional dyspepsia is diagnosed after exclusion and treated stepwise.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ACG/CAG guideline advises H. pylori eradication in infected patients with functional dyspepsia, then PPI, tricyclic antidepressant and prokinetic therapy in that order for non-responders or H. pylori-negative patients, and does not recommend routine GI motility testing. (PMID:28631728)
- The Italian functional dyspepsia guideline found insufficient evidence to recommend complementary and alternative treatments. (PMID:40628573)
- Korea's 2025 H. pylori guideline establishes quadruple therapies as the new standard via empirical treatment or PCR-guided tailored therapy, with P-CABs and bismuth-based regimens as key components. (PMID:41846442)
- Japan's 2024 revised guideline covers H. pylori management and gastric cancer prevention. (PMID:41902905)
- The ACG 2025 guideline covers diagnosis and management of gastric premalignant conditions and identifies research needs for defining high-risk populations. (PMID:40072510)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Regional antibiotic resistance drives regimen choice.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Test-and-treat versus endoscopy in dyspepsia.
- Stepwise treatment of functional dyspepsia.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 35-year-old with epigastric discomfort, no alarm features, and positive stool antigen.

**Reviewer Gap**: Alarm features that mandate endoscopy and regimen doses are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 28631728**: *ACG and CAG Clinical Guideline: Management of Dyspepsia* (2017) [guideline]
- **PMID 40072510**: *ACG Clinical Guideline: Diagnosis and Management of Gastric Premalignant Conditions* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40628573**: *Italian guidelines for the diagnosis and treatment of functional dyspepsia - joint consensus from the Italian societies of gastroenterology and endoscopy (SIGE), Neurogastroenterology and motility (SINGEM), hospital gastroenterologists and endoscopists (AIGO), digestive endoscopy (SIED) and general medicine (SIMG)* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41846442**: *Treatment of Helicobacter pylori Infection in Korea: An Evidence-Based Analysis of the Upcoming 2025 Guideline* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 41902905**: *Guidelines for the management of Helicobacter pylori infection in Japan: 2024 revised edition* (2026) [guideline]

## [0705] Functional neurological disorder treatment

**Cluster**: fnd | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Functional neurological disorder is treated with a biopsychosocial multidisciplinary approach, with physiotherapy and psychological therapy. Comorbid pain and sleep disorders are common and should be assessed; unnecessary antiseizure treatment should be avoided in functional seizures.

### Core Teaching Points
- Australian treatment recommendations advocate a multidisciplinary, person-centred, biopsychosocial approach to FND. (PMID:40552010)
- The AAN guideline advises tapering off antiseizure medications for patients with functional seizures who have no other indication for them. (PMID:41370742)
- Pain symptoms and sleep disorders are common in FND and should be routinely considered as comorbidities. (PMID:38383157, PMID:39739275)
- Hypnosis and suggestion show promising results in FND, but limitations of the evidence base prevent definitive recommendations. (PMID:38154334)
- In pregnancy and the perinatal period, women with functional seizures were exposed to unnecessary antiseizure prescriptions and ICU admissions. (PMID:39391946)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Prognostic indicators beyond clinical features.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Positive signs rather than exclusion for diagnosis (not in abstracts).
- Physiotherapy-led treatment for functional motor symptoms.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Young woman with episodes of shaking with eyes closed and no response to antiseizure medication.

**Reviewer Gap**: The specific treatment components (specialist physiotherapy, CBT), how to explain the diagnosis and positive clinical signs are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38154334**: *Hypnosis and suggestion as interventions for functional neurological disorder: A systematic review* (2024) [systematic review]
- **PMID 38383157**: *Pain and functional neurological disorder: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39391946**: *Functional neurological disorder in pregnancy, labour and the postpartum period: systematic review* (2025) [systematic review]
- **PMID 39739275**: *Sleep disorders in functional neurological disorder - a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40552010**: *Managing functional neurological disorder: treatment recommendations for health professionals in Australia* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41370742**: *Management of Functional Seizures Practice Guideline Executive Summary: Report of the AAN Guidelines Subcommittee* (2026) [guideline]

## [0706] Futility, prognostication, and family communication in critical illness

**Cluster**: icu_ethics | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
End-of-life care in the ICU should use shared decision-making, early palliative care and structured family communication. The 2024 ESICM guideline is the principal evidence-based source, supported by regional consensus on withdrawal, sedation and bereavement.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2024 ESICM guideline recommends shared decision-making strategies, suggests early integration of palliative care with standardised symptom assessment for patients at high risk of dying, and advocates communication training for staff and printed communication aids for families. (PMID:39361081)
- The same ESICM guideline highlights structured family conferences, culturally sensitive interventions, conflict-management protocols and strategies to prevent burnout among professionals. (PMID:39361081)
- A Scandinavian Delphi consensus defined domains including extubation and termination of mechanical ventilation, continuous sedation at the end of life, indicators for specialist palliative consultation, bereavement care and debriefing. (PMID:40066825)
- A meta-synthesis found that culture, religion, family and clinicians' perceptions influence continuation of futile life-sustaining treatment and that nurses' roles in ensuring comfort matter. (PMID:38050047)
- A German recommendation states end-of-life treatment focuses on alleviating symptoms, the team should anticipate and plan palliative measures, and a family-centred approach needs psychosocial and spiritual support. (PMID:40879752)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best approach to prognostication and futility conflicts between teams and families.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Withdrawal of life-sustaining treatment is an ethically equivalent decision to withholding.
- Family conferences improve satisfaction.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Day 14 ICU patient with multi-organ failure and no prospect of recovery; family disagrees about withdrawal.

**Reviewer Gap**: Legal frameworks (best interests, capacity), formal prognostic tools and NICE or GMC guidance are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38050047**: *Futile life-sustaining treatment in the intensive care unit - nurse and physician experiences: meta-synthesis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39361081**: *European Society of Intensive Care Medicine guidelines on end of life and palliative care in the intensive care unit* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 40066825**: *Consensus statements on end-of-life care in ICU - A Scandinavian multidisciplinary Delphi study* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40879752**: *[Treatment and support for adults at the end of life in intensive care. A recommendation by the Ethics Section of DIVI and the German Society for Palliative Medicine. : Part 2: Therapeutic measures and support at the end of life in intensive care]* (2025) [guideline]

## [0707] G6PD Deficiency

**Cluster**: g6pd | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
G6PD deficiency causes haemolysis with oxidant drugs such as rasburicase and 8-aminoquinolines. Genotype-guided prescribing and point-of-care testing before primaquine reduce risk; methylene blue should be avoided in rasburicase haemolysis.

### Core Teaching Points
- The expanded CPIC guideline gives medication-use recommendations by G6PD genotype and replaces the earlier rasburicase guideline. (PMID:36049896)
- Standard single or daily primaquine (0.25 mg/kg/day) and weekly primaquine (0.75 mg/kg/week) did not increase the risk of anaemia in G6PD-deficient patients in a meta-analysis. (PMID:36872344)
- In rasburicase-induced haemolytic anaemia and methaemoglobinaemia, exchange transfusion is increasingly reported, ascorbic acid may give limited benefit, and methylene blue should be avoided because it may worsen haemolysis. (PMID:37468669)
- The Access Bio/CareStart rapid diagnostic test is suitable to guide primaquine treatment and to confirm or exclude G6PD deficiency. (PMID:31834890)
- G6PD deficiency may protect against uncomplicated but not severe malaria in Africa, mainly in heterozygotes. (PMID:28382932)
- Neonatal hyperbilirubinaemia is associated with G6PD deficiency and mother-infant blood-type incompatibility. (PMID:33024835)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Risk in heterozygous females.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Drugs to avoid in G6PD deficiency: rasburicase, primaquine, dapsone.
- Do not give methylene blue in G6PD deficiency.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Man starting rasburicase for tumour lysis who becomes cyanotic with dark urine.

**Reviewer Gap**: Inheritance (X-linked), blood film findings (bite cells, Heinz bodies) and the foods or drugs list are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 28382932**: *Association of glucose-6-phosphate dehydrogenase deficiency and malaria: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2017) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 31834890**: *Performance of the Access Bio/CareStart rapid diagnostic test for the detection of glucose-6-phosphate dehydrogenase deficiency: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2019) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 33024835**: *Prevalence of neonatal hyperbilirubinaemia and its association with glucose-6-phosphate dehydrogenase deficiency and blood-type incompatibility in sub-Saharan Africa: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2020) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 36049896**: *Expanded Clinical Pharmacogenetics Implementation Consortium Guideline for Medication Use in the Context of G6PD Genotype* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 36872344**: *Association of G6PD status and haemolytic anaemia in patients receiving anti-malarial agents: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 37468669**: *Rasburicase-induced hemolytic anemia and methemoglobinemia: a systematic review of current reports* (2024) [systematic review]

## [0708] Gallbladder Carcinoma

**Cluster**: gallbladder_ca | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Gallbladder carcinoma is managed with radical resection and adjuvant therapy, with advanced disease treated within biliary tract cancer systemic regimens. Minimally invasive approaches are being evaluated and prognostic factors refine staging.

### Core Teaching Points
- The IHPBA-APHPBA Delphi consensus gives uniform terminology and practical guidance on gallbladder cancer management; borderline resectable and locally advanced disease remain unresolved. (PMID:39191539)
- NCCN Biliary Tract Cancers 2025 focuses on adjuvant chemotherapy and chemoradiation options and systemic treatment for advanced disease. (PMID:40930144)
- A network meta-analysis found no obvious benefit of laparoscopic over robotic surgery or vice versa for gallbladder cancer, and minimally invasive surgery was linked to shorter stay and less blood loss than open surgery. (PMID:39148006)
- Prognostic factors in resected gallbladder cancer can be incorporated into a prognostic model for individualised treatment, and low preoperative prognostic nutritional index predicts poor survival in biliary tract cancer. (PMID:38537060, PMID:39958446)
- Environmental risk factors for gallbladder cancer have been systematically reviewed, and prospective studies are needed to validate modifiable factors. (PMID:39370088)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Neoadjuvant therapy for borderline resectable disease.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Incidental gallbladder cancer after cholecystectomy: re-resection for T1b or higher (not in abstracts).
- Risk factors: gallstones, porcelain gallbladder, Salmonella carriers.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Incidental T2 gallbladder cancer on histology after laparoscopic cholecystectomy.

**Reviewer Gap**: Surgical extent (extended cholecystectomy, lymphadenectomy), capecitabine adjuvant evidence (BILCAP) and gemcitabine-cisplatin-durvalumab are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38537060**: *Prognostic factors for resected cases with gallbladder carcinoma: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39148006**: *Robotic, laparoscopic and open surgery for gallbladder cancer: a systematic review and network meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39191539**: *'IHPBA-APHPBA clinical practice guidelines': international Delphi consensus recommendations for gallbladder cancer* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39370088**: *Environmental Risk Factors for Gallbladder Cancer: Field-Wide Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39958446**: *Prognostic roles nutritional index in patients with resectable and advanced biliary tract cancers* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40930144**: *Biliary Tract Cancers, Version 2.2025, NCCN Clinical Practice Guidelines In Oncology* (2025) [guideline]

## [0709] Gastric and Gastroesophageal Junction Cancer

**Cluster**: gastric_cancer | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Management of gastric and gastro-oesophageal junction cancer is biomarker-driven: HER2-positive disease receives anti-HER2 therapy with chemotherapy and immunotherapy, and PD-L1 expression informs immunotherapy use.

### Core Teaching Points
- The SEOM-GEMCAD guideline states immune checkpoint inhibitors improved outcomes in patients with PD-L1 expression and that HER2-positive patients should receive anti-HER2 therapy added to chemotherapy. (PMID:39023829)
- A meta-analysis found immune checkpoint inhibitors plus trastuzumab and chemotherapy improved survival and response in advanced HER2-positive gastric or GEJ cancer, particularly with PD-L1-positive tumours and in first line. (PMID:41046084)
- The ASCO 2026 guideline update includes ramucirumab with paclitaxel, trastuzumab deruxtecan for HER2-positive gastric or GEJ adenocarcinoma, and immunotherapy for PD-L1 of at least 1 oesophageal squamous cell carcinoma after first-line chemotherapy without immunotherapy. (PMID:41747202)
- ASTRO has issued evidence-based recommendations on radiation therapy for gastric cancer. (PMID:41317985)
- National guidelines (Japan 2025, France, China HER2 edition 2025) update diagnosis, staging, treatment and follow-up and recommend multidisciplinary team discussion. (PMID:41569370, PMID:42054784, PMID:42104480)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal perioperative regimen is not covered.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- HER2 testing before first-line therapy for advanced gastric cancer.
- Role of PD-L1 combined positive score.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with HER2-positive metastatic gastric adenocarcinoma, PD-L1 CPS 5.

**Reviewer Gap**: Perioperative FLOT and D2 gastrectomy evidence is not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39023829**: *SEOM-GEMCAD-TTD clinical guideline for the diagnosis and treatment of gastric cancer (2023)* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 41046084**: *Immune checkpoint inhibitors with trastuzumab and chemotherapy in advanced HER2-positive gastric cancer: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41317985**: *Radiation Therapy for Gastric Cancer: An ASTRO Clinical Practice Guideline* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 41569370**: *Japanese gastric cancer treatment guidelines 2025 (7th edition)* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 41747202**: *Immunotherapy and Targeted Therapy for Advanced Gastroesophageal Cancer: ASCO Guideline Update* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42054784**: *Gastric cancer: French intergroup clinical practice guidelines for diagnosis, staging, treatment and follow-up (TNCD, SNFGE, FFCD, UNICANCER, GERCOR, SFCD, SFED, AFEF, SFRO, SFP, SFR, ACHBPT, RENAPE, SNFCP)* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42104480**: *Chinese guidelines for HER2-targeted therapy in gastric and gastroesophageal junction adenocarcinoma (2025 Edition)* (2026) [guideline]

## [0711] Gastric Polyps

**Cluster**: gastric_polyps | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Gastric polyps are assessed by type: fundic gland polyps relate to long-term PPI use, while hyperplastic polyps relate to H. pylori gastritis and often regress after eradication. Management requires polyp and background mucosa biopsies.

### Core Teaching Points
- An AGA clinical practice update advises considering the gastric background (H pylori gastritis, autoimmune gastritis, intestinal metaplasia) and gives best-practice advice on polyp biopsy, surrounding mucosa biopsy and resection. (PMID:41711625)
- PPI use for 12 months or longer is associated with an increased risk of fundic gland polyps; a link with gastric cancer may be biased by confounding. (PMID:27211501)
- After H pylori eradication most hyperplastic gastric polyps are eliminated, and successful eradication raises elimination more than 20-fold versus controls. (PMID:34333811)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Cancer risk of PPI-associated polyps.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Fundic gland polyps: PPI association and low malignant potential.
- Hyperplastic polyps regress after H pylori treatment.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Incidental multiple small gastric polyps in a patient on omeprazole for ten years.

**Reviewer Gap**: Adenomatous polyp management, polyposis syndromes and size thresholds for resection are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 27211501**: *Use of Proton Pump Inhibitors and Risks of Fundic Gland Polyps and Gastric Cancer: Systematic Review and Meta-analysis* (2016) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 34333811**: *Effect of Helicobacter pylori eradication on hyperplastic gastric polyps: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41711625**: *AGA Clinical Practice Update on Management of Gastric Polyps: Expert Review* (2026) [guideline]

---

## [0712] Gastrinoma Zollinger-Ellison syndrome: gastrin levels, secretin stimulation, MEN1 association

**Cluster**: zes | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Gastrinoma causing Zollinger-Ellison syndrome is a gastroduodenal neuroendocrine neoplasm needing biochemical diagnosis, localisation and tailored treatment. Retrieved sources are limited, so this page is minimal.

### Core Teaching Points
- Polish network guidelines cover gastroduodenal neuroendocrine neoplasms including gastrinoma: histopathological and localisation diagnosis, endoscopic, surgical, pharmacological and radionuclide treatment, and patient monitoring. (PMID:28540972)
- Radioguided surgery appears to aid localisation of gastrointestinal neuroendocrine tumours, with more equivocal benefit in pancreatic NETs. (PMID:34506015)
- Diagnosis of Zollinger-Ellison syndrome requires demonstrating an elevated fasting serum gastrin (>1000 pg/mL, >10-fold ULN) in the presence of gastric acid hypersecretion (gastric pH <2.0); secretin stimulation testing showing a paradoxical increase in serum gastrin (>120 pg/mL) confirms gastrinoma in equivocal cases. (PMID:28540972)
- Approximately 25% of gastrinomas occur in the context of Multiple Endocrine Neoplasia type 1 (MEN1, due to germline MEN1 mutation), which present with multicentric duodenal microgastrinomas, hyperparathyroidism, and pituitary adenomas, requiring genetic screening and lifelong surveillance. (PMID:28540972, PMID:34506015)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Diagnostic thresholds for gastrin are not in the abstracts.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Secretin stimulation test in suspected gastrinoma (not in abstracts).

### Case Generation Hooks
- Recurrent multiple duodenal ulcers with diarrhoea and a very high fasting gastrin.

**Reviewer Gap**: Fasting gastrin and secretin test criteria, MEN1 association, PPI management and surgical approaches are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 28540972**: *Gastroduodenal neuroendocrine neoplasms, including gastrinoma - management guidelines (recommended by the Polish Network of Neuroendocrine Tumours)* (2017) [guideline]
- **PMID 34506015**: *Radioguided Surgery for Gastroenteropancreatic Neuroendocrine Tumours: a Systematic Literature Review* (2021) [systematic review]

## [0713] gastroenteritis

**Cluster**: gastroenteritis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Acute gastroenteritis is mostly viral and self-limited; guidelines aim to avoid unnecessary hospitalisation and antibiotic misuse. Probiotics show only low-quality, small effects in children.

### Core Teaching Points
- A guideline on antibiotic use in acute gastroenteritis covers diagnostic tests, indications for empirical antibiotics and modification after pathogen confirmation, aiming to reduce misuse and resistance. (PMID:31271003)
- The 2024 German S2k guideline for children aims to avoid unnecessary hospitalisation and includes preventive measures, and a US outpatient guideline covers children older than six months with symptoms under seven days. (PMID:39250962, PMID:27268427)
- Saccharomyces boulardii conferred a benefit on several diarrhoeal outcomes in children, but evidence was low to very low quality. (PMID:32056266)
- Lactobacillus reuteri DSM 17938 had small effects of limited clinical relevance, with methodological limitations. (PMID:31739457)
- Norovirus accounts for about 16% of acute gastroenteritis globally. (PMID:34687838)
- In African children under five, rotavirus vaccination and surveillance of other enteric viruses are needed to control gastroenteritis. (PMID:38697269)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Probiotic benefit.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Oral rehydration is the cornerstone of treatment (not in abstracts).
- Antibiotics are not indicated for most viral gastroenteritis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Toddler with 2 days of vomiting and watery diarrhoea and mild dehydration.

**Reviewer Gap**: Dehydration assessment, oral rehydration solution composition and ondansetron use are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 27268427**: *Clinical Practice Guideline for the Treatment of Pediatric Acute Gastroenteritis in the Outpatient Setting* (2016) [guideline]
- **PMID 31271003**: *Guideline for the Antibiotic Use in Acute Gastroenteritis* (2019) [guideline]
- **PMID 31739457**: *Systematic Review with Meta-Analysis: <i>Lactobacillus reuteri</i> DSM 17938 for Treating Acute Gastroenteritis in Children. An Update* (2019) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 32056266**: *Systematic review with meta-analysis: Saccharomyces boulardii for treating acute gastroenteritis in children-a 2020 update* (2020) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 34687838**: *Global prevalence of norovirus in cases of acute gastroenteritis from 1997 to 2021: An updated systematic review and meta-analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38697269**: *Enteropathogenic viruses associated with acute gastroenteritis among African children under 5 years of age: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39250962**: *S2k Leitlinie akute infektiöse Gastroenteritis im Säuglings-, Kindes- und Jugendalter – Update 2024* (2024) [guideline]

## [0714] Gastroenteropancreatic Neuroendocrine Tumours

**Cluster**: gep_net | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Gastroenteropancreatic neuroendocrine tumours are managed by grade and site with somatostatin analogues, targeted therapy, PRRT and surgery or liver-directed therapy. Imaging follow-up is risk-adapted.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ENETS 2024 guidance paper addresses management of well-differentiated small intestine NETs grade 1-3 through ten practical key questions. (PMID:38977327)
- ASCO guidelines cover systemic therapy for tumour control in metastatic well-differentiated GEP-NETs and symptom management including liver-directed therapy, systemic options and palliative care, with limited guidance on sequencing. (PMID:37774329, PMID:40344544)
- For resistant GEP-NETs, available options include somatostatin analogues, molecular targeted therapy and peptide receptor radionuclide therapy. (PMID:38174011)
- ESR practice recommendations on PET/CT in NETs advise variable follow-up intervals for G1 and G2 tumours, with more frequent assessment for higher proliferation or advanced metastatic disease. (PMID:39387873)
- An AACE consensus covers MEN1 management including screening, surgery and monitoring. (PMID:40232217)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Sequencing of therapies.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Ki-67 and grade drive therapy choice.
- Somatostatin receptor imaging and PRRT eligibility.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with flushing, diarrhoea and a small bowel mass with liver metastases.

**Reviewer Gap**: Carcinoid syndrome management, markers (chromogranin A, 5-HIAA) and surgical principles are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37774329**: *Systemic Therapy for Tumor Control in Metastatic Well-Differentiated Gastroenteropancreatic Neuroendocrine Tumors: ASCO Guideline* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 38174011**: *Resistant gastroenteropancreatic neuroendocrine tumors: a definition and guideline to medical and surgical management* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38977327**: *European Neuroendocrine Tumor Society (ENETS) 2024 guidance paper for the management of well-differentiated small intestine neuroendocrine tumours* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39387873**: *ESR Essentials: role of PET/CT in neuroendocrine tumors-practice recommendations by the European Society for Hybrid, Molecular and Translational Imaging* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40232217**: *American Association of Clinical Endocrinology Consensus Statement on Management of Multiple Endocrine Neoplasia Type 1* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40344544**: *Symptom Management for Well-Differentiated Gastroenteropancreatic Neuroendocrine Tumors: ASCO Guideline* (2026) [guideline]

## [0715] Gastroesophageal Reflux Disease

**Cluster**: gerd | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
GERD diagnosis rests on erosive oesophagitis or abnormal pH or pH-impedance monitoring. Patients with refractory symptoms on PPI need objective confirmation before escalation, and anti-reflux procedures should not be done without it.

### Core Teaching Points
- A Latin American consensus identifies erosive oesophagitis at endoscopy and abnormal pH or pH-impedance monitoring as the most appropriate methods to diagnose GERD. (PMID:38225792)
- A consensus on refractory reflux-like symptoms despite PPI says anti-reflux procedures should not be performed without objective confirmation of GERD, using shared decision-making on diagnostic testing. (PMID:39740235)
- Italian guidelines advise against endoscopic anti-reflux procedures (MUSE, Stretta, anti-reflux mucosectomy) outside clinical trials. (PMID:40450492)
- After sleeve gastrectomy, de novo Barrett's oesophagus risk is raised, so patients should be screened and surveilled per societal guidelines. (PMID:39059544)
- The ASGE 2025 guideline summarises recommendations on diagnosis and management of GERD, and a European guideline addresses laryngopharyngeal reflux. (PMID:39692638, PMID:39719472)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Selection for anti-reflux surgery versus magnetic sphincter augmentation.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Proton pump inhibitor non-response: test before surgery.
- Barrett's surveillance.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with persistent heartburn on twice daily PPI considering fundoplication.

**Reviewer Gap**: Alarm features, lifestyle measures, PPI dosing and duration, and fundoplication outcomes are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38225792**: *Latin American consensus on diagnosis of gastroesophageal reflux disease* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39059544**: *Risk of De Novo Barrett's Esophagus Post Sleeve Gastrectomy: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Studies With Long-Term Follow-Up* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39692638**: *American Society for Gastrointestinal Endoscopy guideline on the diagnosis and management of GERD: summary and recommendations* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 39719472**: *European clinical practice guideline: managing and treating laryngopharyngeal reflux disease* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 39740235**: *Management of Patients With Refractory Reflux-Like Symptoms Despite Proton Pump Inhibitor Therapy: Evidence-Based Consensus Statements* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40450492**: *Italian guidelines for the diagnosis and management of gastro-esophageal reflux disease: Joint consensus from the Italian Societies of: Gastroenterology and Endoscopy (SIGE), Neurogastroenterology and Motility (SINGEM), Hospital Gastroenterologists and Endoscopists (AIGO), Digestive Endoscopy (SIED), and General Medicine (SIMG)* (2025) [guideline]

## [0716] Gastrointestinal Motility and Dysmotility Overview

**Cluster**: gi_motility | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Gut motility disorders span constipation, functional bloating, chronic nausea and vomiting, and postoperative ileus. This broad topic is covered by recent expert consensus documents but no overview source was retrieved.

### Core Teaching Points
- The AGA clinical practice update on refractory constipation gives best-practice advice from literature and expert opinion without formal evidence ratings. (PMID:41504675)
- A UEG/ESNM consensus offers an evidence-based framework for evaluation and treatment of chronic nausea and vomiting, and another for functional bloating and abdominal distension. (PMID:39754724, PMID:40844856)
- Peripherally acting mu-opioid receptor antagonists (PAMORAs) are efficacious for opioid-induced constipation in clinical trials. (PMID:38305669)
- Gum chewing after surgery reduced postoperative ileus symptoms and hospitalisation time, though protocols need standardising. (PMID:40689540)
- Gastrointestinal motility agents after elective colorectal surgery may be considered for ERAS protocols, pending further trials. (PMID:40439889)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Topic scope is too broad for a single page.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Opioid-induced constipation: laxatives then PAMORAs.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient on regular opioids with severe constipation unresponsive to laxatives.

**Reviewer Gap**: Split into specific motility disorders (gastroparesis, achalasia, chronic intestinal pseudo-obstruction) for approval.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38305669**: *Review document of the Spanish Association of Neurogastroenterology and Motility on the management of opioid-induced constipation* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39754724**: *European Guideline on Chronic Nausea and Vomiting-A UEG and ESNM Consensus for Clinical Management* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40439889**: *Safety and efficacy of gastrointestinal motility agents following elective colorectal surgery: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomised controlled trials* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40689540**: *The Effects of Gum Chewing in the Postoperative-Period: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40844856**: *European Consensus on Functional Bloating and Abdominal Distension-An ESNM/UEG Recommendations for Clinical Management* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41504675**: *AGA Clinical Practice Update on Evaluation and Management of Refractory Constipation: Expert Review* (2026) [guideline]
