## [0717] Gastrointestinal Stromal Tumour

**Cluster**: gist | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Gastrointestinal stromal tumours are managed in multidisciplinary reference centres, with imatinib central to systemic therapy. Follow-up schedules for low-risk resected disease may be over-intensive.

### Core Teaching Points
- The SEOM-GEIS guideline strongly encourages management of GIST patients in multidisciplinary teams at reference centres. (PMID:37129716)
- The British Sarcoma Group guideline gives consensus clinical practice recommendations with ESMO levels of evidence. (PMID:38840030)
- In patients with low recurrence risk, relapse risk remained low over 10 or more years of guideline-based follow-up, suggesting follow-up schedules be revised to reduce anxiety, cost and radiation exposure. (PMID:37930700)
- ABCG2 c.421C>A is associated with imatinib trough plasma concentration in GIST and CML patients. (PMID:36834713)
- Japanese 2022 GIST guidelines differ from ESMO in some treatments because of health insurance system differences. (PMID:38609732)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal follow-up for low-risk disease.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- KIT and PDGFRA mutations determine imatinib response (not in abstracts).
- Risk stratification by size, mitotic count and site.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Gastric submucosal mass 6 cm with high mitotic count on biopsy.

**Reviewer Gap**: Adjuvant imatinib duration, mutation-specific therapy and risk classification details are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 36834713**: *Impact of <i>ABCG2</i> and <i>ABCB1</i> Polymorphisms on Imatinib Plasmatic Exposure: An Original Work and Meta-Analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 37129716**: *SEOM-GEIS clinical guideline for gastrointestinal stromal tumors (2022)* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 37930700**: *Guideline-Based Follow-Up Outcomes in Patients With Gastrointestinal Stromal Tumor With Low Risk of Recurrence: A Report From the Italian Sarcoma Group* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 38609732**: *English version of Japanese Clinical Practice Guidelines 2022 for gastrointestinal stromal tumor (GIST) issued by the Japan Society of Clinical Oncology* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38840030**: *Gastrointestinal stromal tumour (GIST): British Sarcoma Group clinical practice guidelines* (2025) [guideline]

## [0718] Gastroparesis

**Cluster**: gastroparesis | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Gastroparesis is managed with dietary measures, glycaemic control, opioid avoidance and prokinetic or antiemetic drugs; metoclopramide or erythromycin is appropriate for initial pharmacologic treatment. Treatment remains an unmet need.

### Core Teaching Points
- The AGA guideline says metoclopramide or erythromycin is appropriate for initial pharmacologic treatment, other recommendations require shared decision-making, and considerable treatment needs remain unmet. (PMID:40976635)
- A Rome Foundation consensus on idiopathic gastroparesis recommends opioid cessation and considers antiemetic and prokinetic agents potentially beneficial. (PMID:39674226)
- Adverse event reporting for domperidone and metoclopramide in gastroparesis is imprecise, limiting firm conclusions. (PMID:36749528)
- The ACG 2022 gastroparesis guideline provides key concept statements based on expert consensus alongside GRADE recommendations. (PMID:35926490)
- A 2026 Australian position statement and an expert consensus on body surface gastric mapping phenotypes propose new approaches to assessment of idiopathic gastroparesis and gastroduodenal disorders. (PMID:42235947, PMID:42381563)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Definition of idiopathic gastroparesis is under review.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Metoclopramide tardive dyskinesia warning.
- Gastric emptying scintigraphy 4-hour protocol (not in abstracts).

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with postprandial fullness and vomiting of undigested food, negative endoscopy.

**Reviewer Gap**: Gastric emptying testing protocol, dietary advice and device or surgical options are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35926490**: *ACG Clinical Guideline: Gastroparesis* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 36749528**: *Risk of Adverse Events Associated with Domperidone and Metoclopramide in Gastroparesis: Systematic Review and Meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39674226**: *Rome Foundation and international neurogastroenterology and motility societies' consensus on idiopathic gastroparesis* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40976635**: *AGA Clinical Practice Guideline on Management of Gastroparesis* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 42235947**: *Gastroenterological Society of Australia Position Statement on the Assessment and Management of Idiopathic Gastroparesis* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42381563**: *Expert Clinical Consensus on Body Surface Gastric Mapping Phenotypes for Gastroduodenal Disorders: 'Auckland Classification' v1.0* (2026) [guideline]

## [0719] Gender-affirming hormone therapy: feminising and masculinising protocols, safety monitoring

**Cluster**: gender_affirming | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Evidence for gender-affirming hormone therapy outcomes is of low certainty in young people, and practice guidance focuses on monitoring and cancer screening for transgender people. Retrieved sources do not state hormone regimens.

### Core Teaching Points
- A systematic review of gender-affirming hormone therapy in people aged under 26 found very uncertain evidence on effects, so benefit or harm cannot be excluded. (PMID:39855725)
- Testosterone therapy in people assigned female at birth may affect renal function, so monitoring is advised particularly in early months or with pre-existing renal disease. (PMID:40575258)
- Cancer care and screening guidance for transgender and gender-diverse people is still developing, with practice gaps in inclusive data collection and population-specific protocols. (PMID:39652385, PMID:41285688)
- A BASHH best practice statement covers management of testosterone-associated vulvovaginitis, with topical steroids for refractory cases and need for trans-specific trials. (PMID:42298790)
- Direct-to-consumer telemedicine providers of gender-affirming hormones offer competitive costs, but compliance with all standards of care is unclear. (PMID:40331257)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Long-term outcomes.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Monitoring on testosterone: haematocrit, lipids and renal function.
- Cancer screening based on organs present.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Transgender man on testosterone with vaginal atrophy and irregular bleeding.

**Reviewer Gap**: Feminising and masculinising regimens, targets and contraindications are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39652385**: *Cancer care for transgender and gender-diverse people: Practical, literature-driven recommendations from the Multinational Association of Supportive Care in Cancer* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 39855725**: *Gender affirming hormone therapy for individuals with gender dysphoria aged &lt;26 years: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40331257**: *The cost and guideline adherence of direct-to-consumer telemedicine companies offering gender-affirming hormone therapy* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40575258**: *Effects of gender affirming hormone therapy with testosterone on renal function of assigned female at birth transgender people: a meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41285688**: *Cancer screening guidelines for transgender individuals: a narrative review of current recommendations and practice gaps* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 42298790**: *BASHH best practice statement on the management of testosterone-associated vulvovaginitis* (2026) [guideline]

## [0721] gestational diabetes

**Cluster**: gdm | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Metformin is a safe and effective alternative to insulin in gestational diabetes in short-term outcomes, with limited long-term child data; continuous glucose monitoring and automated insulin delivery now extend to pregnancy in pregestational diabetes. Follow-up after GDM requires periodic glucose testing.

### Core Teaching Points
- A meta-analysis concluded that metformin appears safe and effective in gestational diabetes, with limited long-term outcome data for children. (PMID:41354637)
- A systematic review comparing pharmacological treatments found metformin a promising alternative to insulin, with uncertainty about glibenclamide safety. (PMID:39923989)
- Guideline-informed expert recommendations support the use of metformin for hyperglycaemia in pregnancy and periconception. (PMID:40734525)
- After a new Danish treatment guideline, labour induction rates rose without a decrease in birthweight or large-for-gestational-age incidence. (PMID:41312703)
- A consensus statement endorsed by 24 societies addresses CGM and automated insulin delivery in pregnancy for women with type 1, type 2 and other diabetes. (PMID:41421368)
- Guidelines for women with prior GDM stress periodic glucose tolerance screening, structured follow-up and education. (PMID:40746619)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Long-term offspring outcomes of metformin.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Postpartum OGTT after gestational diabetes.
- Metformin versus insulin in GDM.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 28-week patient with GDM failing diet control.

**Reviewer Gap**: Screening thresholds (OGTT cut-offs), glycaemic targets and timing of delivery are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39923989**: *Efficacy and safety of pharmacological treatments for gestational diabetes: a systematic review comparing metformin with glibenclamide and insulin* (2025) [systematic review]
- **PMID 40734525**: *Metformin in the treatment of hyperglycemia during pregnancy and periconception: guideline-informed expert recommendations from seven countries/states in the Asia-Pacific region* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40746619**: *Clinical guidelines for women with a history of gestational diabetes and their offspring* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41312703**: *Implications of a new guideline on treating gestational diabetes in Denmark* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41354637**: *Metformin safety during pregnancy in women with gestational diabetes mellitus: A systematic review and meta-analysis of maternal, neonatal and long-term outcomes* (2026) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41421368**: *Application of continuous glucose monitoring and automated insulin delivery technologies for pregnant women with type 1, type 2, or gestational diabetes: an international consensus statement* (2026) [guideline]

## [0723] Gestational Trophoblastic Neoplasia

**Cluster**: gtn | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Gestational trophoblastic neoplasia is highly curable with hCG-monitored chemotherapy; immunotherapy such as pembrolizumab is an option for chemoresistant or relapsed high-risk disease.

### Core Teaching Points
- NCCN guidelines give treatment recommendations for hydatidiform mole, persistent post-molar GTN, low-risk GTN, high-risk GTN and intermediate trophoblastic tumour. (PMID:31693991)
- Pembrolizumab is an effective treatment for high-risk GTN with chemoresistant or relapsed disease, including PSTT and ETT, in a systematic review and meta-analysis. (PMID:40031424)
- European practical guidelines for treatment of gestational trophoblastic disease were developed by consensus with literature support, and EOTTD guidelines cover pathological and genetic diagnosis of hydatidiform moles. (PMID:40359461, PMID:37857997)
- SOGC Guideline 408 covers management of gestational trophoblastic diseases. (PMID:33384141)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal immunotherapy sequencing.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Persistently raised hCG after molar evacuation.
- Low-risk GTN: methotrexate or dactinomycin (not in abstracts).

### Case Generation Hooks
- Woman with rising hCG after evacuation of a complete mole.

**Reviewer Gap**: FIGO scoring and first-line regimens are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 31693991**: *Gestational Trophoblastic Neoplasia, Version 2.2019, NCCN Clinical Practice Guidelines in Oncology* (2019) [guideline]
- **PMID 33384141**: *Guideline No. 408: Management of Gestational Trophoblastic Diseases* (2021) [guideline]
- **PMID 37857997**: *Practical guidelines of the EOTTD for pathological and genetic diagnosis of hydatidiform moles* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 40031424**: *Pembrolizumab in gestational trophoblastic neoplasia: Systematic review and meta-analysis with sub-group analysis of potential prognostic factors* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40359461**: *Practical Guidelines for the Treatment of Gestational Trophoblastic Disease: Collaboration of the European Organisation for the Treatment of Trophoblastic Disease (EOTTD)-European Society of Gynaecologic Oncology (ESGO)-Gynecologic Cancer InterGroup (GCIG)-International Society for the Study of Trophoblastic Diseases (ISSTD)* (2025) [guideline]

## [0724] GI Adverse Effects of Immunotherapy

**Cluster**: gi_irae | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Immune checkpoint inhibitors cause diarrhoea, colitis and hepatitis that are treated with corticosteroids, with early escalation to additional immunosuppressants in steroid-refractory cases. Low-grade diarrhoea may be over-treated.

### Core Teaching Points
- A systematic review of ICI-induced diarrhoea and colitis suggests a risk of corticosteroid overtreatment and a more tailored approach, particularly for low-grade diarrhoea. (PMID:35917654)
- A polygenic risk score for ulcerative colitis predicted ICI-mediated colitis (OR 2.20 per SD with combination ICIs). (PMID:38531883)
- In steroid-resistant immune-related adverse events, additional immunosuppressive agents should be started promptly if corticosteroid response is insufficient within 3 days. (PMID:38233099)
- For steroid-refractory ICI hepatitis, secondary options exist, but practice relies mostly on expert consensus. (PMID:39298568)
- A systematic review of ICI-related gastrointestinal, hepatobiliary and pancreatic adverse events provides a practical clinical tool for diagnosis and management. (PMID:39542654)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal steroid dose and duration; selection of second-line biologic.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Infliximab or vedolizumab for steroid-refractory ICI colitis (not in abstracts).
- Rule out infection before escalating immunosuppression.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient on ipilimumab-nivolumab with 7 loose stools per day above baseline.

**Reviewer Gap**: Grading systems (CTCAE), endoscopic findings and the specific biologics named are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35917654**: *Immune checkpoint Inhibitor-Induced diarrhea and Colitis: Incidence and Management. A systematic review and Meta-analysis* (2022) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38233099**: *Corticosteroid-resistant immune-related adverse events: a systematic review* (2024) [systematic review]
- **PMID 38531883**: *Polygenic risk score for ulcerative colitis predicts immune checkpoint inhibitor-mediated colitis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39298568**: *Steroid-refractory immune checkpoint inhibitor (ICI) hepatitis and ICI rechallenge: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39542654**: *Systematic review of immune checkpoint inhibitor-related gastrointestinal, hepatobiliary, and pancreatic adverse events* (2024) [systematic review]

## [0725] Giant cell arteritis: ESR/CRP, temporal artery ultrasound, high-dose steroids

**Cluster**: gca | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Giant cell arteritis is a vision-threatening emergency requiring rapid assessment, ideally through fast-track clinics with prompt imaging. Imaging now complements or replaces biopsy, and steroid-sparing biologics are guided by national recommendations.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2023 EULAR recommendations update the role of imaging in diagnosis and assessment of large vessel vasculitis, including monitoring of structural damage at sites of earlier inflammation. (PMID:37550004, PMID:42107587)
- Recommendations for GCA fast-track clinics stress partnerships for rapid neurological and ophthalmological examination, MRI, PET-CT and temporal artery biopsy. (PMID:38717506)
- French recommendations standardise use of immunosuppressants and biologics in GCA based on literature and expert experience. (PMID:39500637)
- International recommendations for early referral of suspected polymyalgia rheumatica complement EULAR/ACR management guidance, and a German S2e guideline updates PMR treatment with individualised exercise alongside drugs. (PMID:38050004, PMID:40560440)
- Multidisciplinary protocols for PMR and GCA require higher clinical suspicion and integrated care across specialties and levels. (PMID:38142973)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Which patients need biologics early.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Jaw claudication and visual symptoms prompt urgent steroids and referral.
- Ultrasound halo sign in temporal arteritis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 72-year-old with new headache, scalp tenderness, jaw pain and ESR 90.

**Reviewer Gap**: High-dose prednisolone regimen, tocilizumab use and ESR/CRP thresholds are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 37550004**: *EULAR recommendations for the use of imaging in large vessel vasculitis in clinical practice: 2023 update* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38050004**: *Recommendations for early referral of individuals with suspected polymyalgia rheumatica: an initiative from the international giant cell arteritis and polymyalgia rheumatica study group* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38142973**: *Executive summary on the optimization of the multidisciplinary and integrated approach to polymyalgia rheumatica and giant cell arteritis in Madrid region* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38717506**: *[Recommendations for defining giant cell arteritis fast-track clinics]* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39500637**: *Use of immunosuppressants and biologics in giant cell arteritis: Recommendations of the French Study Group for Large Vessel Vasculitis (GEFA)* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40560440**: *[S2e guidelines on the treatment of polymyalgia rheumatica: update 2024 : Evidence-based guidelines of the German Society for Rheumatology and Clinical Immunology (DGRh), the Austrian Society for Rheumatology and Rehabilitation (ÖGR) and the Swiss Society for Rheumatology (SGR) and the participating medical scientific specialist societies and other organizations]* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 42107587**: *French recommendations for the use of imaging in giant cell arteritis* (2026) [guideline]

## [0728] GI Bleeding in Anticoagulated Patients

**Cluster**: gi_bleed_anticoag | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
In anticoagulated patients with GI bleeding, resuscitation and endoscopic haemostasis come first, reversal agents are reserved for life-threatening bleeds, and anticoagulation is resumed promptly, usually within a week.

### Core Teaching Points
- Anticoagulant reversal agents are recognised in guidelines for GI bleeding but should be reserved for truly life-threatening scenarios. (PMID:33403486)
- An expert consensus on antidotes for DOAC-associated severe GI bleeding advises resuming anticoagulation after successful endoscopic haemostasis, usually within a week, based on individual bleeding and thromboembolic risk. (PMID:37586394)
- The 2023 ACG lower GI bleeding guideline gives updated recommendations on resuming antiplatelet and anticoagulant medications after bleeding has stopped. (PMID:36735555)
- The ACG-CAG guideline found insufficient evidence to recommend same-day versus delayed (1 to 7 days) resumption of anticoagulants or P2Y12 inhibitors after endoscopic procedures. (PMID:35297395)
- Among patients with atrial fibrillation and end-stage renal disease, it cannot be concluded that GI bleeding is lower with DOACs than vitamin K antagonists. (PMID:37648582)
- Where specific reversal agents are unavailable, prothrombin complex concentrates may be considered for DOAC-related bleeding. (PMID:40448969)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Timing of resumption.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Reversal is for life-threatening bleeding only.
- Resume anticoagulation within about a week after haemostasis.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient on apixaban with melaena and haemoglobin drop.

**Reviewer Gap**: Haemodynamic resuscitation targets, andexanet versus PCC choice and endoscopy timing are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 33403486**: *Anticoagulant Reversal in Gastrointestinal Bleeding: Review of Treatment Guidelines* (2021) [guideline]
- **PMID 35297395**: *American College of Gastroenterology-Canadian Association of Gastroenterology Clinical Practice Guideline: Management of Anticoagulants and Antiplatelets During Acute Gastrointestinal Bleeding and the Periendoscopic Period* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 36735555**: *Management of Patients With Acute Lower Gastrointestinal Bleeding: An Updated ACG Guideline* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 37586394**: *[Use of specific antidotes in DOAC-associated severe gastrointestinal bleeding - an expert consensus - Antagonozation of direct oral anticoagulants in gastrointestinal hemorrhages]* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 37648582**: *Efficacy and safety of direct oral anticoagulants vs vitamin K antagonists in patients with atrial fibrillation and end-stage renal disease on hemodialysis: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40448969**: *2025 Guidelines for direct oral anticoagulants: a practical guidance on the prescription, laboratory testing, peri-operative and bleeding management* (2025) [guideline]

## [0729] GI Endoscopy Quality Indicators

**Cluster**: endoscopy_quality | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Endoscopy quality is measured by performance indicators for each procedure, and AI-assisted colonoscopy improves adenoma detection though not sessile serrated lesion detection. Barriers to implementing quality measurement are organisational.

### Core Teaching Points
- AI-assisted colonoscopy significantly improved adenoma detection but not sessile serrated lesion detection, irrespective of endoscopist experience or setting. (PMID:39216648)
- Computer-aided colonoscopy lowered adenoma miss rates and improved polyp detection across sizes and morphologies. (PMID:36350048)
- ESGE updated performance measures for small-bowel endoscopy in 2025, and published ERCP quality indicators are divided into pre-procedure, intra-procedural and post-procedure measures. (PMID:39909070, PMID:37041734)
- Societies should make quality measurement intrinsic to daily endoscopy practice and survey which measures are local priorities. (PMID:33323062)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Impact of AI on patient outcomes is separate and uncertain.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Adenoma detection rate as the key colonoscopy quality indicator.
- Bowel preparation adequacy target above 90% (see colonoscopy page).

### Case Generation Hooks
- Unit audit shows low adenoma detection for one endoscopist.

**Reviewer Gap**: Numeric ADR benchmarks, caecal intubation and withdrawal time targets are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 33323062**: *Overcoming the barriers to dissemination and implementation of quality measures for gastrointestinal endoscopy: European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE) and United European Gastroenterology (UEG) position statement* (2021) [guideline]
- **PMID 36350048**: *Effect of computer-aided colonoscopy on adenoma miss rates and polyp detection: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2023) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 37041734**: *Quality indicators in endoscopic retrograde cholangiopancreatography: a brief review of established guidelines* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 39216648**: *Use of artificial intelligence improves colonoscopy performance in adenoma detection: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39909070**: *Performance measures for small-bowel endoscopy: a European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE) Quality Improvement Initiative - Update 2025* (2025) [guideline]

## [0730] GI Endoscopy Sedation and Monitoring

**Cluster**: endoscopy_sedation | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Sedation guidance for GI endoscopy varies and often lacks supporting evidence. Remimazolam shows similar efficacy to propofol with a better safety profile, and non-anaesthesiologist propofol requires training and protocols.

### Core Teaching Points
- A systematic review found sedation recommendations for routine GI endoscopy differ across guidelines and often lack supporting evidence, with implications for safety and efficiency. (PMID:33413147)
- Remimazolam had clinically similar efficacy and greater safety than propofol for sedation in GI endoscopy, but evidence in elderly patients is not yet strong enough. (PMID:38443286, PMID:38261005)
- The Italian position paper describes training of non-anaesthesiologist personnel and shared hospital protocols for propofol use. (PMID:28951114)
- Korean and Singapore guidelines provide recommendations to minimise risk in endoscopic sedation, including by non-anaesthesiologists. (PMID:35189678, PMID:35091728)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Routine capnography benefit.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Monitoring during moderate sedation: oxygen saturation and heart rate.
- Propofol use by trained non-anaesthesiologists.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Elderly patient with COPD booked for colonoscopy: sedation choice.

**Reviewer Gap**: ASA classification, fasting rules and capnography recommendations are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 28951114**: *Italian Society of Digestive Endoscopy (SIED) position paper on the non-anaesthesiologist administration of propofol for gastrointestinal endoscopy* (2017) [guideline]
- **PMID 33413147**: *Sedation practices for routine gastrointestinal endoscopy: a systematic review of recommendations* (2021) [guideline]
- **PMID 35091728**: *Academy of Medicine, Singapore clinical guideline on the use of sedation by non-anaesthesiologists during gastrointestinal endoscopy in the hospital setting* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 35189678**: *2021 Korean Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy Clinical Practice Guidelines for Endoscopic Sedation* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 38261005**: *Remimazolam versus propofol for sedation in gastrointestinal endoscopy and colonoscopy within elderly patients: a meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38443286**: *Remimazolam versus propofol for sedation in gastrointestinal endoscopic procedures: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]

## [0731] Glioblastoma Multiforme

**Cluster**: gbm | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Standard care for newly diagnosed glioblastoma is maximal safe resection followed by radiotherapy with temozolomide. Tumour treating fields add survival in pooled data, and bevacizumab adds no overall survival benefit.

### Core Teaching Points
- Adding temozolomide to radiotherapy generally improved overall and progression-free survival compared with radiotherapy alone, with similar toxicity. (PMID:40045273)
- A systematic review found no evidence that adding bevacizumab to temozolomide and radiotherapy improves overall or progression-free survival. (PMID:39216020)
- Tumour treating fields as an adjuvant to temozolomide maintenance improved overall and progression-free survival in a pooled analysis of randomised trials and real-world studies. (PMID:41741710)
- Reoperation for recurrent glioblastoma may improve survival in selected cases, and complete resection may correlate with better post-recurrence survival than partial resection. (PMID:40314867)
- The ASTRO guideline gives recommendations for radiation therapy in WHO grade 4 adult-type diffuse glioma and stresses multidisciplinary care and trial participation. (PMID:40578479)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Value of immune checkpoint inhibitors is very uncertain.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- MGMT promoter methylation predicts benefit from temozolomide (not in abstracts).
- Stupp protocol.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 60-year-old with ring-enhancing frontal lesion confirmed as glioblastoma.

**Reviewer Gap**: The Stupp trial itself, MGMT methylation and doses are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 39216020**: *A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis on the Effectiveness of Radiotherapy and Temozolomide Treatment With or Without Bevacizumab in Patients With Glioblastoma Multiforme* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40045273**: *The role of temozolomide as adjuvant therapy in glioblastoma management: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40314867**: *Current trends in reoperation for recurrent glioblastoma: a meta-analysis (2007-2023)* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 40578479**: *Radiation Therapy for WHO Grade 4 Adult-Type Diffuse Glioma: An ASTRO Clinical Practice Guideline* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41741710**: *Impact of tumor treating fields on overall and progression-free survival in newly diagnosed glioblastoma: A systematic review and meta-analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis]

## [0733] global burden bacterial antimicrobial resistance

**Cluster**: amr_burden | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Bacterial antimicrobial resistance caused about 1.27 million attributable and 4.95 million associated deaths in 2019 according to the Global Research on Antimicrobial Resistance analysis, with drug-resistant pathogens including MRSA among the leading causes.

### Core Teaching Points
- The GRAM analysis estimated 4.95 million deaths associated with bacterial AMR and 1.27 million attributable to it in 2019. (PMID:35065702)
- Six leading pathogens (E coli, S aureus, K pneumoniae, S pneumoniae, A baumannii and P aeruginosa) caused 929,000 attributable and 3.57 million associated AMR deaths, and MRSA alone caused more than 100,000 attributable deaths. (PMID:35065702)
- Vancomycin-resistant Enterococcus is detectable along the food chain, supporting a One Health approach. (PMID:39849844)
- Carbapenem-resistant Klebsiella pneumoniae shows regional and age-related resistance variation in China, with mortality implications. (PMID:41088290)
- Strategies to reduce AMR in newborns in low- and middle-income countries face barriers including delays in culture reporting, non-adherence to infection prevention and control, and prescribing for culture-negative sepsis. (PMID:41856138)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Uncertainty in modelled estimates for data-poor regions.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Leading pathogens in AMR mortality.
- One Health approach.

### Case Generation Hooks
- ICU outbreak of carbapenem-resistant Klebsiella.

**Reviewer Gap**: Regional burden patterns (highest in sub-Saharan Africa), and the antimicrobial classes involved are not fully stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35065702**: *Global burden of bacterial antimicrobial resistance in 2019: a systematic analysis* (2022) [systematic review]
- **PMID 39849844**: *Vancomycin-resistant Enterococcus prevalence and its association along the food chain: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41088290**: *Resistance profile and influence factors of carbapenem-resistant Klebsiella pneumoniae (CRKP) causing infections in China: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41856138**: *Strategies to reduce antimicrobial resistance in newborns in low-income and middle-income countries: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2026) [meta-analysis]

## [0734] global prevalence depression anxiety disorders COVID-19 pandemic

**Cluster**: covid_mental_health | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
The COVID-19 pandemic increased the burden of depressive and anxiety disorders globally, with higher SARS-CoV-2 infection rates and reduced mobility associated with higher prevalence. Healthcare workers, students and perinatal women are affected groups.

### Core Teaching Points
- A global analysis found daily SARS-CoV-2 infection rates and reductions in human mobility were associated with increased prevalence of major depressive disorder and anxiety disorders, and called for strengthened mental health systems. (PMID:34634250)
- Umbrella reviews report high prevalence of stress, anxiety, depression and sleep disturbance among healthcare workers during the pandemic, with calls for institutional support. (PMID:38722888, PMID:39862981)
- A global meta-analysis examined the prevalence of anxiety and depression among medical students during the pandemic. (PMID:38858700)
- Perinatal depression and anxiety were common during the pandemic, but it cannot be firmly affirmed that the pandemic was the main cause. (PMID:38113292)
- Depression, anxiety and stress symptoms vary by trimester of pregnancy. (PMID:39700731)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Causal contribution of the pandemic versus other factors.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Risk groups for pandemic-related mental distress.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Nurse with insomnia and low mood after repeated ICU surges.

**Reviewer Gap**: Headline percentage increases and treatment recommendations are not in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 34634250**: *Global prevalence and burden of depressive and anxiety disorders in 204 countries and territories in 2020 due to the COVID-19 pandemic* (2021) [systematic review]
- **PMID 38113292**: *Global prevalence of perinatal depression and anxiety during the COVID-19 pandemic: An umbrella review and meta-analytic synthesis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38722888**: *Stress, anxiety, depression and sleep disturbance among healthcare professional during the COVID-19 pandemic: An umbrella review of 72 meta-analyses* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38858700**: *Global prevalence of anxiety and depression among medical students during the COVID-19 pandemic: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39700731**: *Global prevalence of depression, anxiety, and stress symptoms in different trimesters of pregnancy: A meta-analysis and meta-regression* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39862981**: *An umbrella review and meta-analysis of 87 meta-analyses examining healthcare workers' mental health during the COVID-19 pandemic* (2025) [meta-analysis]

## [0735] GLP-1 receptor agonist liraglutide cardiovascular outcomes type 2 diabetes

**Cluster**: glp1 | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
GLP-1 receptor agonists lower cardiovascular events in type 2 diabetes (LEADER, SUSTAIN-6) and produce substantial weight loss, with tirzepatide and semaglutide the most effective. Adverse effect monitoring and treatment individualisation are emphasised.

### Core Teaching Points
- LEADER found the rate of cardiovascular death, nonfatal myocardial infarction or nonfatal stroke was lower with liraglutide than placebo in type 2 diabetes at high cardiovascular risk (HR 0.87, 95% CI 0.78-0.97). (PMID:27295427)
- SUSTAIN-6 found the same composite was significantly lower with semaglutide than placebo (HR 0.74, 95% CI 0.58-0.95), confirming noninferiority. (PMID:27633186)
- A meta-analysis of randomised trials found semaglutide reduces major cardiovascular outcomes without significantly affecting nonfatal stroke or hospitalisation, lowers serious adverse events and increases treatment discontinuation. (PMID:41272444)
- For weight loss, GLP-1-based therapy is an effective component of obesity management, with tirzepatide and semaglutide showing the greatest relative efficacy; tirzepatide also remitted obstructive sleep apnoea and MASH. (PMID:41824845, PMID:41039116)
- A meta-analysis of semaglutide found increased risk of non-arteritic anterior ischaemic optic neuropathy with prolonged exposure and predisposing factors, supporting risk-benefit assessment and ophthalmologic monitoring. (PMID:40962119)
- GLP-1 receptor agonists as an adjunct to bariatric surgery gave significant weight loss and cardiometabolic improvement compared with placebo or lifestyle measures. (PMID:41071360)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Long-term safety and weight regain after stopping.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Cardiovascular benefit of liraglutide and semaglutide in type 2 diabetes with established CVD.
- GI adverse effects and gallbladder disease.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Type 2 diabetic with prior MI and BMI 35: choosing between SGLT2 inhibitor and GLP-1 RA.

**Reviewer Gap**: Contraindications (medullary thyroid carcinoma, MEN2), pancreatitis risk and gastroparesis considerations are not in the retrieved abstracts.

## [0736] GLP-1 receptor agonists: weight loss, cardiovascular outcomes, GI tolerability

**Cluster**: glp1 | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
GLP-1 receptor agonists lower cardiovascular events in type 2 diabetes (LEADER, SUSTAIN-6) and produce substantial weight loss, with tirzepatide and semaglutide the most effective. Adverse effect monitoring and treatment individualisation are emphasised.

### Core Teaching Points
- LEADER found the rate of cardiovascular death, nonfatal myocardial infarction or nonfatal stroke was lower with liraglutide than placebo in type 2 diabetes at high cardiovascular risk (HR 0.87, 95% CI 0.78-0.97). (PMID:27295427)
- SUSTAIN-6 found the same composite was significantly lower with semaglutide than placebo (HR 0.74, 95% CI 0.58-0.95), confirming noninferiority. (PMID:27633186)
- A meta-analysis of randomised trials found semaglutide reduces major cardiovascular outcomes without significantly affecting nonfatal stroke or hospitalisation, lowers serious adverse events and increases treatment discontinuation. (PMID:41272444)
- For weight loss, GLP-1-based therapy is an effective component of obesity management, with tirzepatide and semaglutide showing the greatest relative efficacy; tirzepatide also remitted obstructive sleep apnoea and MASH. (PMID:41824845, PMID:41039116)
- A meta-analysis of semaglutide found increased risk of non-arteritic anterior ischaemic optic neuropathy with prolonged exposure and predisposing factors, supporting risk-benefit assessment and ophthalmologic monitoring. (PMID:40962119)
- GLP-1 receptor agonists as an adjunct to bariatric surgery gave significant weight loss and cardiometabolic improvement compared with placebo or lifestyle measures. (PMID:41071360)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Long-term safety and weight regain after stopping.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Cardiovascular benefit of liraglutide and semaglutide in type 2 diabetes with established CVD.
- GI adverse effects and gallbladder disease.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Type 2 diabetic with prior MI and BMI 35: choosing between SGLT2 inhibitor and GLP-1 RA.

**Reviewer Gap**: Contraindications (medullary thyroid carcinoma, MEN2), pancreatitis risk and gastroparesis considerations are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 27295427**: *Liraglutide and Cardiovascular Outcomes in Type 2 Diabetes* (2016) [other]
- **PMID 27633186**: *Semaglutide and Cardiovascular Outcomes in Patients with Type 2 Diabetes* (2016) [other]
- **PMID 40962119**: *Does semaglutide increase the risk of non-arteritic anterior ischemic optic neuropathy? A systematic review and meta-analysis of emerging evidence* (2026) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41039116**: *A systematic review and meta-analysis of the efficacy and safety of pharmacological treatments for obesity in adults* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41071360**: *GLP-1 receptor agonists as an adjunct to bariatric surgery for weight loss and metabolic outcome improvement: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41272444**: *Cardiovascular benefits of semaglutide: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41824845**: *GLP-1 receptor agonists for weight loss: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2026) [meta-analysis]

## [0738] Glucocorticoid-induced osteoporosis: ACR guidelines, fracture risk, prophylaxis timing

**Cluster**: giop | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Glucocorticoid-induced osteoporosis is under-treated; ACR 2022, ECTS 2024 and national guidelines give fracture-risk-based approaches to prevention and drug treatment, and urgent efforts are needed to close the treatment gap.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2022 ACR guideline provides direction for prevention and treatment of GIOP and is not intended to limit access to therapies. (PMID:37845798, PMID:37884467)
- The 2024 ECTS recommendations give clear pragmatic advice on prevention and treatment of GIOP in adults for health practitioners who are not bone specialists. (PMID:39556468)
- Belgian Bone Club consensus says efforts to close the treatment gap in GIOP and implement effective fracture prevention strategies are urgently needed. (PMID:35757436)
- Latin American GIOP guidelines issued fifty statements, of which all but four reached agreement. (PMID:36348222)
- The UK osteoporosis guideline, endorsed by IOF and ESCEO, covers prevention and treatment of osteoporosis including at-risk groups. (PMID:40921943)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Thresholds for starting drugs differ across guidelines.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Start bone protection with prednisolone 7.5 mg or more for 3 months or more (not in abstracts).
- Bisphosphonate as first-line drug.

### Case Generation Hooks
- 68-year-old with polymyalgia rheumatica starting 15 mg prednisolone.

**Reviewer Gap**: Specific dose and duration thresholds, fracture risk calculators (FRAX adjustment) and drug choice are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 35757436**: *Prevention and Treatment of Glucocorticoid-Induced Osteoporosis in Adults: Consensus Recommendations From the Belgian Bone Club* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 36348222**: *Evidence based Latin American Guidelines of clinical practice on prevention, diagnosis, management and treatment of glucocorticoid induced osteoporosis. A 2022 update : This manuscript has been produced under the auspices of the Committee of National Societies (CNS) and the Committee of Scientific Advisors (CSA) of the International Osteoporosis Foundation (IOF)* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 37845798**: *2022 American College of Rheumatology Guideline for the Prevention and Treatment of Glucocorticoid-Induced Osteoporosis* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 37884467**: *2022 American College of Rheumatology Guideline for the Prevention and Treatment of Glucocorticoid-Induced Osteoporosis* (2023) [guideline]
- **PMID 39556468**: *Prevention and treatment of glucocorticoid-induced osteoporosis in adults: recommendations from the European Calcified Tissue Society* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 40921943**: *The 2024 UK clinical guideline for the prevention and treatment of osteoporosis* (2025) [guideline]

## [0740] Glucocorticoid tapering: adrenal suppression, disease flare prevention, steroid-sparing agents

**Cluster**: steroid_taper | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Long-term glucocorticoid use needs planned tapering and attention to adrenal insufficiency; withdrawal in practice is slower than guidelines advocate. Higher steroid exposure in lupus nephritis improves renal response at a price of infections and death.

### Core Teaching Points
- The joint ESE/Endocrine Society guideline gives guidance on diagnosis and therapy of glucocorticoid-induced adrenal insufficiency in patients on chronic glucocorticoid treatment. (PMID:38724043, PMID:38714321)
- In rheumatoid arthritis patients starting biologic DMARDs, glucocorticoid withdrawal was much slower than advocated by guidelines, so more effort should go into tapering once low disease activity is reached. (PMID:38042363)
- Higher glucocorticoid exposure during initial lupus nephritis therapy was associated with better renal outcomes but with more infections and death. (PMID:38766897)
- German-speaking societies' PMR recommendations advise offering older and frail patients an individualised exercise programme. (PMID:41346249)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal taper speed and testing for HPA axis recovery.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Adrenal crisis risk after abrupt steroid withdrawal.
- Sick-day rules for patients on long-term steroids.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient stopping 5 mg prednisolone after 18 months with fatigue and nausea.

**Reviewer Gap**: Specific taper schedules, morning cortisol and Synacthen thresholds are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38042363**: *Oral glucocorticoid use in patients with rheumatoid arthritis initiating TNF-inhibitors, tocilizumab or abatacept: Results from the international TOCERRA and PANABA observational collaborative studies* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38714321**: *European Society of Endocrinology and Endocrine Society Joint Clinical Guideline: Diagnosis and therapy of glucocorticoid-induced adrenal insufficiency* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38724043**: *European Society of Endocrinology and Endocrine Society Joint Clinical Guideline: Diagnosis and Therapy of Glucocorticoid-induced Adrenal Insufficiency* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38766897**: *Impact of Glucocorticoid Dose on Complete Response, Serious Infections, and Mortality During the Initial Therapy of Lupus Nephritis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of the Control Arms of Randomized Controlled Trials* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 41346249**: *Recommendations of the German, Austrian and Swiss rheumatology and other medical societies for the management of polymyalgia rheumatica* (2026) [guideline]

## [0741] Glycaemic control in the ICU: insulin infusion protocols and targets

**Cluster**: tight_glycaemic_control | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Tight glucose control in the ICU does not improve outcomes and increases severe hypoglycaemia. The 2024 SCCM guideline recommends against intensive targets, and moderate targets with safe insulin protocols are preferred.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2024 SCCM guideline recommends avoiding intensive glucose targets and minimising hypoglycaemia in critically ill children and adults. (PMID:38240484)
- A patient-level meta-analysis of intensive glucose control in critically ill adults found no subgroup in which intensive control was beneficial, with excess severe hypoglycaemia. (PMID:38864749)
- In septic ICU patients, intensive and liberal glucose control had comparable effects on outcomes, but intensive control carried a higher risk of severe hypoglycaemia. (PMID:38796958)
- An updated 2026 comparison suggests a range of 110-140 mg/dL (6.1-7.8 mmol/L) may be acceptable in select centres where hypoglycaemia risk is negligible and optimised protocols are used. (PMID:42262517)
- Perioperative intensive glucose control in diabetic surgical patients reduced some complications but increased hypoglycaemia. (PMID:34509960)
- In non-ICU hospitalised patients with diabetes, adding continuous glucose monitoring to point-of-care testing improved glycaemic control and reduced hypoglycaemia. (PMID:39798897)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Optimal target within the moderate range.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- NICE-SUGAR lesson: intensive control increases hypoglycaemia and mortality risk.
- Typical ICU target around 140-180 mg/dL (not in abstracts).

### Case Generation Hooks
- Septic ICU patient with glucose 11 mmol/L: target and insulin infusion protocol.

**Reviewer Gap**: The explicit upper threshold to start insulin and the 140-180 mg/dL target are not in the retrieved abstracts; NICE-SUGAR and Leuven trials were not retrieved.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 34509960**: *Intensive glucose control during the perioperative period for diabetic patients undergoing surgery: An updated systematic review and meta-analysis* (2021) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38240484**: *Society of Critical Care Medicine Guidelines on Glycemic Control for Critically Ill Children and Adults 2024* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38796958**: *Intensive or liberal glucose control in intensive care units for septic patients? A meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 38864749**: *A Patient-Level Meta-Analysis of Intensive Glucose Control in Critically Ill Adults* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39798897**: *Use of continuous glucose monitoring and point-of-care glucose testing in hospitalized patients with diabetes mellitus in non-intensive care unit settings: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 42262517**: *Comparison of Intensive Versus Conventional Glycemic Control Targets: An Updated Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of the 2024 Society of Critical Care Medicine Guidelines on Glycemic Control for Critically Ill Adults* (2026) [guideline]

## [0743] Goals of Care and Hospice Referral

**Cluster**: hospice | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Goals-of-care discussions and timely hospice referral rely on structured patient-clinician communication and integration of palliative care into disease pathways. Retrieved evidence is general and disease-specific rather than a single referral guideline.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ASCO patient-clinician communication update states that full implementation of communication best practices requires health systems to provide adequate time, training and support for clinicians. (PMID:41805420)
- A Korean heart failure guideline aims to bridge heart failure and palliative care through collaborative care and goal-concordant, patient-centred decision-making. (PMID:42110709)
- Palliative care guidelines now exist for specific diseases such as stroke, and a guideline addresses medication management of distressing symptoms in older patients receiving end-of-life palliative care. (PMID:42190798, PMID:42433014)
- A Chinese expert consensus defines palliative care and hospice care and their relationship, emphasising the holistic nature of palliative care. (PMID:40394878)
- The MASCC-ASCO guideline on opioid conversion in adults with cancer aims to establish a best-practice baseline. (PMID:40029420)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Triggers and timing for hospice referral.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Early palliative care alongside disease-directed treatment.
- Components of a goals-of-care conversation.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Patient with end-stage heart failure and recurrent admissions: discussing goals and hospice eligibility.

**Reviewer Gap**: Prognostic triggers and hospice eligibility criteria are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 40029420**: *Opioid conversion in adults with cancer: MASCC-ASCO-AAHPM-HPNA-NICSO guideline* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40394878**: *Chinese Expert Consensus on the Definitions of Palliative Care and Hospice Care (2025)* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41805420**: *Patient-Clinician Communication: ASCO Guideline Update* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42110709**: *Korean Society of Heart Failure Guidelines for the Palliative Care and Hospice for Heart Failure Patients* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42190798**: *Palliative care in stroke: Guidelines of the Spanish Society of Neurology's Stroke Study Group* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42433014**: *Guideline for Medication Management of Distressing Symptoms in Older Patients Receiving End-of-Life Palliative Care* (2026) [guideline]

## [0744] Gonorrhea and chlamydia treatment

**Cluster**: gonorrhoea_chlamydia | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
The 2025 European chlamydia guideline prefers doxycycline over single-dose azithromycin, and doxycycline post-exposure prophylaxis reduces chlamydia and syphilis with possible effect on gonorrhoea depending on local resistance.

### Core Teaching Points
- The 2025 European guideline recommends doxycycline as first line for Chlamydia trachomatis infection in preference to single-dose azithromycin, and finds limited value of broad screening in asymptomatic populations. (PMID:40037375)
- A meta-analysis found doxycycline prophylaxis reduces new cases of chlamydia and syphilis and is potentially effective against gonorrhoea, influenced by local resistance patterns. (PMID:39122208)
- CDC guidance on doxycycline post-exposure prophylaxis advises STI screening at baseline and every 3-6 months, reassessing the need every 3-6 months, and HIV screening for HIV-negative MSM and transgender women. (PMID:38833414)
- Information on bacterial resistance and long-term adverse events after doxy-PEP is still lacking. (PMID:39097410)
- The 2025 BASHH national guideline on gonorrhoea updates the 2018 guideline on diagnosis, treatment and health promotion. (PMID:40448704)
- A German urethritis guideline includes pathogen-specific treatments with alternatives where doxycycline is contraindicated. (PMID:39822084)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Doxy-PEP resistance consequences.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Doxycycline for chlamydia.
- Doxy-PEP and gonorrhoeal resistance.

### Case Generation Hooks
- MSM with recurrent STIs asking about doxycycline prophylaxis.

**Reviewer Gap**: Gonorrhoea treatment (ceftriaxone dose), dual therapy history and test-of-cure are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 38833414**: *CDC Clinical Guidelines on the Use of Doxycycline Postexposure Prophylaxis for Bacterial Sexually Transmitted Infection Prevention, United States, 2024* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 39097410**: *Efficacy of postexposure prophylaxis with doxycycline (Doxy-PEP) in reducing sexually transmitted infections: a systematic review and meta-analysis* (2025) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39122208**: *Doxycycline prophylaxis for the prevention of sexually transmitted infections: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials* (2024) [meta-analysis]
- **PMID 39822084**: *German evidence- and consensus-based guideline on the management of penile urethritis* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40037375**: *2025 European guideline on the management of &lt;i&gt;Chlamydia trachomatis&lt;/i&gt; infections* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40448704**: *British Association of Sexual Health and HIV UK National Guideline for the Management of infection with &lt;i&gt;Neisseria gonorrhoeae&lt;/i&gt;, 2025* (2025) [guideline]

## [0746] gout

**Cluster**: gout | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Gout management combines acute flare treatment with colchicine, NSAIDs or steroids and long-term urate-lowering therapy, with prophylaxis against flares during the first months of therapy. Guideline gaps remain over treat-to-target versus symptom-based strategies.

### Core Teaching Points
- A review of guideline-based treatment emphasises prophylaxis against flares during the first months of urate-lowering treatment, and that adherence to long-term urate-lowering medicines is crucial. (PMID:39998560)
- There is uncertainty about treat-to-target urate strategy, with a proposed 'treat-to-avoid-symptoms' alternative needing definition. (PMID:38219395)
- The 2021 APLAR gout guideline formulated recommendations including non-pharmacologic interventions for individualised care. (PMID:34931463)
- A 2025 expert consensus on hyperuricaemia with high cardiovascular risk notes that randomised trials are needed to show whether urate lowering improves hard cardiovascular and renal outcomes. (PMID:41786537)
- A survey showed rheumatologists' practice largely concordant with the 2020 ACR gout guideline but with gaps in dosing knowledge. (PMID:38621796)
- Updated Chinese guidelines address general patients and those with comorbidities, and a 2025 consensus covers hyperuricaemia and gout in adolescents. (PMID:40692263, PMID:40851321, PMID:40693733)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Best urate target strategy.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Start urate-lowering therapy with flare prophylaxis.
- Treat-to-target serum urate (commonly below 360 micromol/L, not in abstracts).

### Case Generation Hooks
- Man with second gout flare in a year and serum urate 520.

**Reviewer Gap**: Acute flare drug doses, allopurinol titration, HLA-B*58:01 and urate targets are not stated in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 34931463**: *2021 Asia-Pacific League of Associations for Rheumatology clinical practice guideline for treatment of gout* (2022) [guideline]
- **PMID 38219395**: *Moving urate-lowering therapy in gout beyond guideline recommendations* (2024) [guideline]
- **PMID 38621796**: *Knowledge of and Stated Adherence to the 2020 American College of Rheumatology Guideline for Gout Management: Results of a Survey of US Rheumatologists* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 39998560**: *[Gout : From the diagnosis to guideline-based treatment]* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40692263**: *2024 Update of Chinese Guidelines for Diagnosis and Treatment of Hyperuricemia and Gout Part I: Recommendations for General Patients* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40693733**: *Clinical Practice Consensus Statement 2025: Management of Hyperuricemia and Gout in Adolescents* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40851321**: *2024 Update of Chinese Guidelines for Management of Hyperuricemia and Gout Part II: Recommendations for Patients With Common Comorbidities* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 41786537**: *Expert consensus for the diagnosis and treatment of patients with hyperuricemia and high cardiovascular risk: 2025 update* (2026) [guideline]

## [0750] Graft-Versus-Host Disease

**Cluster**: gvhd | **Status**: published

### Clinical Mentor Synopsis
Graft-versus-host disease is treated with steroids first line and ruxolitinib second line in acute disease; third-line choice and chronic GVHD organ-specific care are active research areas.

### Core Teaching Points
- The ASTCT evidence-based review of acute GVHD management covers third-line therapy, and ongoing studies seek risk-adapted strategies. (PMID:42155643)
- French recommendations discuss the third-line treatment after steroids and ruxolitinib, noting all options are the subject of intense research, and stressing supportive measures. (PMID:40914633)
- The NIH 2025 chronic GVHD consensus update reports modest progress on most initiatives. (PMID:40409691)
- Consensus statements on pulmonary chronic GVHD recommend standardised pulmonary function surveillance, early intervention and individualised immunosuppression. (PMID:40926299, PMID:42851173)
- A Canadian review covers topical therapies and lifestyle modifications for skin, mouth, eye and genital chronic GVHD as adjuncts to systemic treatment. (PMID:42782894)

### Key Controversies & Uncertainties
- Third-line therapy choice.

### High-Yield MCQ Angles
- Steroid-refractory acute GVHD: ruxolitinib.
- Organs involved in acute GVHD: skin, gut, liver.

### Case Generation Hooks
- Day +40 after allogeneic transplant with rash, diarrhoea and rising bilirubin.

**Reviewer Gap**: Grading of acute GVHD, prophylaxis regimens and first-line steroid dosing are not in the retrieved abstracts.

### Evidence Sources
- **PMID 40409691**: *NIH Chronic Graft-Versus-Host Disease Consensus Conference 2025 Update* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 40914633**: *[Acute graft-versus-host disease therapy: Which third line treatment after steroids and ruxolitinib? (SFGM-TC)]* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 40926299**: *Taiwan consensus on pulmonary chronic graft-versus-host disease: A joint statement from the Taiwan Society of Blood and Marrow Transplantation (TBMT) and Taiwan Society of Pulmonary and Critical Care Medicine (TSPCCM)* (2025) [guideline]
- **PMID 42155643**: *Clinical Management of Acute Graft-Versus-Host Disease: An Evidence-Based Review from the ASTCT Committee on Practice Guidelines* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42782894**: *Cell Therapy Transplant Canada (CTTC) Consensus-Based Review for the Use of Supportive Care and Topical Therapies for Chronic Graft-Versus-Host Disease* (2026) [guideline]
- **PMID 42851173**: *[Chinese expert consensus on the diagnosis and treatment of pulmonary chronic graft-versus-host disease (2026)]* (2026) [guideline]
